We explain how to define charge flows using the holographic stress tensor and what form they take in terms of multipole moments at second order in perturbations. We conclude with a some remarks on the results.

</details>


### [28] [Extended spherically symmetric solutions in revised Deser--Woodard nonlocal gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22086)
*Haida Li,Xiangdong Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends static, spherically symmetric black hole solutions in revised Deser–Woodard nonlocal gravity by linearly perturbing the Schwarzschild metric; it identifies two physically motivated asymptotically flat corrections—logarithmically dressed (long-range, slow decay) and exponentially suppressed (short-range, horizon-shifted)—linked respectively to quantum corrections and finite-range/screened gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore physically realistic nonlocal modifications of black hole spacetimes in revised Deser–Woodard gravity, particularly those consistent with asymptotic flatness and motivated by quantum gravity or screening mechanisms.

Method: Linear perturbation analysis of the field equations around Schwarzschild; exploitation of linearity to define mode-by-mode superposition; construction and analysis of two specific asymptotically flat correction ansätze: logarithmic dressing and exponential suppression.

Result: Two new families of asymptotically flat black hole solutions are obtained: (i) a logarithmically dressed correction yielding a slowly decaying deviation at large radii, and (ii) an exponentially suppressed correction localized near the horizon that shifts the horizon inward for positive amplitude.

Conclusion: The linearity of revised D-W gravity enables systematic construction of physically motivated nonlocal black hole extensions; the logarithmic and exponential corrections represent distinct physical regimes—long-range quantum-like effects versus short-range screened dynamics—both compatible with asymptotic flatness.

Abstract: In this work, we extend the static spherically symmetric black hole solutions of revised Deser--Woodard (D-W) nonlocal gravity. Due to the linearity of the field equations, we show that the first-order expansion around the Schwarzschild solution is linear in both the temporal metric component and the reciprocal of the radial metric component. Therefore, a mode-by-mode superposition can be properly defined for different types of correction terms. We then further study two additional asymptotically flat extensions, a logarithmically dressed correction and an exponentially suppressed correction. The logarithmically dressed correction gives a slowly decaying deviation from Schwarzschild as the radius becomes large, making it a more extended nonlocal effect, whereas the exponentially suppressed correction is localized near the horizon and shifts the horizon inward for positive correction amplitude. These correction terms are physically motivated by common mechanisms in modified gravity: Logarithmic terms are possibly related to effects of quantum corrections, and exponential terms can arise from finite-range or screened gravitational effects.

</details>


### [29] [Horizon Singularities in the Schwarzschild Geometry of the Teleparallel Equivalent of General Relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22121)
*D. F. López,A. A. Coley,R. J. van den Hoogen*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper resolves horizon singularities in Schwarzschild TEGR by classifying tetrad–spin connection (Lorentz sector) configurations into regular and singular subclasses; only the regular subclass yields finite torsion scalars, admits analytic extensions across the horizon, and supports a consistent black hole interpretation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the apparent horizon singularities in Schwarzschild TEGR—where certain torsion scalar invariants diverge—by determining whether these are genuine geometric singularities or artifacts of improper Lorentz-frame choices.

Method: Classification of Schwarzschild TEGR geometries via their Lorentz sector (tetrad and spin connection); application of three independent criteria—(i) inertial frame condition, (ii) horizon-penetrating coordinates (e.g., Eddington–Finkelstein), and (iii) horizon regularity criterion (finiteness of all torsion scalars)—to identify admissible Lorentz-sector functions.

Result: Two disjoint subclasses emerge: a regular subclass where all torsion scalar invariants remain finite at the horizon, analytic extensions exist, and the horizon is part of the manifold; and a singular subclass where divergences persist and the horizon is excised. Crucially, horizon regularity is shown to be independent of inertial effects—the proper frame alone does not remove singularities.

Conclusion: The black hole interpretation of Schwarzschild spacetime in TEGR is viable only for tetrads/spin connections belonging to the regular Lorentz subclass; this underscores the physical relevance of the full Lorentz structure—not just the metric—in teleparallel gravity.

Abstract: Certain torsion scalar invariants are known to diverge at the horizon of the Schwarzschild solution in the Teleparallel Equivalent of General Relativity (TEGR), obstructing its interpretation as a black hole spacetime. We show that Schwarzschild TEGR geometries split into two distinct subclasses determined by the Lorentz sector of the geometry -- the elements of the tetrad and spin connection not encoded in the metric but appearing in the torsion. In the regular subclass, the divergences are absent and the horizon belongs to the manifold, supporting a consistent black hole interpretation. In the singular subclass, the divergences are genuine and the horizon is excluded from the manifold. As part of this analysis, we clarify the role of inertial contributions in teleparallel gravity, showing that the proper frame does not, by itself, eliminate inertial effects and that the horizon singularities are independent of the inertial structure of the frame. Using three independent approaches -- the inertial frame condition, horizon-penetrating coordinates, and the horizon regularity criterion -- we determine the complete class of Lorentz sector functions compatible with a regular horizon in Schwarzschild TEGR geometries. For this class, all torsion scalar invariants remain finite at the horizon and analytic extensions across it are admitted.

</details>


### [30] [Covariant virtual work and the d'Alembert-Lagrange formulation of general relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22196)
*A. Cuevas,C. Ortiz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a covariant virtual work framework for spacetime geometry, reformulating General Relativity via a d'Alembert-Lagrange principle—where Einstein’s equations follow from vanishing total covariant virtual work on metric variations, not action extremization; it classifies constraint reactions (ideal vs. non-ideal), extends to one-sided constraints yielding inequalities, and derives the cosmological constant as an ideal reaction from an isoperimetric constraint, fixing it globally via spacetime averages.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a geometrically covariant, constraint-aware mechanical formulation of gravity—bypassing reliance on action principles and offering a physical interpretation of the cosmological constant as arising from admissibility constraints rather than vacuum energy.

Method: Develops a covariant virtual work structure for Lorentzian metrics, defines admissible variations under geometric constraints (e.g., isoperimetric), constructs a d'Alembert-Lagrange principle using covariant virtual displacements, and analyzes reaction forces induced by constraints via decomposition into ideal (zero virtual work) and non-ideal (nonzero virtual work) sectors.

Result: Einstein field equations emerge as the condition of zero total covariant virtual work; ideal reactions from isoperimetric constraints yield a cosmological term with magnitude fixed by global spacetime averages—rendering the cosmological constant a nonlocal, geometrically determined parameter; the framework naturally accommodates inequality-based (one-sided) constraints.

Conclusion: General Relativity admits a fully covariant mechanical interpretation rooted in virtual work and constraint dynamics, wherein the cosmological constant is not a free parameter but a consequence of admissibility conditions—offering a novel, non-perturbative, and geometrically intrinsic origin for dark energy-like behavior.

Abstract: We develop a covariant virtual work structure and a corresponding d'Alembert-Lagrange principle for spacetime geometry. Within this framework, General Relativity arises as a particular realization of the principle, leading to a covariant d'Alembert-Lagrange formulation in which the Einstein field equations arise from the vanishing of total covariant virtual work on admissible metric variations, rather than from action extremality. The covariant virtual work structure provides a covariant classification of constraint-induced contributions, distinguishing ideal reactions, which perform no virtual work, from non-ideal sectors contribute explicitly to it. The structure extends naturally to one-sided admissibility conditions, yielding a covariant inequality structure. Constraints generate reaction terms. In particular, an isoperimetric constraint produces a cosmological term as an ideal reaction fixed by spacetime averages, so that the cosmological constant emerges as a global parameter determined by admissibility, reflecting an intrinsically nonlocal geometric origin.

</details>


### [31] [Non-Metricity Corrections Approach to Alleviate $H _0$ Tension: The Logarithmic and Nonlinear $f(Q)$ Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22262)
*Rahul Bhagat,Kesava Chodavarapu,B. Mishra*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether symmetric teleparallel $f(Q)$ gravity models—specifically logarithmic and nonlinear saturation forms—can alleviate the $H_0$ tension without Λ, finding the logarithmic model yields intermediate $H_0$ values and better statistical performance than ΛCDM across multiple datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the persistent $H_0$ tension between early- and late-Universe measurements by testing whether geometric modifications in symmetric teleparallel gravity ($f(Q)$) can reconcile discrepancies without invoking dark energy (Λ).

Method: Bayesian MCMC analysis of two $f(Q)$ models (logarithmic and nonlinear saturation) constrained by cosmic chronometers, Type Ia supernovae (Pantheon, Pantheon+SH0ES, DES SN5YR), and BAO (SDSS, DESI); model comparison via AIC/BIC.

Result: Both $f(Q)$ models are statistically competitive with ΛCDM; the logarithmic model consistently yields $H_0$ values intermediate between Planck and SH0ES estimates and achieves lower AIC/BIC scores.

Conclusion: Logarithmic $f(Q)$ gravity is a viable, statistically preferred modified-gravity alternative that partially alleviates the $H_0$ tension, suggesting geometric extensions beyond GR warrant further scrutiny as alternatives to ΛCDM.

Abstract: The persistent discrepancy between early-time and late-Universe measurements of the Hubble constant commonly known as the $H_0$ tension remains one of the most pressing open questions in modern cosmology. In this work, we explore whether modifications to the gravitational sector, specifically within the framework of symmetric teleparallel gravity, can offer a viable pathway toward alleviating this tension. We consider two functional forms of $f(Q)$ gravity: a logarithmic model and a nonlinear saturation model, both of which introduce geometric corrections to the standard expansion history without invoking a cosmological constant. Constraining these models through a Bayesian MCMC analysis against a comprehensive suite of observational data, including cosmic chronometers, Type Ia supernova compilations (Pantheon, Pantheon$+$SH0ES, and DES SN5YR), and BAO measurements from SDSS and DESI, we find that both models remain statistically competitive with $Λ$CDM. The logarithmic model, in particular, consistently infers intermediate values of $H_0$ between the \textit{Planck} and SH0ES benchmarks across all dataset combinations, and carries lower AIC and BIC penalties, establishing it as the more promising candidate for partially easing the $H_0$ tension within a modified gravity framework.

</details>


### [32] [Null geodesic defocusing in dynamical black-hole-to-white-hole transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22404)
*Johanna Borissova,Stefano Liberati,Matt Visser*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proves that dynamical black-hole-to-white-hole transitions require violation of the null convergence condition—a key energy condition—regardless of the underlying gravity theory; it demonstrates this via kinematic analysis of null geodesics and constructs explicit bouncing geometries, showing the violation is localized in the transition phase and diverges for instantaneous transitions, implying breakdown of semiclassical spacetime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether singularity-resolving black-hole-to-white-hole transitions in quantum gravity can be compatible with classical energy conditions, particularly the null convergence condition (NCC), without assuming specific field equations.

Method: Kinematic analysis of null geodesic expansions across trapping/anti-trapping horizons in generic dynamical spacetimes; construction of explicit time-dependent bouncing geometries in generalized Painlevé–Gullstrand coordinates using regular static black-hole metrics (e.g., Bardeen-type) promoted to time-dependent mass functions.

Result: Violation of the null convergence condition is necessary and localized during the dynamical transition phase; instantaneous transitions would require unbounded NCC violation, indicating failure of the effective metric description.

Conclusion: Singularity resolution via black-hole-to-white-hole transitions generically violates the null convergence condition—a robust, model-independent consequence of horizon dynamics—implying such transitions cannot be described within classical or standard semiclassical gravity alone and necessitate quantum gravitational input.

Abstract: We investigate the defocusing of null geodesics in dynamical, non-singular black-hole-to-white-hole transitions. Working at the level of spacetime kinematics, and without assuming any specific gravitational field equations, we show that the contraction and disappearance of a trapped region, as well as the subsequent formation and expansion of an anti-trapped region, necessarily require a violation of the null convergence condition. This conclusion follows directly from the behaviour of the null expansions across the trapping and anti-trapping horizons, and is therefore independent of the microscopic mechanism responsible for singularity resolution. We then illustrate this general argument by constructing a class of explicit bouncing geometries in generalised Painlevé-Gullstrand coordinates, obtained by promoting static regular black holes with de Sitter cores to time-dependent black-hole-to-white-hole transition models. For a Bardeen-type mass function, we show that the required violation of the null convergence condition is localised within the intermediate dynamical phase in which the trapped region evaporates and the anti-trapped region forms. Finally, we argue that the limiting case of an instantaneous black-hole-to-white-hole transition would require an unbounded violation of the null convergence condition, signalling a breakdown of the effective continuum metric description, and the need to appeal to a full quantum-gravitational description.

</details>


### [33] [Starobinsky-inflation in asymptotically safe shift-symmetric scalar-tensor theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22408)
*Benjamin Koch,Cristobal Laporte,Frank Saueressig*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores how scalaron-driven inflation constrains asymptotically safe scalar-tensor theories, showing that the non-minimal gravity-matter coupling parameter influences both matter and gravitational higher-derivative terms; using multi-field inflation formalism and observational data, it rules out two of three non-Gaussian fixed points as UV completions, identifying the third as phenomenologically viable.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether asymptotically safe scalar-tensor theories—particularly their UV fixed point structure—are compatible with inflationary cosmology and current observational constraints.

Method: Renormalization group improvement applied to a Horndeski-type scalar-tensor theory yields higher-derivative couplings fixed by microscopic Gaussian fixed point parameters; multi-field inflation formalism is employed to analyze dynamics and derive observational bounds on the non-minimal coupling.

Result: The non-minimal gravity-matter coupling parameter propagates into gravitational-sector higher-derivative terms; cosmological observations constrain this coupling, allowing exclusion of two non-Gaussian fixed points as viable UV completions.

Conclusion: Only one of the three non-Gaussian fixed points supports RG trajectories consistent with scalaron-driven inflation and observational data, thereby narrowing viable asymptotically safe UV completions of scalar-tensor gravity.

Abstract: We investigate the constraining power of scalaron-driven inflation on asymptotically safe scalar-tensor theories. Starting from a Horndeski-type theory and applying a renormalization group improvement procedure generates higher-derivative couplings which are fixed in terms of the microscopic parameters - a structure that is expected to occur also within first principle computations based on the asymptotic safety mechanism. The latter are taken to be the free parameters appearing at the Gaussian fixed point. We find that the free parameter initially associated with the non-minimal gravity-matter coupling is not confined to the gravity-matter sector of the theory and also enters the effective higher-derivative couplings in the gravitational sector. We review the setting of multi-field inflationary models which is appropriate to analyze the inflationary dynamics in this context and illustrate their applicability by working out the explicit bounds on the non-minimal gravity-matter coupling resulting from cosmological observations. Given the fixed point structure of asymptotically safe scalar-tensor theories, the results indicate that UV-completions by two of the three available non-Gaussian fixed points can be ruled out while pinpointing phenomenologically viable RG trajectories emanating from the third fixed point.

</details>


### [34] [An Interplay Between Fractional Calculus and Holographic Dark Energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22431)
*Ayush Bidlan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This dissertation introduces Fractional Holographic Dark Energy (FHDE) by incorporating a Riesz fractional spatial derivative into black hole thermodynamics via the Wheeler–DeWitt equation, yielding α-dependent corrections to Bekenstein–Hawking entropy; it constructs a new dark energy density, analyzes cosmological evolution and EoS across modified gravity frameworks (BD, DGP, Horndeski), and classifies late-time singularities—all to address Hubble-cutoff limitations of standard HDE and link quantum non-locality to cosmic acceleration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve theoretical challenges of standard holographic dark energy—particularly the Hubble cutoff issue—by embedding non-local, memory-based physics from fractional calculus into black hole thermodynamics and quantum geometrodynamics, thereby connecting quantum gravity features to late-time cosmic acceleration.

Method: Introduces a Riesz fractional spatial derivative into the Hamiltonian constraint of a Schwarzschild black hole in quantum geometrodynamics, deriving a Fractional Wheeler–DeWitt equation; solves it to obtain α-dependent fractional Bekenstein–Hawking entropy; applies Cohen's inequality to construct FHDE density; performs cosmological reconstruction using spin-0/spin-1 effective field descriptions; extends FHDE to BD, DGP, EPN, and Horndeski theories; analyzes EoS, deceleration parameter, and late-time singularities (big/little/pseudo-rip) within Granda–Oliveros cutoff.

Result: Derives fractionally corrected black hole entropy scaling with Lévy index α (1 < α ≤ 2); constructs FHDE density with explicit α-dependence; obtains redshift- and α-dependent kinetic/potential terms in effective field descriptions; computes analytical expressions for equation-of-state w(z,α) and deceleration q(z,α) across multiple modified gravity theories; classifies singularity types (Type I–III) under FHDE, showing α tunes transition between phantom and quintessence regimes.

Conclusion: FHDE provides a theoretically grounded, non-local extension of HDE rooted in fractional quantum gravity, successfully alleviates Hubble-cutoff inconsistencies, reproduces viable late-time acceleration, and enables systematic discrimination among dark energy models and future singularities via the Lévy index α—offering a bridge between quantum spacetime structure and observational cosmology beyond ΛCDM.

Abstract: This dissertation aims to put forth a systematic construction of a fractional-calculus extension of holographic dark energy (HDE). We show that linking late-time cosmic acceleration to non-local or memory effects encoded in a fractional (Riesz) derivative within black hole thermodynamics produces deviations from standard HDE and can address some challenges of the Hubble cutoff. In particular, a Riesz fractional spatial derivative is introduced into the Hamiltonian constraint of a Schwarzschild black hole in quantum geometrodynamics, leading to a Fractional Wheeler--DeWitt equation whose solutions yield fractionally corrected thermodynamic quantities, notably fractional Bekenstein--Hawking entropy governed by the Lévy index \(α\), with \(1<α\leq2\). Using this entropy with Cohen's inequality, a new dark energy density is constructed, defining the Fractional Holographic Dark Energy (FHDE) framework.
  The cosmological implications of FHDE are then investigated. Within the Hubble cutoff, its late-time evolution is analysed through cosmological observables, and the model is reconstructed using effective field descriptions with spin-\(0\) and spin-\(1\) candidates, allowing kinetic and potential terms to be extracted as functions of redshift \(z\) and \(α\). The framework is then extended to BD, DGP, EPN, and Horndeski theories to derive the equation-of-state and deceleration parameters in terms of \(z\) and \(α\). In addition, the fate of the Universe is studied through late-time singularities, namely the big, little, and pseudo-rip, within the Granda--Oliveros FHDE setting. In short, this dissertation proposes FHDE as a theoretically motivated extension of HDE, bridging non-locality in quantum gravity with the late-time dynamics of the Universe, and offering a route toward understanding cosmic acceleration beyond \(Λ\)CDM.

</details>


### [35] [Steering a warp drive without exotic matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22531)
*An T. Le*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper resolves the long-standing problem of accelerating warp drives within general relativity by proving a conservation law forbidding steering without radiation, then constructing the first explicit positive-energy, accelerating warp drive via a timelike shell matched to a Kinnersley photon rocket exterior — achieving causal, subluminal acceleration powered solely by Bondi mass loss, bounded by energy conditions and Buchdahl-type acceleration limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the fundamental incompatibility between warp drive acceleration and energy conditions: prior positive-energy warp drives were static or constant-velocity; accelerating ones required exotic matter, and Bobrick & Martire had noted warp drives 'do not have any natural way of changing their velocities.'

Method: 1) Derive a Bondi–Sachs four-momentum conservation law for asymptotically flat spacetimes with confined dominant-energy sources and standard peeling, showing acceleration requires radiation to null infinity. 2) Construct an exact positive-energy accelerating spacetime: prescribe a passenger worldtube, match an exact Kinnersley photon rocket exterior, and solve for the interior timelike shell via junction conditions; certify shell properties perturbatively and numerically. 3) Analyze energy conditions (surface dominant energy condition), acceleration bounds (Buchdahl-type frontier), stability (marginal vs. strict), and optimal maneuver (Damour dipole) on gravitational-wave–silent class.

Result: 1) A rigorous no-steering-without-radiation theorem under dominant energy and asymptotic flatness. 2) First explicit positive-energy accelerating warp drive: exact exterior + perturbatively/numerically verified shell; acceleration powered by photon-rocket recoil in a tidally protected flat cavity; satisfies $n^2 \ge 0$ (exterior) and surface dominant energy condition for $2m/R < 24/25$. 3) Acceleration bound $a_{\max} R \le g(2m/R)$ with lower bound ~0.2 and upper bound $\frac{1}{2}(1 - 2m/R)$. 4) Causal, subluminal, and energy-budget–limited—not exotic-matter–limited.

Conclusion: Steering a warp drive is physically possible within standard general relativity and positive-energy conditions, but fundamentally constrained by radiative momentum loss and geometric energy bounds; the obstacle is energetic cost and engineering feasibility—not violation of energy conditions or causality.

Abstract: A useful warp drive must change its velocity, yet known positive-energy constructions are static or constant-velocity, while accelerating ones require exotic matter; Bobrick and Martire noted that warp drives ``do not have any natural way of changing their velocities.'' First, a conservation law settles the principle: for any asymptotically flat drive with a confined dominant-energy source and standard peeling, the Bondi--Sachs four-momentum changes only through radiation to null infinity, so the drive cannot steer without radiating. Second, we construct a positive-energy spacetime that saturates this bound. Prescribing the passenger worldtube, we take the exact Kinnersley photon rocket as exterior and solve for the matching timelike shell; the exterior and steering law are exact, while the shell is certified perturbatively and numerically. The mechanism is photon-rocket recoil on a tidally protected, exactly flat cavity; the warp drive is the decoupled flat interior, not a warp field. The exterior energy conditions reduce to $n^2\ge0$, and steering obeys $-\dot m\ge 3m|a|$, paid for by Bondi mass loss. The shell satisfies the surface dominant energy condition for $2m/R<24/25$; admissible accelerating shells exist near the Schwarzschild--Minkowski anchor and, for slow burns, as time-evolved spacetimes. A Buchdahl-type frontier $a_{\max}R\le g(2m/R)$ caps the acceleration between a numerical lower bound $\sim\!0.2$ and a closed-form ceiling ${1\over2}(1-2m/R)$. On the gravitational-wave--silent class, the optimal maneuver is the Damour dipole. The wall is marginally stable, with strict stability available at no energy-condition cost; fully dynamical flux-coupled stability remains open. The drive is causal, subluminal, and energetically costly but positive-energy: steering is a problem of energy budget, not exotic matter.

</details>


### [36] [Extended parameterized spin expansion formalism for ringdown analysis with GW250114](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22580)
*Jia-Ning Chen,Liang-Bi Wu,Zong-Kuan Guo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends the ParSpec framework for black hole ringdown tests by treating the scaling index p̃ and length scale ℓ̃ as free, theory-agnostic parameters—rather than fixing p̃ to integer values—and applies it to GW250114 (and jointly with GW231123), finding that current data are insufficient to constrain p̃, yield only weak upper bounds on ℓ̃ (~83 km at 90% CL under γ<1), and provide minimal information gain beyond priors; the 220-only model outperforms 220+221 in constraining ℓ̃.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To move beyond theory-specific assumptions in black hole ringdown tests by adopting a theory-agnostic, effective-field-theory-inspired parameterization of deviations from Kerr geometry via free parameters ℓ̃ and p̃, enabling model-independent tests of general relativity.

Method: Extended the Parameterized Spin Expansion Coefficients (ParSpec) framework to jointly sample ℓ̃ and p̃; used pyRing for Bayesian inference on GW250114 with informative priors on final mass M_f and luminosity distance D_L; compared 220-only vs. 220+221 quasinormal mode models under varying γ conditions (e.g., γ<1); analyzed posterior geometry and information gain via Kullback-Leibler divergence; performed joint analysis with GW231123.

Result: Posterior on p̃ is prior-dominated—no data-driven constraint; ℓ̃ is weakly constrained (ℓ̃_90 ≈ 83 km) only under γ < 1, due to parameter-space geometry; Kullback-Leibler analysis shows negligible information gain on ℓ̃ and logγ; 220-only model yields tighter ℓ̃ constraints than 220+221; joint analysis with GW231123 is dominated by GW250114.

Conclusion: Current ringdown SNR is insufficient to meaningfully constrain the scaling behavior (p̃) of non-Kerr corrections; only weak, geometry-dependent bounds on the characteristic length scale (ℓ̃) are attainable under physically motivated γ<1; future higher-SNR detections or improved modeling are needed for robust, theory-agnostic tests.

Abstract: We extend the Parameterized Spin Expansion Coefficients (ParSpec) framework for ringdown tests of black holes by simultaneously sampling the characteristic length scale $\ell$ and the scaling index $p$, where the two parameters are replaced by $\tilde{p}$ and $\tilde{\ell}$ in subsequent analysis, respectively. Unlike previous analyses that fix $\tilde{p}$ to theory-motivated integer values, this extension provides a theory-agnostic description inspired by effective-field theory to the Kerr ringdown spectrum. Using \texttt{pyRing}, we apply the framework to GW250114 with informative priors on $M_{\rm f}$ and $D_{\rm L}$, comparing the $220$-only and $220+221$ mode models under different conditions on $γ$. The posterior distributions of $\tilde{\ell}, \tilde{p}$ and $γ$ are presented with different ringdown start times. We show that the posterior of $\tilde{p}$ remains largely prior dominated, indicating that current ringdown data cannot distinguish different scaling behaviors of the correction. The inferred constraint on $\tilde{\ell}$ is instead mainly controlled by the geometry of the allowed parameter space induced by the $γ$ condition. In particular, $γ<1$ provides the most natural prescription in the $(\tilde{\ell},\tilde{p})$ plane and yields stable but weak upper bounds of $\tilde{\ell}_{90}\simeq 83\, {\rm km}$. An analysis based on the Kullback-Leibler divergence further shows that, after accounting for the effective prior associated with $γ<1$, the data-driven information gain on $\tilde{\ell}$ and $\logγ$ is very small. At the current SNR level, the $220$-only model provides a more informative constraint than the $220+221$ model. We also perform a joint analysis including GW231123 and find that the combined constraint is dominated by GW250114.

</details>


### [37] [Bianchi IX dynamics with a phantom field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22675)
*Adel Awad,Dmitry Chirkov,Alexey Golovnev,Alexey Toporensky*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies Bianchi IX cosmological dynamics with a massless phantom field, revealing novel BKL oscillatory behavior—including simultaneous negative Kasner indices and large-magnitude negative indices—and explains recently observed volume oscillations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how a massless phantom field modifies Bianchi IX dynamics compared to vacuum or standard scalar field cases, especially regarding BKL oscillations and emergent volume oscillations.

Method: Analytical investigation of Bianchi IX equations with a massless phantom field, focusing on Kasner index behavior and asymptotic dynamics near the singularity; interpretation of prior numerical results on volume oscillations.

Result: Demonstrates that phantom fields allow two simultaneously negative Kasner indices and arbitrarily large |negative index|, enabling qualitatively new oscillatory regimes and explaining the origin of volume oscillations observed numerically.

Conclusion: The phantom field fundamentally alters the BKL structure, introducing previously forbidden dynamical features and providing an analytical basis for numerically discovered volume oscillations in Bianchi IX spacetimes.

Abstract: We consider Bianchi IX dynamics of a Universe filled with a massless phantom field. Such an exotic matter source enables regimes impossible in vacuum or with a standard scalar field. In particular, two Kasner indices of BKL oscillations can be simultaneously negative, and the absolute value of a negative index can be large. We describe the consequences of these features and explain the nature of volume oscillations recently discovered in such a system by numerical methods.

</details>


### [38] [Orientation matters: Consequences for gravitational-wave background detectability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22985)
*Nelson Christensen,Joseph D. Romano,Mairi Sakellariadou,Jishnu Suresh*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how the relative orientation and separation of Earth-based L-shaped laser interferometers affect the detectability of isotropic and anisotropic gravitational-wave backgrounds via cross-correlation searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand and quantify the impact of detector geometry—specifically physical separation and relative orientation—on the sensitivity of cross-correlation searches for gravitational-wave backgrounds.

Abstract: We investigate timelike geodesics and gravitational wave signatures of periodic motion around a static magnetically charged black hole arising from nonlinear electrodynamics and immersed in a quintessence background. We analyze the effective potential for massive particles, determine the marginally bound and innermost stable circular orbits, and classify the resulting bound trajectories using the zoom-whirl taxonomy $(\mathit{z},\mathit{w},\mathit{v})$. We show that the quintessence parameter $c_q$ systematically shifts the orbital radii, conserved quantities, and turning-point structure associated with representative periodic families.
  We then model the gravitational radiation emitted by periodic extreme-mass-ratio inspirals within the numerical kludge approximation. The resulting waveforms exhibit the characteristic burst-like structure of zoom-whirl motion, while variations in the quintessence coupling parameter modify the phase evolution, burst timing, and harmonic content of the signal. The corresponding Fourier spectra display a discrete comb-like structure, and the characteristic strain is concentrated in the millihertz band relevant for space-based detectors such as LISA. ### [40] [MaxWave Signal: Rapid, coherent maximum likelihood wavelet reconstruction of transient signals in gravitational wave data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23035)
*Sudhi Mathur,Neil J. Cornish*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends the MaxWave algorithm to enable coherent multi-detector gravitational-wave signal reconstruction and glitch rejection, using z-statistic alignment, adaptive noise weighting, and synthetic detector formation for real-time, model-independent, low-latency analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Increasing gravitational-wave detection rates due to improved detector sensitivity necessitate faster, automated, and robust analysis methods capable of distinguishing weak astrophysical signals from instrumental glitches.

Abstract: Advances in gravitational-wave detector sensitivity have increased the rate of transient signal detections, demanding faster automated analysis. We extend MaxWave, a fast maximum likelihood wavelet reconstruction algorithm, to perform coherent multi-detector signal reconstruction and glitch rejection. We coherently search for a common set of wavelets modeling the signal in all detectors. Multi-detector data are aligned using z-statistic time and phase offsets and amplitude scalings relative to the dominant reconstruction, as well as adaptive noise weightings derived from a geometrically averaged noise spectrum. By aligning and weighting individual detectors, we form a synthetic detector that amplifies non-Gaussian features, down-weights noisy detectors, and preserves Gaussian noise statistics. We extract the coherent signal using this synthetic detector, improving sensitivity to weak events while rejecting coincident glitches that lack consistent phase and amplitude evolution. Our algorithm provides real-time, low-latency, model-independent signal reconstructions, safely denoises gravitational wave data without removing transient signals, and can complement existing burst search and reconstruction frameworks through a fundamentally distinct approach, strengthening detection confidence and improving sensitivity to diverse signal morphologies.

  <summary>Details</summary>

Abstract: We investigate the polarization properties and effective stress--energy tensor of gravitational waves in metric $f(R)$ gravity within the linearized approximation around Minkowski spacetime. Owing to the additional scalar degree of freedom inherent in the theory, gravitational waves exhibit polarization states beyond the two tensor modes predicted by general relativity. Using the electric components of the linearized Riemann tensor, we derive explicit expressions for the polarization amplitudes and show that a massless scalar field excites a transverse breathing mode, whereas a massive scalar field generates both breathing and longitudinal responses through a single propagating scalar excitation. Employing the Isaacson high-frequency averaging formalism, we further derive the effective stress--energy tensor and demonstrate that both tensor and scalar perturbations contribute to the total gravitational-wave energy density. The energy transport associated with the massive scalar mode is reduced by its subluminal group velocity, leading to a frequency-dependent suppression of the scalar energy flux. These results establish a unified connection between gravitational-wave polarization and energy transport in metric $f(R)$ gravity and provide potential observational signatures for testing modified gravity with current and future gravitational-wave detectors.

</details>


### [42] [Dark matter from the quadratic spinor Lagrangian II: A spin-3/2 no-go and the uniqueness of the spin-1/2 candidate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23273)

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether elevating the spinor 1-form Ψ_μ to an independent Dirac-vector field in the QSL framework generates a genuine massive spin-3/2 dark matter mode.

Method: Exact Clifford reduction of torsional terms; analysis of second-order kinetic structure 2DΨγ₅DΨ via component expansion and spinor-curvature identity; second variation of the curvature-coupled action S = −∫ψ̄ψ R √−g with metric and scalar both composite in Ψ; spectral analysis of propagating poles.

Result: Three key results: (1) torsional mass is frame-aligned and confined to time-component sector—not uniform spin-3/2 mass; (2) 2DΨγ₅DΨ contributes only boundary terms with vanishing bulk kinetic/cross terms; (3) full dynamics reside in curvature term where metric g = Ψ⊗ₛΨ and scalar ψ̄ψ are both Ψ-composites, leading to factorized second variation δ²S and massless graviton + scalar poles only—no massive spin-3/2 mode on any background.

Conclusion: The composite spin-1/2 Dirac fermion is the sole propagating matter excitation—and thus the unique dark matter candidate—in the QSL; its interpretation as the Goldstino of metric-induced local supersymmetry breaking provides a gravitational, composite realization of the light-gravitino paradigm.

Abstract: The composite Quadratic Spinor Lagrangian (QSL) propagates a spin-1/2 Dirac fermion whose mass is generated geometrically by cosmological trace torsion. It is natural to ask whether promoting the spinor 1-form $Ψ_μ$ to an \emph{independent} Dirac-vector field yields a genuine spin-3/2 dark-matter candidate. We prove that it does not. Three results combine into a no-go theorem. First, the torsional term, computed exactly by Clifford reduction, is a frame-aligned mass confined to the time-component sector -- not a uniform spin-3/2 mass. Second, the second-order form $2 DΨγ_5 DΨ$ has identically vanishing kinetic and cross terms for the independent field: as a component expression it is the boundary part of the spinor-curvature identity and carries no bulk dynamics. Third, the genuine dynamics therefore reside in the curvature side of that identity, $S=-\int\barψψR\sqrt{-g}$, in which the metric $g=Ψ\otimes_SΨ$ and the scalar $\barψψ$ are \emph{both} composites of $Ψ$; the second variation consequently factors, $δ^2S=\mathcal Q(h_{μν}[δΨ],δΦ[δΨ])$, through the linearized metric and a scalar, both massless. Every propagating pole therefore lies on the metric light cone $k^2=0$ -- the graviton and a scalar -- and no massive spin-3/2 mode exists, on any background. This is the dynamical completion of the kinematic fact that the composite spinor 1-form has no spin-3/2 part, and it establishes the composite spin-1/2 Dirac fermion as the unique propagating matter excitation, and the unique dark-matter candidate, of the QSL. Through the super-SL(2,C) structure this surviving mode is naturally read as the Goldstino of the local supersymmetry broken by the metric condensate -- a composite, gravitational descendant of the light-gravitino dark matter of Pagels and Primack.

</details>


### [43] [Primordial Black Holes from Vector-Induced Curvature Perturbations Sourced by Primordial Magnetic Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23307)

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel post-inflationary mechanism—driven by vector perturbations from primordial magnetic fields during a stiff/kination epoch—to enhance small-scale curvature perturbations and generate observationally relevant primordial black holes (PBHs) as dark matter candidates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the generation of a substantial abundance of primordial black holes (PBHs), which requires significant enhancement of primordial curvature perturbations on small scales—beyond what standard inflation typically produces.

Result: Vector-induced curvature perturbations are infrared-amplified during kination and scale as P_R(k) ∝ k^{-5}; this spectrum can yield PBHs with mass ~10^{−12} M_⊙ and abundance sufficient to account for a sizable fraction of dark matter.

Conclusion: A kination-era amplification of vector-mode-sourced second-order scalar perturbations provides a viable, physically motivated pathway to PBH dark matter, linking primordial magnetogenesis, early-universe dynamics, and observable PBH signatures.

Abstract: Generating an appreciable abundance of primordial black holes (PBHs) requires a substantial enhancement of primordial curvature perturbations on small scales. In this work, we propose a new post-inflationary mechanism in which such an enhancement arises during a stiff, or kination, epoch. The mechanism is driven by metric vector perturbations sourced by the vector component of the electromagnetic stress-energy tensor associated with primordial magnetic fields (PMFs). Since these first-order vector modes remain approximately constant during kination, they act as persistent nonlinear sources for second-order scalar perturbations. We show that the resulting vector-induced curvature perturbations are amplified toward the infrared cutoff of the kination band and exhibit the characteristic scaling $\mathcal P_{\mathcal R}(k)\propto k^{-5}$. As a concrete realization, we consider PMFs generated in a Ratra-type magnetogenesis scenario and find that the induced curvature perturbations can produce PBHs with an abundance large enough to constitute a substantial fraction of the dark matter.

</details>


### [44] [Scattered wave functions and worldline instantons for particle production in curved spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23389)
*Philip Semrén,Greger Torgrimsson*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops two complementary methods—the extended scattered-wave-function (SWF) method and the open-worldline-instanton method with pre-exponential factor—to compute spin-1/2 particle-antiparticle pair production in multidimensional curved spacetimes; both yield consistent results for 2D metrics, with SWF offering numerical precision and the instanton method enabling scalability to higher dimensions and extreme regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable accurate and scalable computation of fermionic pair production in generic curved spacetimes with nontrivial multi-coordinate dependence—beyond the reach of existing methods limited to symmetry-restricted or low-dimensional settings.

Method: 1) Extension of the scattered-wave-function (SWF) method from electromagnetic to curved spacetime backgrounds; 2) Completion of the open-worldline-instanton formalism by deriving the full pre-exponential factor for the pair-production probability.

Result: Both methods yield quantitatively consistent pair-production probabilities for several two-dimensional metrics; SWF delivers numerically exact results efficiently in those cases, while the instanton method demonstrates superior asymptotic and dimensional scalability.

Conclusion: The developed methods constitute novel, complementary tools for first-principles study of quantum vacuum pair production in realistic, multidimensional gravitational backgrounds—bridging a key gap between analytical tractability and physical generality.

Abstract: We study the production of spin-$1/2$ particle-antiparticle pairs in curved spacetimes with nontrivial dependence on more than one coordinate. To this end, we develop two complementary approaches. First, we extend the scattered-wave-function (SWF) method, originally introduced for pair production in electromagnetic backgrounds, to curved spacetime backgrounds. Second, we complete the development of an open-worldline-instanton method by deriving the pre-exponential factor of the pair-production probability. We apply both methods to several two-dimensional metrics and find good agreement between the resulting probabilities. While the SWF approach provides numerically exact results and is particularly efficient for the examples considered here, the instanton approach offers favorable scaling to higher-dimensional backgrounds and more extreme parameter regimes. These methods provide new tools for studying pair production in multidimensional gravitational backgrounds beyond the reach of many existing approaches.

</details>


### [45] [High-Stability Deformable Mirrors for Correcting Non-Axisymmetric Residual Aberrations in Thermal Compensation of Future Gravitational Wave Interferometers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23415)
*Luciano Antonio Corubolo,Matteo Lorenzini,Lorenzo Aiello,Elisabetta Cesarini,Maria Cifaldi,Matteo Ianni,Diana Lumaca,Yury Minenkov,Ilaria Nardecchia,Alessio Rocchi,Claudia Taranto,Viviana Fafone*

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current thermal correction methods (e.g., CO2 laser projectors, thermal actuators) in Advanced LIGO and Advanced Virgo only address axisymmetric distortions; non-axisymmetric wavefront aberrations remain uncorrected—especially critical for next-generation high-power detectors like the Einstein Telescope.

Method: A deformable mirror is used to shape the phase of a CO2 laser beam; the required phase map is computed using a modified Gerchberg-Saxton algorithm to generate target asymmetric intensity patterns on lensing optics.

Result: Simulations confirm accurate projection of desired intensity patterns; experimental validation demonstrates consistent reproduction of those patterns.

Conclusion: Deformable mirrors coupled with phase retrieval via modified Gerchberg-Saxton offer a viable, frequency-independent solution for mitigating non-axisymmetric optical aberrations in high-power gravitational wave interferometers.

Abstract: In gravitational wave detectors, optical aberrations arise mainly from laser absorption in coatings and production process defects in the optics along the laser path. If left uncorrected, these optical path distortions drive the interferometer away from its optimal working point, degrading both stability and sensitivity. Future instruments such as the Einstein Telescope high-frequency detector will operate with unprecedented circulating power, further amplifying the aberration budget. In the current detectors Advanced Virgo and Advanced LIGO, the axisymmetric distortions are corrected using thermal actuators and CO2 laser projectors, however, non-axisymmetric wavefront distortions remain unmitigated. Deformable mirrors are investigated as a flexible solution for mitigating such defects: by shaping the CO2 beam phase upon reflection, they can imprint the required asymmetric intensity pattern on the lensing optics without introducing frequency dependent noise. The target phase map is computed via a modified Gerchberg-Saxton algorithm. We present simulations of this projection strategy and experimental validation demonstrating consistent reproduction of the desired intensity patterns.

</details>

*Roh-Suan Tung*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes that spacetime torsion alone—without Higgs or free mass parameters—generates the mass of fermionic dark matter produced gravitationally via stochastic gravitational waves; the resulting Dirac mass is locked to the Hubble rate at production, yielding a one-parameter relic abundance prediction Ωh² ∝ H_*^{5/2}, with the composite spin-1/2 fermion uniquely identified as the dark matter candidate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the need for unspecified late-time physics to generate mass for gravitationally produced dark matter fermions, by identifying a fundamental geometric origin—spacetime torsion—in Einstein–Cartan gravity.

Result: A torsion-generated, Hubble-locked Dirac mass M_eff ≃ (c_χ/√6)H_* with no pseudoscalar or mixing terms; relic abundance Ωh² ∝ H_*^{5/2}; exclusion of propagating spin-3/2 modes ensures uniqueness of the composite spin-1/2 fermion as dark matter.

Conclusion: Spacetime torsion in Einstein–Cartan gravity fully determines the mass and abundance of gravitationally produced dark matter fermions, eliminating ad hoc mass inputs and establishing a geometric, predictive dark matter framework.

Abstract: The gravitational production of dark matter from stochastic gravitational waves requires the produced fermion to acquire a mass by unspecified late-time physics. We show that this mass is supplied by spacetime torsion alone -- no Higgs sector and no free mass parameter. In the Quadratic Spinor Lagrangian formulation of general relativity, extended to Einstein--Cartan, a cosmological spinor condensate generates a vectorial trace torsion $K\propto\dotχ/χ$; an explicit Clifford reduction confers on the produced spin-1/2 fermion a pure Dirac mass $M_{\rm eff}=(1/\sqrt6)\,|\dotχ/χ|$, with no pseudoscalar or cross terms, locked to the Hubble rate at production, $M_{\rm eff}\simeq(c_χ/\sqrt6)H_*$. The relic abundance is then a one-parameter prediction, $Ωh^2\propto H_*^{5/2}$, and the spin is fixed: the same framework admits no propagating spin-3/2 mode, so the composite spin-1/2 Dirac fermion is its unique dark-matter candidate.

</details>


### [47] [Closed and broken electromagnetic orbits in Kerr--Newman spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23446)
*Erasmo Caponio,Giulio Sanzeni,Stefan Suhr*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates timelike solutions of the Lorentz force equation in the sub-extremal Kerr–Newman spacetime, focusing on electromagnetic orbits—closed, spherical, flyby, and broken—in the time-machine region where the axial Killing field is timelike; it constructs explicit orbits, derives parameter relations, and demonstrates energy extraction and causality-violating processes via broken orbits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand charged-particle dynamics in the causally pathological time-machine region of Kerr–Newman black holes, especially how electromagnetic interactions enable closed or causality-violating trajectories, and to model charge-changing decay processes with momentum and charge conservation.

Method: Analytical study of the Lorentz force equation in Kerr–Newman geometry; construction of smooth closed and spherical electromagnetic orbits tangent to ∂_φ; derivation of explicit algebraic relations among radius, charge-to-mass ratio, energy, and angular momentum; classification of equatorial orbits as flybys using turning-point analysis; introduction and construction of 'broken electromagnetic orbits' satisfying four-momentum and charge conservation at kinks.

Result: Existence proofs and explicit constructions of: (i) closed circular orbits in the positive equatorial time-machine region, tied to sign-reversed charge-to-mass ratio; (ii) spherical equatorial orbits with analytic radius–parameter relations; (iii) flyby orbits with radial turning points in 𝔗; (iv) closed broken orbits enabling causality violation via concatenation of flyby branches and a spherical orbit—realized both in r > 0 and r < 0 regions inside the inner horizon.

Conclusion: Charged particles in the Kerr–Newman time-machine region admit rich orbital structure—including stable spherical, scattering flyby, and causality-violating broken orbits—enabling both energy extraction and closed timelike-like worldlines when charge-to-mass ratio flips sign relative to the black hole; this highlights intrinsic interplay between electromagnetic coupling, spacetime geometry, and causal pathology.

Abstract: We study future-pointing timelike solutions of the Lorentz force equation in the sub-extremal Kerr--Newman spacetime, with special attention to the time-machine region $\mathfrak T$, where the axial Killing field $\partial_φ$ is timelike. We first construct smooth closed electromagnetic orbits tangent to $\partial_φ$ in the positive equatorial part of $\mathfrak T$: the radius of such a circle determines, and is determined by, the charge-to-mass ratio of the particle which must have opposite sign to that of the black hole charge. We then prove the existence of spherical electromagnetic orbits contained in the equatorial time-machine region and derive explicit relations between their radius, charge-to-mass ratio, energy and angular momentum. Next we give sufficient conditions ensuring that an equatorial electromagnetic orbit is a flyby orbit with radial turning point in $\mathfrak T$. Finally, to describe charged-particle decay processes whose fragments have different charge-to-mass ratios, we introduce the notion of a broken electromagnetic orbit: a continuous, piecewise smooth, future-pointing worldline whose smooth pieces solve the Lorentz force equation. Imposing conservation of kinetic four-momentum and electric charge at the decay vertices, we exhibit an energy extraction process followed by a causality-violating one. The latter is realized by a closed broken electromagnetic orbit, which we construct in both the $r$-positive and the $r$-negative regions inside the inner horizon, by concatenating two flyby branches sharing a common radial turning value $\bar r$, and having charge-to-mass ratios with opposite sign to that of the black hole charge, with a spherical electromagnetic orbit of radius $\bar r$.

</details>


### [48] [Dark matter from the quadratic spinor Lagrangian I: Geometric mass for a gravitationally produced spin-1/2 fermion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23447)
*Roh-Suan Tung*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces the Quadratic Spinor Lagrangian (QSL) framework in Einstein–Cartan gravity to geometrically generate Dirac mass for dark fermions produced via gravitational-wave freeze-in, linking their mass directly to the Hubble rate at production and yielding a predictive relic abundance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To supply a geometric origin for the mass of dark fermions generated by gravitational-wave-induced freeze-in — a mechanism previously requiring ad hoc mass generation — thereby closing a key gap in the Maleknejad–Kopp (2026) scenario.

Method: Develops the Quadratic Spinor Lagrangian (QSL) formulation of general relativity extended to Einstein–Cartan theory; constructs a spinor 1-form from a Dirac field, identifies its gamma-traceless part (vanishing identically), computes torsion sourced by a cosmological spinor condensate, and performs explicit Clifford reduction to derive the effective Dirac mass. Analyzes propagation content to exclude spin-3/2 degrees of freedom.

Result: Derives a geometric, non-free Dirac mass $M_{\text{eff}} = (1/\sqrt{6}) |\dot{\chi}/\chi| \simeq (c_\chi/\sqrt{6}) H_*$, locked to the Hubble rate $H_*$ at production; shows relic abundance scales as $\Omega h^2 \propto H_*^{5/2}$; proves the only propagating fermionic excitation is a composite spin-1/2 Dirac fermion — the unique QSL dark-matter candidate.

Conclusion: The QSL framework in Einstein–Cartan gravity fully realizes geometric fermion mass generation for GW-induced freeze-in dark matter, eliminating the need for external mass mechanisms and yielding a highly constrained, predictive dark-matter model tied to early-universe expansion dynamics and asymptotically free scalar cosmology.

Abstract: The gravitational-wave induced freeze-in of Maleknejad and Kopp (2026) produces dark fermions from a stochastic gravitational-wave background, but requires them to acquire mass by separate means. We develop the Quadratic Spinor Lagrangian (QSL) formulation of general relativity, extended to Einstein--Cartan, as a framework that supplies this mass geometrically. The spinor 1-form built from a single Dirac field is purely spin-1/2 -- its gamma-traceless (spin-3/2) part vanishes identically -- so the propagating excitation is a Dirac fermion, the same content as the produced Weyl fermion. A cosmological spinor condensate sources a vectorial trace torsion $K\propto\dotχ/χ$, and an explicit Clifford reduction shows that this torsion gives the fermion a pure Dirac mass $M_{eff}=(1/\sqrt6)\,|\dotχ/χ|$, with no pseudoscalar or cross terms. The mass is not a free parameter but is locked to the Hubble rate at production, $M_{eff}\simeq(c_χ/\sqrt6)H_*$, making the relic abundance a function of essentially the single scale $H_*$ ($Ωh^2\propto H_*^{5/2}$) and supplying the mass the parent mechanism must postulate. Whether promoting the spinor 1-form to an independent field yields a propagating spin-3/2 candidate is a distinct dynamical question; Paper II shows that it does not -- the QSL channels all propagation into the gravitational sector -- so the composite spin-1/2 Dirac fermion is the unique QSL dark-matter candidate. We discuss the resulting dark-matter phenomenology and its link to asymptotically free scalar-field cosmology.

</details>


### [49] [Affine quantization of the dynamical Reissner--Nordström region](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23552)

TL;DR: This paper applies affine quantization to the dynamical region of the Reissner–Nordström black hole in a minisuperspace model, yielding separable Wheeler–DeWitt solutions (Hermite and Gaussian-like), modified short-distance behavior, and charge-dependent semiclassical wave packet dynamics.

Abstract: We study the quantum dynamics of the dynamical region of the Reissner--Nordström geometry using a minisuperspace reduction and affine quantization, which is naturally suited for positive-definite geometrical variables. The resulting Wheeler--DeWitt equation becomes separable, yielding Hermite-polynomial modes in one sector and Gaussian-like radial solutions in the other. Affine quantization introduces additional short-distance contributions that modify the small-radius behaviour of the wave function. By constructing normalizable semiclassical wave packets, we analyze the resulting probability distributions in minisuperspace and the role played by the electric charge in the quantum dynamics. Our results extend previous affine-quantization studies of the Schwarzschild case to the charged Reissner--Nordström geometry.

</details>


### [50] [Equatorial Periodic Orbits and Gravitational Wave Phenomenology around Spherically-symmetric vacuum solution in Freund-Nambu scalar-tensor gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23635)

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe strong-field gravity and constrain scalar-tensor extensions of general relativity using orbital dynamics and gravitational wave phenomenology in a generalized naked singularity spacetime.

Result: Scalar-particle coupling $g_s > 0$ shifts ISCO inward; distinct zoom-whirl orbits are identified; scalar-tensor corrections induce macroscopic temporal dephasing in high-frequency GW bursts while preserving spatial orbit topology.

Conclusion: The characteristic GW phase shifts serve as robust observational signatures for space-based interferometers like LISA to test scalar-tensor gravity in the strong-field regime.

Abstract: We investigate test particle dynamics and gravitational wave (GW) phenomenology in an exact spherically symmetric vacuum solution of Freund - Nambu scalar - tensor gravity. This framework generalizes the Janis - Newman - Winicour (JNW) naked singularity via a geometric non - linear coupling $q$ and a direct scalar - particle coupling $g_s$. We demonstrate that these parameters systematically modify the Innermost Stable Circular Orbit (ISCO) - which shifts inward for $g_s > 0$ - and the Marginally Bound Orbit (MBO). Furthermore, we classify bound periodic trajectories to isolate extreme zoom - whirl orbits exhibiting intense periapsis precession. By applying the Numerical Kludge method to Extreme Mass - Ratio Inspirals (EMRIs), we reveal that scalar - tensor corrections induce a macroscopic temporal dephasing in high - frequency GW bursts, even when the orbit's spatial topology is preserved. These unique phase shifts offer a robust diagnostic signature for future space-based observatories like LISA to probe the strong - field regime and constrain scalar - tensor extensions of general relativity.

</details>
