### [51] [Gauge invariant generalizations of the Proca equation and the Yang-Mills-Proca equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20828)
*Nikolay Marchuk*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a gauge-invariant generalization of the Proca equation by introducing an auxiliary vector field, and extends it to a non-Abelian setting compatible with Yang–Mills theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the lack of gauge invariance in the standard Proca equation while preserving its physical description of massive spin-1 fields, avoiding reliance on Stueckelberg's mechanism.

Method: Introduces an additional vector field to modify the Proca Lagrangian, ensuring gauge invariance; then generalizes the construction to non-Abelian gauge groups, yielding Yang-Mills-Proca equations.

Result: A manifestly gauge-invariant formulation of massive vector fields is obtained; the extended Yang-Mills-Proca equations retain local non-Abelian symmetry while accommodating mass terms without breaking gauge invariance.

Conclusion: The proposed framework provides a consistent, gauge-invariant alternative to the Proca and Stueckelberg formalisms for massive spin-1 fields, applicable to both Abelian and non-Abelian contexts.

Abstract: The Proca equations (1936) are used in quantum field theory to describe vector bosons (spin 1) with a nonzero mass. The Proca equations are not gauge invariant. In contrast to Stueckelberg's approach (1938), this article presents a gauge invariant generalization of the Proca equation by introducing an additional vector field into the Proca equation. The results are extended to the Yang-Mills-Proca equations, leading to equations with non-Abelian gauge symmetry.

</details>


### [52] [Poisson structures of deformed $W$-algebras and classical $W$-algebras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21150)
*Dong Jun Choi,Uhi Rinn Suh*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates how classical W-algebras of type A arise as limits of q-deformed W-algebras, using Hamiltonian reduction of deformed affine Poisson algebras and establishing a correspondence between generators via affine Poisson vertex algebras.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the relationship between classical and q-deformed W-algebras in type A by realizing the former as a limit of the latter.

Method: Hamiltonian reduction of deformed affine Poisson algebras; derivation of affine Poisson vertex algebras from their Poisson structures; identification of generator sets between deformed and classical W-algebras.

Result: A rigorous construction showing that classical W-algebras in type A emerge as q → 1 limits of q-deformed W-algebras, with explicit generator correspondence established via Poisson vertex algebra formalism.

Conclusion: The limit procedure is well-defined and structurally consistent, confirming the compatibility of deformation quantization and Hamiltonian reduction frameworks for W-algebras in type A.

Abstract: In this paper, we explain how classical $W$-algebras can be obtained as limits of $q$-deformed $W$-algebras in type $A$. To this end, we give a detailed examination of the construction of deformed $W$-algebras via Hamiltonian reduction of deformed affine Poisson algebras. We first derive an affine Poisson vertex algebra (PVA) from the Poisson structure of the deformed affine Poisson algebra. This construction identifies a specific set of generators of the deformed $W$-algebra with generators of the classical $W$-algebra.

</details>


### [53] [Measuring a quantum system without problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21211)
*Marco Falconi,Clotilde Fermanian Kammerer,Raphaël Gautier*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper resolves the quantum measurement problem by introducing a measurement scheme where the probe undergoes a quantum-to-classical transition while adhering to standard quantum mechanical measurement postulates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the century-old quantum measurement problem—the inability to obtain definite outcomes from quantum-scale measurements.

Method: Mathematical proof of existence of a measurement scheme ensuring probe-induced quantum-to-classical transition, consistent with standard quantum measurement axioms (e.g., Born rule, state collapse, repeatability).

Result: Demonstration that a physically realizable measurement process exists wherein the probe evolves irreversibly into a classical-like state, yielding definite outcomes without violating quantum formalism.

Conclusion: The measurement problem is not fundamental but operational: definite outcomes emerge via controlled probe dynamics satisfying all standard quantum measurement requirements.

Abstract: The process of measuring quantum observables has been plagued, since the inception of quantum mechanics, by the so-called measurement problem: it is impossible to read a definite outcome on a quantum scale. In this paper, we overcome this century-old problem by proving the existence of a measurement scheme in which the probe undergoes a quantum-to-classical transition, all the while satisfying the requirements of measurements in standard quantum mechanics.

</details>


### [54] [Integrating Polchinski's equation by convergent binary tree expansions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21644)
*Paul Obernolte,Manfred Salmhofer*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: The paper provides a convergent binary-tree expansion solution to Polchinski's equation for the Wilsonian effective action in fermionic field theories, under conditions of finite determinant and decay of the fermionic covariance; combinatorial estimates on leaf-labeled binary trees are central to the convergence proof.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously solve Polchinski's exact renormalization group equation for the Wilsonian effective action in fermionic systems, where convergence of perturbative expansions is nontrivial due to combinatorial complexity and infrared/ultraviolet coupling.

Method: Constructs a formal solution to Polchinski's equation as a series indexed by binary trees; establishes convergence via new combinatorial bounds on the number of leaf-labeled binary trees and analytic control using determinant and decay properties of the fermionic covariance.

Result: Proof of absolute convergence of the binary-tree expansion for the Wilsonian effective action in general fermionic QFT and quantum statistical mechanical models satisfying finite-determinant and exponential (or sufficient polynomial) decay of the covariance.

Conclusion: The binary-tree expansion yields a mathematically well-defined, non-perturbative representation of the Wilsonian effective action for fermionic systems, enabling rigorous renormalization group analysis beyond standard Feynman diagrammatics.

Abstract: We give a solution to Polchinski's equation for the Wilsonian effective action in terms of an expansion in binary trees, and prove that this expansion converges in fermionic field theories, provided the fermionic covariance has finite determinant and decay constants. A novel element of the proof are detailed combinatorial estimates for the number of leaf trees associated to binary trees. The method can be used on standard models of fermionic quantum field theory and quantum statistical mechanics.

</details>


### [55] [Heat Kernel and Resurgence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21909)
*Si Li,Yong Li,Xinxing Tang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the resurgent structure of short-time heat kernel asymptotics using Picard-Lefschetz theory, revealing connections between real and complex geodesics via alien operators and Morse-Floer theory, validated on the hyperbolic plane.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover hidden complex-geometric information encoded in the Borel transform of the heat kernel's 1-Gevrey small-time expansion, beyond classical real geodesic contributions.

Method: Applies Picard-Lefschetz theory to the holomorphic energy functional on complexified path space; formulates an infinite-dimensional Morse-Floer-type problem; introduces pointed alien operators acting on asymptotic expansions; tests predictions on H².

Result: Establishes a heat-kernel analogue of the Picard-Lefschetz/Alien correspondence; shows alien operators generate formal sectors from other holomorphic geodesics, with coefficients given by signed counts of Morse flow trajectories; confirmed explicitly on H².

Conclusion: The resurgent structure of the heat kernel is governed by holomorphic geodesics and their Morse-theoretic interactions, unifying asymptotic analysis, complex geometry, and resurgence via Picard-Lefschetz theory.

Abstract: We study the resurgent structure of short-time heat kernel asymptotics from the viewpoint of Picard-Lefschetz theory. For a real analytic Riemannian manifold, we show the heat kernel admits a 1-Gevrey small-time expansion whose Borel transform detects complex-geometric data beyond the real geodesic sector. We formulate an infinite-dimensional Picard-Lefschetz problem of Morse-Floer type for the holomorphic energy functional on the complexified path space, and propose a heat-kernel analogue of the Picard-Lefschetz/Alien correspondence. In this framework, pointed alien operators acting on the asymptotic expansion associated with the real geodesic are predicted to produce the formal heat-kernel sectors associated with other holomorphic geodesics, with coefficients given by signed counts of connecting trajectories of the Morse flow. We perform a confirming test of this proposal on the hyperbolic plane $H^2$.

</details>


### [56] [The asymmetric five vertex model on a rectangle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22004)
*Jan de Gier,Richard Kenyon,Michael Wheeler,Yuyang Zhou*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: The paper derives a determinantal expression for the inhomogeneous asymmetric five-vertex model on a rectangular lattice with arbitrary top/bottom boundary conditions, overcoming limitations of standard free-fermion or non-intersecting path methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard non-intersecting lattice path (free fermion) techniques fail for this inhomogeneous asymmetric five-vertex model with arbitrary boundary conditions, necessitating a new approach to obtain a determinantal representation.

Method: Algebraic and combinatorial analysis tailored to the inhomogeneous asymmetric five-vertex model; derivation of a determinantal formula without relying on free-fermion mapping or non-intersecting path bijections.

Result: A novel determinantal expression for the partition function (or related observables) of the inhomogeneous asymmetric five-vertex model on a rectangle with arbitrary top and bottom boundary conditions.

Conclusion: Determinantal structure persists beyond free-fermion solvability, revealing deeper algebraic or integrable structure in this asymmetric, inhomogeneous setting.

Abstract: We derive a determinantal expression for the inhomogeneous asymmetric five vertex model in a rectangular geometry with arbitrary boundary conditions at the bottom and top. Standard non-intersecting lattice path, or free fermion, approaches are not applicable and the determinantal form thus is not immediate.

</details>


### [57] [Electromagnetic Characterization of Magnetic Bar: Case of Square Cross-Section Shape](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22117)
*Taha El Hajji,Bruno Ricardo Marques,Lars Sjöberg*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a 2D analytical model for square-section solid magnetic bars under sinusoidal excitation, deriving exact expressions for impedance, fields, flux, and core losses using Maxwell’s equations and complex permeability; it introduces an apparent permeability parameter enabling fast magnetic steel characterization without FEA.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a fast, accurate, and physically insightful alternative to computationally expensive Finite Element Analysis (FEA) for characterizing magnetic steel behavior—particularly core losses and frequency-dependent permeability—in solid magnetic bars.

Method: Analytical solution of Maxwell's equations in Cartesian coordinates for a square-section solid magnetic bar; incorporation of complex permeability; separation of variables via hyperbolic functions; mathematical decoupling of eddy current and hysteresis losses; derivation of mutual impedance, internal magnetic fields, flux linkage, and core losses.

Result: Exact closed-form expressions for impedance, flux, and field distribution; identification of edge flux accumulation and 2D skin effect; decoupled eddy current and hysteresis loss components; introduction of a frequency-dependent apparent relative permeability that decreases from 500 to 300 over 1 Hz–1 MHz and exhibits a resistance peak near 700 kHz indicating transition to surface-dominated loss regime.

Conclusion: The proposed 2D analytical model provides both physical insight and computational efficiency for magnetic steel characterization, enabling rapid prediction of core losses and permeability dispersion without resorting to FEA.

Abstract: This paper presents a complete two-dimensional theoretical model for the electromagnetic behavior of square-section solid magnetic bars under sinusoidal loading. Through the application of Maxwell's equations within a Cartesian coordinate system and the integration of complex permeability, exact mathematical expressions are derived for mutual impedance, internal magnetic fields, flux, and core losses. Hyperbolic functions are utilized to separate the variables, enabling the accurate representation of edge flux accumulation and the 2D skin effect. In addition to mathematically decoupling eddy current and hysteresis losses, this formulation yields a new apparent permeability parameter. This parameter establishes a fast, reliable method for magnetic steel characterization that bypasses the extensive processing times associated with Finite Element Analysis (FEA). Numerical results over 1 Hz-1 MHz show the apparent relative permeability decreasing from 500 to 300 and a characteristic resistance peak near 700 kHz, marking the transition from volumetric to surface-dominated loss regimes.

</details>


### [58] [Level lines of the Gaussian free field and $c=1$ degenerate conformal blocks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22228)
*Alex Karrila,Eveliina Peltola,Lukas Schoug*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a rigorous connection between crossing probabilities of Gaussian free field (GFF) level lines and conformal field theory (CFT) with central charge c=1, showing they are encoded in degenerate conformal blocks solving BPZ equations; it also proves convergence of metric graph GFF level-set crossing probabilities to these CFT predictions, with combinatorial constraints on admissible boundary conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously link stochastic geometric observables of the Gaussian free field—specifically crossing probabilities of its level lines—to conformal field theory predictions, and to validate this correspondence via scaling limits from discrete approximations (metric graph GFF).

Method: Analytical and probabilistic techniques: conformal mapping, SLE theory (particularly fused multiple SLE₄), representation-theoretic construction of fused Specht polynomials, BPZ partial differential equation analysis, and scaling limit arguments for metric graph GFF. The authors leverage degeneracy of primary fields at c=1 and monotonicity constraints on boundary data aligned with generalized Dyck paths/semi-standard Young tableaux.

Result: Crossing probabilities of GFF level lines on simply connected domains with piecewise constant Dirichlet boundary data are exactly given by ratios of c=1 degenerate conformal blocks (solving arbitrary-order BPZ PDEs) or equivalently by ratios of partition functions of fused multiple SLE₄ curves expressed via fused Specht polynomials; these formulas are confirmed as the scaling limit of metric graph GFF level-set crossing probabilities.

Conclusion: The geometry of level-set percolation for both continuum and metric-graph Gaussian free fields admits a precise CFT description at c=1, where only a subset of combinatorially allowed boundary conditions—those satisfying monotonicity constraints corresponding to specific semi-standard Young tableaux—yield physically realizable GFF observables.

Abstract: We consider Gaussian free field (GFF) on simply connected domains with piecewise constant Dirichlet boundary data. We show that the crossing probabilities for its level lines are determined by conformal blocks of primary fields in a conformal field theory (CFT) with central charge $c = 1$ which are degenerate at each insertion. Alternatively, the crossing probabilities are ratios of explicit partition functions of fused multiple $\mathrm{SLE}_4$ curves, which can be written in terms of fused Specht polynomials introduced recently by Lafay, Peltola, & Roussillon in a representation-theoretic context.
  We also prove that for the metric graph GFF introduced by Lupu, with appropriate boundary conditions, the crossing probabilities for its level sets converge in the scaling limit to our formulas. In particular, the geometry of the level-set percolation for both the continuum GFF and the metric graph GFF has a CFT description in terms of the aforementioned $c=1$ conformal blocks, which are linearly independent and solve the Belavin-Polyakov-Zamolodchikov (BPZ) PDEs of arbitrary orders.
  Interestingly, not all combinatorial boundary conditions are amenable for the GFF models -- while the CFT contains conformal blocks of primary fields labeled by generalized Dyck paths (i.e., semi-standard Young tableaux), the ones appearing in the above models satisfy specific monotonicity constraints.

</details>


### [59] [Singular barriers and quartic integrability breaking in the TTW system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22322)
*Adrian M. Escobar Ruiz,Miguel E. Gómez Quintanar,Lidia Jiménez-Lara,Jaume Llibre*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a quartic deformation of the Tempesta-Turbiner-Winternitz (TTW) system, showing that small quartic coupling κ breaks integrability via Poincaré's nonintegrability criterion, evidenced by phase-locked orbits and chaotic dynamics; inverse-square barriers confine motion but do not restore integrability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how a symmetric quartic perturbation affects integrability and dynamical structure of the TTW system—interpolating between the superintegrable Smorodinsky-Winternitz oscillator and the nonintegrable Contopoulos oscillator—while probing the role of singular inverse-square potentials in phase-space transport.

Method: Resonant averaging for small κ to construct phase-locked periodic orbits; application of Poincaré's nonintegrability criterion via analysis of characteristic multipliers of the reduced Poincaré map; numerical diagnostics including Poincaré sections and finite-time Lyapunov exponent maps to assess chaos onset and invariant curve breakup.

Result: For sufficiently small nonzero κ and γ > 0, a phase-locked periodic orbit exists whose reduced Poincaré map lacks unit characteristic multipliers—implying local nonintegrability (no second C¹ first integral nearby); numerics confirm chaotic layer growth with energy and κ, while TTW walls suppress global transport without restoring integrability.

Conclusion: The quartic deformation destroys local integrability robustly for small κ, and singular barriers localize dynamics but cannot compensate for nonintegrability induced by coupling; the system exhibits mixed regular-chaotic behavior with energy- and coupling-dependent chaos strength.

Abstract: We study a symmetric quartic deformation of the classical ($k=1$) Tempesta-Turbiner-Winternitz system, $H=\frac{1}{2}\left(P_X^2+P_Y^2\right)+X^2+Y^2 +\fracγ{X^2}+\fracγ{Y^2} +κ\,X^2Y^2 $, which interpolates between the maximally superintegrable Smorodinsky-Winternitz oscillator and the quartically coupled Contopoulos oscillator. For $γ>0$, the inverse-square terms generate impenetrable walls at ($X=0$) and ($Y=0$), splitting configuration space into invariant sectors. We show that, for sufficiently small nonzero $κ$, resonant averaging produces a phase-locked periodic orbit whose fixed-energy reduced Poincaré map has no unit characteristic multiplier. The Poincaré's nonintegrability criterion then excludes a second independent $C^1$ first integral in any invariant neighbourhood of this orbit. The result is local in phase space and perturbative in $κ$. At finite coupling, Poincaré sections and finite-time Lyapunov maps show the breakup of invariant curves and the growth of chaotic layers with increasing energy and quartic coupling. Comparison with the barrier-free Contopoulos limit shows that the singular TTW walls suppress phase-space transport without restoring integrability.

</details>


### [60] [Localized oscillation of an Euler--Bernoulli beam with time-varying parameters on a visco-elastic foundation: asymptotics, adiabatic invariant, and equivalent Hamiltonian system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22444)
*Ekaterina V. Shishkina,Serge N. Gavrilov,Yulia A. Mochalova*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the localized oscillation of an Euler–Bernoulli beam on a visco-elastic foundation coupled to a damped discrete oscillator, with slowly varying parameters; it derives consistent amplitude formulas for the conservative case using three analytic methods (asymptotics, adiabatic invariance, Hamiltonian equivalence), and obtains the amplitude in the dissipative case solely via asymptotics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how slow temporal variations in system parameters affect localized oscillations in a coupled beam-oscillator system with visco-elastic support, particularly distinguishing conservative versus dissipative dynamics.

Method: Three analytical approaches for the conservative case: (1) asymptotic analysis, (2) adiabatic invariance of the action integral for trapped waves, and (3) equivalence to a time-varying Hamiltonian system; only asymptotics is applied to the dissipative (visco-elastic + damped oscillator) case.

Result: Identical amplitude expressions for the conservative case across all three methods; a distinct amplitude formula for the dissipative case derived exclusively via asymptotics.

Conclusion: The amplitude evolution under slow parameter variation is robustly captured by multiple conservative frameworks, validating adiabatic invariance as a unifying principle; in the presence of dissipation, asymptotics remains effective but other conservative-based methods no longer apply.

Abstract: We consider localized oscillation of an Euler--Bernoulli beam on a visco-elastic foundation coupled to a damped discrete oscillator. All parameters of the system independently vary in time in a slow manner. For the conservative case, we use three various analytic approaches. Namely, these are asymptotics, the method based on the adiabatic invariance of the action of a trapped wave, and the consideration of the equivalent Hamiltonian system. All approaches result in the same formula for the amplitude of oscillation. In the dissipative case, we obtain the amplitude of oscillation only utilizing the asymptotic approach.

</details>


### [61] [Minimum Virtual Proper Time and Finite Mass--Charge Matching in QED](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22666)
*Mustafa Bakr*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a finite-proper-time formulation of QED that preserves gauge covariance and UV finiteness by imposing a physical lower cutoff $s_0 = \Lambda^{-2}$ on Schwinger proper-time integrals, yielding finite, transverse vacuum polarization, positive spectral functions, unitary fermion propagation, and a calculable finite anomalous magnetic moment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve UV divergences in standard QED while preserving gauge covariance, unitarity, and reflection positivity—without ad hoc renormalization—by introducing a physically motivated proper-time cutoff.

Method: Constructs a gauge-covariant generating functional with finite Schwinger proper-time integrals (lower bound $s_0 = \Lambda^{-2}$); defines closed loops via heat-kernel determinant and open lines via functional differentiation of a finite-proper-time kernel; proves Osterwalder–Schrader positivity, Ward–Takahashi identity, and spectral properties rigorously.

Result: All one-loop quantities—including vacuum polarization (exactly transverse and finite), fermion mass shift (0.482 MeV for $\Lambda = 13$ TeV), anomalous magnetic moment, and dressed propagator spectral function—are finite and physically consistent; $Z_2 > 0$ confirms fermion unitarity; worldline bound $Q \le 0$ ensures Euclidean UV finiteness at fixed order.

Conclusion: Finite-proper-time QED provides a mathematically consistent, UV-finite, gauge-covariant, and unitary alternative to standard QED, with predictive power beyond the standard model (e.g., $O(m^2/\Lambda^2)$ corrections), grounded in reflection positivity and spectral reality.

Abstract: We formulate a finite-proper-time version of QED defined by a gauge-covariant generating functional in which every Schwinger proper-time integral has a physical lower endpoint $s_0=Λ^{-2}$ that is not removed. Closed fermion loops are defined by the gauge-covariant heat-kernel determinant, and open fermion lines are obtained by functional differentiation of the same finite-proper-time open-line kernel. The Ward--Takahashi identity follows exactly from gauge covariance to all orders. The vacuum polarisation is finite and exactly transverse. The on-shell mass relation gives 0.482$$ MeV for an illustrative choice $Λ=13\$ TeV at one loop. The free finite-proper-time propagators are proved to satisfy Osterwalder--Schrader reflection positivity, with a positive Kallen--Lehmann spectral function and no additional ghost states. The on-shell residue $Z_2>0$ at one loop confirms unitarity of the fermion sector, and the perturbative spectral function of the dressed propagator is positive. A worldline bound $Q\le0$ gives fixed-order Euclidean UV finiteness. The one-loop vertex correction gives a finite anomalous magnetic moment, a calculable $O(m^2/Λ^2)$ prediction absent in standard QED.

</details>


### [62] [Why Hadamard states?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22767)
*Eleanor March*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper critically examines the Hadamard condition in quantum field theory on curved spacetime, challenges existing justifications, and proposes a novel characterization: the Hadamard condition is necessary and sufficient for defining a well-behaved operator product on a broad class of observables—thereby clarifying its foundational role, connection to renormalization, stress-energy expectation values, and vacuum-like behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The philosophical and foundational basis of the Hadamard condition remains unclear despite its central role in renormalization, stress-energy definition, and Hawking radiation; existing motivations are insufficient or incomplete.

Method: Critical analysis of extant justifications for the Hadamard condition, followed by a constructive argument establishing its equivalence to the existence of a well-defined operator product satisfying key algebraic and physical constraints (a converse to prior results).

Result: A new, rigorous justification: the Hadamard condition is necessary and sufficient for the existence of a well-defined operator product on a sufficiently large observable algebra—resolving ambiguities about its status and implications.

Conclusion: The Hadamard condition should be understood not as an ad hoc physicality criterion but as a fundamental algebraic requirement for consistent local quantum field theory on curved spacetimes, unifying its roles in renormalization, equivalence principle compliance, and vacuum characterization.

Abstract: In quantum field theory on curved spacetime, and in locally-covariant quantum field theory, the Hadamard condition is often presented as a necessary condition on 'physically reasonable' states of the quantum field, and plays a central role in many theoretical and foundational applications - ranging from proofs of the renormalizability of Wick polynomials to derivations of the Hawking temperature. Yet despite this, the philosophical and foundational underpinnings of the Hadamard condition remain murky. I critically discuss existing motivations for the Hadamard condition in the literature, before arguing in favour of an alternative justification for the Hadamard condition, according to which it is best understood as a necessary and sufficient condition for the existence of a well-defined operator product on a sufficiently large space of observables of the quantum field, satisfying a variety of further conditions (thus proving a converse to a result which was already discussed in this context). This clarifies the role and status of the Hadamard condition, including its relationship to the equivalence principle, to well-definedness of physical quantities such as Wick polynomials and the expectation value of the stress-energy operator, and the sense in which Hadamard states are 'vacuum-like'.

</details>


### [63] [Compatible Lie conformal bialgebras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22941)
*Lamei Yuan,Jiansen Zhao*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces compatible Lie conformal bialgebras—conformal analogues of compatible Lie bialgebras—characterizes them via matched pairs, conformal Manin triples, and coboundary structures, and establishes necessary/sufficient conditions on r-matrices involving three conformal Yang–Baxter equations, distinguishing them from the compatible conformal classical Yang–Baxter equation (CYBE).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a conformal analogue of compatible Lie bialgebras—structures encoding two compatible Poisson–Lie group structures—and extend the theory of Lie conformal algebras to include compatibility, duality, and integrable systems-related r-matrix formalism.

Method: Constructs compatible Lie conformal bialgebras as $\mathbb{C}[\partial]$-modules equipped with two compatible Lie conformal brackets and two compatible conformal cobrackets; develops representation theory, matched pairs, conformal duals, and finite-rank equivalence with compatible conformal Manin triples; analyzes coboundary cases via r-matrices and derives three conformal Yang–Baxter conditions plus a separate compatible conformal CYBE.

Result: Proves equivalence among compatible Lie conformal bialgebras, standard compatible conformal Manin triples, and matched pairs for finite-rank free $\mathbb{C}[\partial]$-modules; gives precise r-matrix characterization requiring invariance of symmetric part and three distinct conformal Yang–Baxter equations; shows strict implication: compatible conformal CYBE solutions satisfy the three conditions, but not conversely—verified by explicit counterexamples.

Conclusion: Compatible Lie conformal bialgebras form a robust algebraic framework unifying compatibility, duality, and r-matrix theory in conformal settings; the three conformal Yang–Baxter conditions are necessary but not sufficient for the compatible conformal CYBE, revealing a finer stratification of integrability conditions in conformal representation theory.

Abstract: We introduce and study compatible Lie conformal bialgebras as conformal counterparts of compatible Lie bialgebras. Such a structure consists of two compatible Lie conformal brackets and two compatible conformal cobrackets on the same $\C[\partial]$-module, and every simultaneous linear combination of them is again a Lie conformal bialgebra. We develop representations and matched pairs for compatible Lie conformal algebras, introduce compatible Lie conformal coalgebras, and establish their duality through the conformal dual in the finite case. For $\C[\p]$-modules that are free of finite rank, we prove the equivalence among compatible Lie conformal bialgebras, standard compatible conformal Manin triples and matched pairs. In the coboundary case, we characterize the tensors $r$ that determine compatible Lie conformal bialgebras. The characterization requires the symmetric part of $r$ to be invariant with respect to both brackets and three conformal Yang--Baxter conditions to hold. The first two conditions correspond to the two brackets separately, whereas the third is the compatible conformal Yang--Baxter condition. For comparison, we introduce the compatible conformal classical Yang--Baxter equation (CYBE) and show that each of its solutions satisfies these three conditions, while the converse fails. One explicit example shows that the first two conditions do not imply the third. Another shows that all three conformal Yang-Baxter conditions may hold even though $r$ is not a solution of compatible conformal CYBE.

</details>


### [64] [Statistical Physics of Planar Carroll Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23039)
*Fei Huang,Loïc Marsot*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a well-defined statistical physics framework for planar Carrollian systems—enabled by central extensions of the 2D Carroll algebra and angular momentum—using geometric thermodynamics; it finds that thermal equilibrium requires uniform rotation, derives entropy (logarithmic in area) and pressure (2D ideal gas law), and constructs an effective Hamiltonian from symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the non-convergence of Carrollian statistical physics in general dimensions by exploiting unique features of the planar (2D) Carroll algebra—specifically its central extensions and compatibility with angular momentum—to define a consistent thermodynamic framework.

Method: Applies Souriau's geometric thermodynamics to construct the phase space and partition function for particles on a uniformly rotating disc; uses symmetry analysis of the centrally extended 2D Carroll algebra to derive thermodynamic observables and infer the effective Hamiltonian.

Result: Demonstrates that thermal equilibrium necessitates uniform rotation, with one central charge fixing its direction; obtains entropy scaling as log(area) and pressure obeying the 2D ideal gas law; constructs an explicit effective Hamiltonian consistent with symmetry-derived thermodynamics.

Conclusion: A consistent, symmetry-based statistical physics for planar Carrollian systems exists—unlike higher-dimensional cases—and exhibits distinctive thermodynamic behavior rooted in the algebra’s central extensions and rotational constraints.

Abstract: In this article, we define and study the statistical physics of planar Carrollian systems. While it has been shown recently that, for general dimensions, the Carroll limit of Poincaré statistical physics typically does not converge, we show that thanks to the central extensions of the Carroll algebra in the plane, and by considering systems with angular momentum, there exists a well defined notion of planar Carrollian statistical physics.
  Using Souriau's geometric thermodynamics, we compute the partition function for particles on a uniformly rotating disc, and show that rotation is inevitable for thermal equilibrium of planar Carroll systems, with one of the central charges determining the direction of rotation. We derive thermodynamic quantities in particular entropy, which scales logarithmically with the disc area, and pressure, which follows the two-dimensional ideal gas law. Though all results are obtained from symmetry considerations, we also derive the corresponding effective Hamiltonian.

</details>


### [65] [Jacobi exceptional orthogonal polynomials for extended Scarf I potentials with position-dependent mass](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23264)
*Christiane Quesne*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper solves the Scarf I potential problem with a specific position-dependent mass using point canonical transformation, derives new exactly-solvable rational extensions linked to X_m-Jacobi exceptional orthogonal polynomials, and reveals a deformed shape invariance within a deformed supersymmetric framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend solvability of the Scarf I potential to position-dependent mass systems and uncover new exactly-solvable potentials tied to exceptional orthogonal polynomials and deformed supersymmetry.

Method: Point canonical transformation (PCT) mapping the position-dependent mass Schrödinger equation onto the constant-mass Scarf I problem; inverse PCT to construct rational extensions; analysis of deformed shape invariance in a deformed supersymmetric framework.

Result: Exact solvability of Scarf I potential under m(α;x) = (1 + α sin x)^{-2}; construction of rational extensions associated with X_m-Jacobi EOPs of types I, II, III; demonstration of deformed shape invariance for both original and extended (types I/II) potentials.

Conclusion: The Scarf I potential with this class of position-dependent mass retains exact solvability and admits rational extensions linked to exceptional orthogonal polynomials, while exhibiting deformed shape invariance—generalizing standard supersymmetric quantum mechanics.

Abstract: We show that the Scarf I potential problem in a position-dependent mass background of the type $m(α;x) = (1 + α\sin x)^{-2}$, $0<α<1$, can be solved by using a point canonical transformation mapping the corresponding Schr\" odinger equation onto that of the Scarf I potential with constant mass. The inverse point canonical transformation then provides some exactly-solvable rational extensions of the Scarf I potential with positive-dependent mass associated with $X_m$-Jacobi exceptional orthogonal polynomials of type I, II, or III. The Scarf I potential problem with position-dependent mass is shown to exhibit a deformed shape invariance property in a deformed supersymmetric framework. Such a property is also valid for extended potentials of type I and II. The results are illustrated with a simple example.

</details>


### [66] [Cantor Spectrum via a Reducibility-Duality Bridge for the Mosaic Almost Mathieu Operator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23422)
*Jiawei He,Yuan Shan,Yongjian Wang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves the Cantor nature of the spectrum for the mosaic Almost Mathieu operator at all noncritical parameters, using duality, reducibility, and Moser–Pöschel techniques.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand spectral properties—particularly the Cantor structure—of the mosaic Almost Mathieu operator, a quasiperiodic model admitting a singular strip-Jacobi representation.

Method: Establishes a duality framework; extends the IDS–fibered rotation number correspondence; reduces the operator to SL(2,R) cocycles; applies Aubry duality, reducibility theory, and the Moser–Pöschel argument.

Result: The spectrum is proven to be a Cantor set for all noncritical parameters.

Conclusion: The mosaic Almost Mathieu operator exhibits Cantor spectrum universally away from criticality, confirming structural rigidity analogous to the classical Almost Mathieu case but in a generalized strip-Jacobi setting.

Abstract: We study the mosaic Almost Mathieu operator, a quasiperiodic model that naturally admits a singular strip-Jacobi representation. By establishing a duality framework and extending the correspondence between the integrated density of states and the fibered rotation number to this setting, we obtain an effective reduction to $SL(2,\mathbb{R})$ cocycles. As a consequence, combining Aubry duality, reducibility theory, and the Moser--Pöschel argument, we prove that the spectrum is a Cantor set for all noncritical parameters.

</details>


### [67] [Which Waveguide Network Realizes a Prescribed Transmission Profile? An Exact Forward Construction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23645)
*Tristan M. Lawrie*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Introduces an analytically invertible framework for designing periodic waveguide networks that directly maps target transmission coefficients to physical parameters (bond connections, refractive indices, lengths, gauge phases) via Fourier expansion of lattice reactance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of conventional inverse-design methods by enabling direct, closed-form synthesis of physically realizable waveguide structures from prescribed wave transmission profiles.

Method: Leverages exact transmission coefficients of periodic waveguide networks governed by a gauge-shifted Helmholtz operator; expresses lattice reactance as a Fourier series whose coefficients are analytically linked to graph architecture and physical parameters.

Result: Enables exact, analytical reconstruction of 1D angular filters and 2D image-intensity patterns through spectral control of scattering response.

Conclusion: Provides a rigorous, invertible design paradigm bridging desired wave transmission behavior to implementable photonic structures without iterative optimization.

Abstract: We introduce an analytically invertible framework for wavefront construction based on the scattering properties of periodic waveguide networks governed by a gauge-shifted Helmholtz operator. By determining the exact transmission coefficients of the network, we express the lattice reactance as a Fourier expansion whose coefficients are analytically mapped onto the underlying graph architecture, allowing the required bond connections, refractive indices, lengths, and gauge phases to be determined directly from a prescribed target transmission coefficient. In contrast to conventional inverse-design approaches, the present formulation provides a closed-form route from desired wave transmission profiles to physically realisable structures. The framework extends naturally from one-dimensional angular filtering to two-dimensional image synthesis, where arbitrary transmitted intensity patterns are reconstructed through exact spectral control of the network scattering response.

</details>


### [68] [Position-Space Renormalization and Half-Space Truncations in $φ^4_4$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23650)
*Majdouline Borji*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves that half-space truncations of renormalized correlators in massive Euclidean φ⁴₄ theory require no additional renormalization, establishing uniform convergence in Besov spaces via a position-space Wilson–Polchinski flow analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously justify the use of half-space observables in massive Euclidean φ⁴₄ theory without introducing new renormalization counterterms, addressing analytic challenges posed by boundary singularities and ultraviolet regularization.

Method: Develops a position-space renormalization framework based on the Wilson–Polchinski flow equation, employing a hierarchy of power-counting spaces tailored to the singularity structure of correlators; derives uniform power-counting bounds and Besov–Hölder regularity estimates.

Result: Half-space truncated renormalized correlators converge distributionally and uniformly in the ultraviolet cutoff; the limits lie in explicitly identified Besov spaces; no extra renormalization is needed for such truncations.

Conclusion: The renormalized correlators in massive Euclidean φ⁴₄ theory are robust under half-space truncation—analytically well-behaved and convergent in appropriate Besov topologies—validating a class of semi-infinite observables within rigorous QFT frameworks.

Abstract: In this paper, we study half-space observables in the massive Euclidean $φ^4_4$ theory. We prove that the renormalized correlators can be multiplied by half-space truncations without requiring any additional renormalization. More precisely, products with smooth approximations of the half-space indicator converge to well-defined distributional limits, uniformly in the ultraviolet cutoff. The proof relies on a position-space renormalization framework for the Wilson--Polchinski flow equation based on a hierarchy of power-counting spaces adapted to the singularity structure of the correlators. This yields uniform power-counting bounds and Besov--Hölder regularity estimates for the renormalized correlators. As a consequence, the correlators converge as the ultraviolet cutoff tends to infinity, and the convergence takes place in explicitly identified Besov spaces.

</details>

### [69] [Complexity Inequalities for Quantum Subsystems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20790)
*Pawel Caputa,Giuseppe Di Giulio,Tran Quang Loc*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper introduces 'tripartite complexity' and 'complexity gap' for three disjoint subsystems, analyzes their sign structure across holographic (CV), Gaussian state (Fisher-Rao), and Krylov-inspired frameworks, and finds the former is not sign-definite while the latter consistently exhibits a definite sign—suggesting it as a candidate for subsystem complexity inequalities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To constrain entanglement structure of states with classical gravitational duals via holographic entropy cone analogues, by exploring how subsystem complexities combine in multipartite quantum systems—specifically for three disjoint regions.

Method: Defined two quantities—tripartite complexity (analogous to tripartite information) and complexity gap (quantifying emergent complexity beyond constituents)—and evaluated their sign behavior across three subsystem complexity frameworks: (1) complexity=volume in AdS holography, (2) Fisher-Rao complexity for Gaussian many-body states, and (3) a novel Krylov-space inspired framework for reduced density matrices, validated on few-qubit systems and coherent-state dynamics.

Result: Tripartite complexity is not sign-definite in any framework; complexity gap is sign-definite in all tested cases, though its sign (positive/negative) depends on the chosen complexity definition.

Conclusion: The complexity gap is a robust, universally signed quantity across disparate complexity measures, making it a promising foundational element for a hierarchy of subsystem complexity inequalities—paralleling the role of mutual information in entropy inequalities.

Abstract: Motivated by the role of the holographic entropy cone in constraining the entanglement structure of states with classical gravitational duals, we investigate combinations of subsystem complexities associated with reduced density matrices in multipartite quantum systems. Focusing on subsystems composed of three disjoint regions, we introduce two quantities: a tripartite complexity, inspired by the tripartite information, and a complexity gap, designed to characterize emergent complexity in the full quantum state beyond that of its constituents. We study the sign structure of these quantities in three selected approaches to subsystem complexity. In holography, we employ the complexity=volume proposal in AdS spacetimes; for Gaussian many-body states, we use Fisher-Rao subsystem complexity; and we further develop a Krylov-space inspired, effective framework for reduced density matrices, which we test in few-qubit systems and coherent-state dynamics. Across all three approaches, we find that the tripartite complexity is not sign-definite in general. By contrast, the complexity gap exhibits a definite sign in every example we analyze, although the sign itself depends on the underlying notion of subsystem complexity. Our results suggest that the complexity gap could be a natural candidate building block for a prospective hierarchy of subsystem complexity inequalities.

</details>


### [70] [$N=1$ Supersymmetry, Weil-Petersson Volume Recursion, and a Spectral Curve](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20796)
*Clifford V. Johnson*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves that the Stanford-Witten-Norbury volume recursion for Weil-Petersson volumes of $N=1$ supersymmetric Riemann surfaces with Neveu-Schwarz boundaries and Ramond punctures follows directly from a recently derived spectral curve via topological recursion; it further shows an alternative recursion arises depending on whether Ramond data is encoded in initial conditions or recursion kernels, enabling geometric interpretation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous link between the Stanford-Witten-Norbury volume recursion and the spectral curve formalism, and to clarify the role of Ramond punctures in the recursion structure—particularly whether they belong in initial data or kernel definitions—for deeper geometric insight.

Method: Analytic derivation using topological recursion applied to the known spectral curve for $N=1$ super-Teichmüller theory; comparison and equivalence proof between recursive structures via Laplace-transformed correlators $W^{(2m)}_{g,n}({\hat z}_i)$ and original volume functions $V^{(2m)}_{g,n}(\{b_i\})$.

Result: The Stanford-Witten-Norbury recursion is shown to be a direct consequence of the spectral curve and topological recursion; an alternative, geometrically motivated recursion is derived where Ramond puncture data resides in the recursion kernels rather than initial conditions.

Conclusion: The spectral curve fully encodes the supersymmetric Weil-Petersson volume structure; the choice of embedding Ramond data determines the recursion's geometric interpretability—kernel-based encoding yields a more natural geometric understanding of Ramond punctures in moduli space.

Abstract: The Stanford-Witten-Norbury generalization of Mirzakhani's volume recursion computes $V^{(2m)}_{g,n}(\{b_i\})$, the Weil-Petersson volumes of the moduli space of $N=1$ supersymmetric Riemann surfaces of genus $g$ with $n$ Neveu-Schwarz boundaries of geodesic lengths $b_i$ ($i{=}1,\ldots,n$), and $2m$ Ramond punctures. Recently, a spectral curve has been derived that allows their Laplace transforms $W^{(2m)}_{g,n}(\{{\hat z}_i\})$ to be computed using topological recursion. We prove that the Stanford-Witten-Norbury volume recursion is directly derivable from the spectral curve. An alternative volume recursion can also be derived from it. The difference comes from whether the Ramond information is in the initial data, or in the volume recursion's kernels. The latter invites a geometrical understanding.

</details>


### [71] [Renormalization effects fade away during inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20915)
*Christian Durán Romero,Luis J. Garay,Mercedes Martín-Benito,Rita B. Neves*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Inflation dynamically suppresses renormalization effects, making the primordial power spectrum robust against UV ambiguities at observable scales.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether ultraviolet (UV) renormalization effects during inflation significantly alter cosmological predictions for the primordial power spectrum.

Method: Analyzing the time evolution of renormalization contributions relative to super-Hubble mode freezing during inflation, using dynamical suppression arguments within quantum field theory in curved spacetime.

Result: Renormalization contributions decay rapidly during inflation while super-Hubble perturbations freeze, leading to negligible impact on the observable primordial spectrum.

Conclusion: Standard inflationary predictions for the primordial power spectrum are robust under renormalization at observable scales, resolving concerns about UV sensitivity.

Abstract: The renormalization of the primordial inflationary power spectrum has long raised the possibility that ultraviolet effects could significantly alter predictions for cosmological observables. We demonstrate that inflation dynamically suppresses the entire renormalization sector: while super-Hubble perturbations freeze after horizon crossing, renormalization contributions decay rapidly during inflation. As a consequence, the observable primordial spectrum is remarkably insensitive to renormalization ambiguities, providing strong evidence for the robustness under renormalization of standard inflationary predictions at observable scales.

</details>


### [72] [Dark bubbles, dark dimensions and fat gravitons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20942)
*Ulf Danielsson,Suvendu Giri*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The dark bubble model uses de Sitter swampland instabilities to explain cosmic acceleration, predicting a micron-scale dark dimension, gravity weakening at micron scales, ~10–100 TeV string scale, and positive spatial curvature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile a positive cosmological constant with the de Sitter swampland conjectures by leveraging their underlying instabilities, while unifying cosmological, gravitational, and string scales.

Method: Constructs a 'dark bubble' solution in higher-dimensional gravity/string theory where de Sitter instability drives inevitable late-time acceleration; derives dimensional reduction and effective 4D phenomenology including modified gravity and curvature.

Result: Predicts (i) a micron-sized extra dimension ('dark dimension'), (ii) gravity fading below ~micron distances (fat graviton behavior), (iii) string scale ~O(10–100) TeV, and (iv) measurable positive spatial curvature (Ω_k < 0).

Conclusion: The dark bubble model provides a consistent, predictive framework linking quantum gravity constraints (swampland), cosmic acceleration, and tabletop gravity experiments—offering testable alternatives to standard ΛCDM and large-extra-dimension scenarios.

Abstract: The dark bubble model explains the existence of a positive cosmological constant by making explicit use of the instabilities underlying the de Sitter swampland conjectures to make the accelerated expansion of the universe inevitable. A distinctive consequence of the construction is a unique hierarchy connecting cosmological, gravitational, string, and higher-dimensional scales. In particular, the model naturally predicts the existence of a dark dimension of micron size, an idea that has been argued for on independent grounds in the literature. The same framework also predicts a weakening of gravity at distances of order the dark-dimension scale, leading to a fading of the gravitational force at micron distances. We argue that the dark bubble therefore provides a concrete realization of both the dark dimension proposal and Sundrum's fat graviton scenario, in which gravity effectively ceases to probe shorter distances. Additional predictions include a string scale of order tens of TeV and a measurable positive spatial curvature of the universe. We review these key aspects of the model, discuss their implications for gravity and cosmology, and highlight its key predictions.

</details>


### [73] [Linear Growth of Holographic Time-like Entanglement Entropy and Kasner exponents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21079)
*Zi-Hao Li,Run-Qiu Yang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the late-time growth of time-like entanglement entropy (TEE) in asymptotically AdS black holes using a piece-wise extremal surface prescription; it analytically identifies a critical interior surface governing linear TEE growth and establishes universal bounds on its real and imaginary parts via energy conditions and numerical evidence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how time-like entanglement entropy evolves at late times in black hole interiors—particularly whether its growth is universally constrained by near-singularity geometry and energy conditions.

Method: Adopts a geometric prescription based on piece-wise extremal surfaces; assumes Kasner geometry near space-like singularity; applies null and dominant energy conditions; combines analytical extremal surface analysis inside the event horizon with numerical computations in Einstein-scalar theory.

Result: A critical extremal surface $\mathcal{A}_c$ inside the horizon governs late-time linear TEE growth; vacuum Schwarzschild-AdS sets upper bound on Re(TEE growth rate) and lower bound on Im(TEE growth rate); bounds are proven for static planar symmetric cases under dominant energy condition.

Conclusion: Late-time TEE growth is geometrically rigid—dictated by interior critical surfaces and bounded universally by vacuum AdS black hole behavior, suggesting deep connections between singularity structure, energy conditions, and holographic entanglement dynamics.

Abstract: The holographic time-like entanglement entropy (TEE) extends entanglement to time-like boundary subregions. While its definitive holographic dictionary remains debated, one concrete proposal utilizes piece-wise extremal surfaces. In this work, we adopt this geometric prescription as an exploratory framework to holographically investigate the late-time ($τ_0\to \infty$) growth of TEE in asymptotically AdS black holes with a space-like singularity and no inner horizon. By assuming a Kasner geometry near the space-like singularity and using null energy condition, we analytically show that a critical extremal surface $\mathcal{A}_c$ inside the event horizon completely governs the late-time linear growth of the TEE. This result suggests that the late-time behavior of TEE is tightly constrained by the geometry of black hole interiors. Using numerical results from Einstein-scalar theory, we find a robust behavior: the vacuum Schwarzschild-AdS geometry sets an upper bound on the growth rate of the real part and a lower bound on the imaginary part. We prove these bounds in static planar symmetric case under dominant energy condition and conjecture that it should be true in more general cases.

</details>


### [74] [Metamorphosis of fractional instantons on a twisted $T^4$ with a double-trace deformation: a numerical study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22078)
*Benjamin Dobozy,Erich Poppitz*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper numerically minimizes the lattice action of trace-deformed Yang-Mills theory on T^4 with twisted boundary conditions to study classical minimum-action configurations of fractional topological charge across varying torus geometries (R^{4−k} × T^k), revealing morphological transitions among monopole-instantons (k=1), center vortices (k=2), and fractional instantons (k=3,4), and analyzing the role of deformation potential in continuity and level crossing of these transitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how classical saddle-point configurations of fractional topological charge—such as monopole-instantons, center vortices, and fractional instantons—interrelate and transform across different spacetime geometries, and to assess the robustness of analytic deformation-based pictures (e.g., monopole chains forming vortex sheets) in pure Yang-Mills theory.

Method: Numerical minimization of the lattice action of trace-deformed Yang-Mills theory on a 4-torus T^4 with twisted boundary conditions; systematic variation of twist angles and aspect ratios to interpolate between R^{4−k} × T^k geometries; analysis of minimum-action configurations and their topological properties, including dependence on deformation potential.

Result: Demonstrates continuous morphing of fractional-charge saddle points across k = 1–4 geometries; shows that the analytic 'monopole-chain-to-vortex-sheet' picture persists in pure Yang-Mills when twist-induced abelianization aligns with deformation-induced one; identifies deformation-potential-dependent discontinuous transitions with level crossing between minimal-action configurations.

Conclusion: Fractional topological objects are geometrically connected across dimensional reductions, and their interplay is governed by both boundary conditions (twists) and deformation potentials; the deformation, while technical, can be physically emulated by geometry in pure gauge theory, suggesting deeper universality in nonperturbative structure.

Abstract: We use numerical minimization of the lattice action of trace-deformed Yang-Mills theory on $T^4$ with twisted boundary conditions to find the classical minimum action configurations of fractional topological charge. We vary the twists and ratios of torus periods to interpolate between different $R^{4-k} \times T^k$ geometries. This allows us to see how the corresponding minimum action saddle point configurations -- monopole-instantons ($k=1$), center vortices ($k=2$), and fractional instantons ($k=3,4$) -- morph into each other. We also study how the transition between them depends on the presence of a deformation potential. In particular, we argue that the recent analytic picture of chains of monopole-instantons collimating their flux into center-vortex sheets, while technically relying on the deformation potential, also holds in pure Yang-Mills theory, for tori whose shape causes the abelianization due to the deformation to align with the one due to the twists. Our results also indicate that with nonzero deformation potential, some transitions between different minimal-action fractional charge configurations may be discontinuous and involve level crossing.

</details>


### [75] [The Entanglement Wedge Polygon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21081)
*Kosei Fujiki,Jonathan Harper,Tadashi Takayanagi,Nicolò Zenoni*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Introduces the entanglement wedge polygon (EWP), a codimension-1 region in holographic spacetimes defined via intersections of entanglement wedges; shows its topological nature in AdS₃ via Gauss-Bonnet, extends analysis to higher dimensions and various bulk geometries, and generalizes to mixed states with implications for multipartite entanglement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To define and characterize a geometric object—the entanglement wedge polygon—that captures collective entanglement structure across multiple boundary regions in holography, and to explore its topological and physical significance across diverse bulk geometries and quantum states.

Method: Uses holographic entanglement entropy prescriptions (RT/HRT surfaces), homology constraints, and differential geometry (e.g., Gauss-Bonnet theorem); analyzes explicit examples including vacuum AdS_{d+1}, black branes, solitons, and end-of-the-world brane setups; extends definition to mixed states via purification and wedge reconstruction.

Result: EWP is topologically invariant in AdS₃ due to Gauss-Bonnet; in higher dimensions, it remains well-defined and geometrically meaningful across varied bulk solutions; admits natural extension to mixed states; suggests potential utility as a geometric proxy for multipartite entanglement measures.

Conclusion: The entanglement wedge polygon provides a robust, geometrically grounded framework for studying multi-region entanglement structure in holography, unifying topological, geometric, and quantum information perspectives across pure and mixed states and diverse gravitational backgrounds.

Abstract: In this work we consider a particular codimension-1 region of a holographic spacetime which we call the entanglement wedge polygon (EWP). For a pure state and a partition of the boundary into a number of regions $A_i$ the EWP is defined as the region external to all the individual homology regions $r_{A_i}$ which consists of the intersection of the entanglement wedge EW($A_i$) with the time slice. In vacuum AdS$_3$ the quantity is topological as a direct consequence of the Gauss-Bonnet theorem. In higher dimensions we make progress by considering a number of concrete examples including vacuum, black brane, and soliton solutions of AdS$_{d+1}$ as well as spacetime geometries with end of the world branes dual to boundary conformal field theories. We provide a suitable generalization to mixed states and comment on possible connections between the EWP and measures of multi-partite entanglement.

</details>


### [76] [Phase diagram of the massless Gross-Neveu model with two components](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21246)
*Michael Thies*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the semiclassical analysis of the two-component Gross-Neveu model at finite density to finite temperature, constructing the full large-N phase diagram—including inhomogeneous phases—using stability analyses instead of solving the thermal Hartree-Fock problem, and identifies a critical filling fraction inducing qualitative change in the phase structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize recent exact results for the Gross-Neveu model at finite density to finite temperature and explore how restricting the chemical potential to a subset of fermion flavors affects thermodynamic phase structure.

Method: Semiclassical (large N) analysis combined with stability analyses—including modulational, translational, and chiral instabilities—to map the full phase diagram without solving the thermal Hartree-Fock equations.

Result: Complete large-N phase diagram incorporating homogeneous and inhomogeneous phases at finite temperature; identification of a critical filling fraction where the topology of the phase diagram changes qualitatively.

Conclusion: The phase structure of the two-component Gross-Neveu model is highly sensitive to the filling fraction, and finite-temperature effects induce new inhomogeneous phases whose onset is governed by stability thresholds rather than explicit Hartree-Fock solutions.

Abstract: Recently, Benini, Mamroud, Reis and Serone have presented exact results for the Gross-Neveu model at finite density, both at finite N and in the large N limit. Generalizing previous studies, they introduce a chemical potential acting only on a subset of fermion flavors - the two-component Gross-Neveu model. Here, we take up this idea and extend the semiclassical study to finite temperature. The full (large N) phase diagram including inhomogeneous phases is constructed without solving the thermal Hartree-Fock problem. All phase boundaries can be found on the basis of various kinds of stability analyses. As a result, we find a qualitative change of the phase diagram at some critical filling fraction.

</details>


### [77] [Non-relativistic limits of $\mathcal N=4$ supersymmetric Yang-Mills theory and S-duality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21494)
*Hyungrok Kim,Joseph Smith*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores non-relativistic limits of 4d maximally supersymmetric Yang-Mills (MSYM) via a Type IIB brane construction involving D3-branes and (p,q)-strings, yielding topological deformations of supersymmetric Galilean Yang-Mills or monopole moduli space quantum mechanics; these theories form a 3D moduli space with enriched PSL(2,Z) duality structure beyond the relativistic S-duality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the nonperturbative SL(2;Z) S-duality of relativistic 4d MSYM manifests in non-relativistic limits, and to classify and characterize the resulting Galilean-invariant quantum field theories and their dualities.

Method: Constructs a family of non-relativistic limits using a Type IIB brane configuration (D3-brane + (p,q)-strings); identifies resulting theories as topological deformations of supersymmetric Galilean Yang-Mills or quantum mechanics on BPS monopole moduli space; analyzes duality actions on the parameter space; verifies Abelian duality via path integral and supports non-Abelian duality via one-particle spectrum matching and symmetry comparison (Galilean spacetime and electric/magnetic 1-form symmetries).

Result: A three-dimensional moduli space of non-relativistic theories is identified, each corresponding to a distinct (p,q) limit; these include Galilean Yang-Mills with θ-term and monopole-charge coupling, or moduli-space quantum mechanics with only θ-term; PSL(2,Z) acts nontrivially on this space, exhibiting richer duality structure than in relativistic MSYM; duality is rigorously established in Abelian case and strongly supported in non-Abelian case.

Conclusion: Non-relativistic limits of 4d MSYM realize a geometrically structured duality web—encoded in a topologically nontrivial 3D moduli space—where SL(2,Z) S-duality lifts to a more intricate PSL(2,Z) action, revealing deeper connections between Galilean symmetry, topological terms, and generalized symmetries in quantum field theory.

Abstract: We investigate non-relativistic limits of four-dimensional maximally supersymmetric Yang-Mills theory (4d MSYM) and their relation to the nonperturbative $\operatorname{SL}(2;\mathbb Z)$ S-duality of the relativistic theory. We construct a general family of non-relativistic limits using a Type IIB brane set-up with a D3-brane and $(p,q)$-strings and show that the resulting theories are topological deformations of supersymmetric Galilean Yang-Mills theory or quantum mechanics on the moduli space of BPS monopoles. The deformations of the Galilean Yang-Mills theory are the familiar $θ$-term and a coupling to the monopole charge, while in the moduli space theory the only deformation is a $θ$-term. This family of theories fit together into a three-dimensional moduli space with nontrivial topology, on which $\operatorname{PSL}(2;\mathbb Z)$-valued dualities act in a richer and more complex way than in the relativistic parent theory. In the Abelian case, we establish the duality directly using the path integral, while in the non-Abelian case we support our claim by matching the one-particle spectrum as well as the Galilean spacetime symmetries and electric/magnetic invertible one-form symmetries.

</details>


### [78] [Asymptotically safe quantum gravity and its phenomenology -- a review](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21522)
*Astrid Eichhorn*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This review introduces asymptotically safe quantum gravity—a quantum field theory approach to gravity based on ultraviolet quantum scale symmetry (the Reuter fixed point)—and surveys recent progress: robust evidence in 4D Euclidean pure gravity, inclusion of Standard Model and BSM matter, incorporation of Lorentzian signature, and resulting predictivity across scales (particle physics, black holes, cosmology), with discussion of connections to other quantum gravity frameworks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish quantum gravity as a predictive, renormalizable quantum field theory via asymptotic safety—leveraging quantum scale symmetry (a UV fixed point) rather than requiring new physics beyond QFT.

Method: Non-perturbative functional renormalization group (FRG) analysis of gravitational and matter field effective average actions; systematic truncations and global stability studies of the Reuter fixed point in Euclidean and increasingly Lorentzian settings.

Result: Robust evidence for the Reuter fixed point in 4D Euclidean pure gravity; consistent extension to full Standard Model + BSM matter; recent progress incorporating Lorentzian signature; high predictivity across energy scales due to finite-dimensional UV critical surface.

Conclusion: Asymptotic safety is a viable, increasingly realistic, and observationally testable framework for quantum gravity within QFT; its connections to other approaches may reveal universal features of quantum spacetime.

Abstract: Asymptotically safe quantum gravity is an approach to quantum gravity. It is based on the premise that quantum field theory can describe the quantum nature of gravity in our universe. At its core lies quantum scale symmetry. This review provides an introduction to the key ideas of the approach and surveys the current status of the field. Over the last years, the field has taken large strides towards an increasingly realistic setting: First, compelling evidence for quantum scale symmetry exists in four-dimensional, Euclidean, pure gravity, establishing the Reuter fixed point robustly. Second, matter fields, including the Standard Model as well as beyond-Standard-Model-candidates, have been studied in depth, with increasingly conclusive evidence for quantum scale symmetry. Most recently, the final gap to a realistic description of quantum gravity is being closed, because Lorentzian spacetime signature can now be accounted for. As a consequence of quantum scale symmetry in the ultraviolet, the approach is highly predictive at all scales. This review discusses the physics of asymptotic safety across all scales. Predictive power for particle physics, black holes and cosmology provides a clear pathway to confronting quantum gravity with current and near-future observations. The review closes by discussing the connection to other approaches to quantum gravity. It advocates the perspective that such connections between approaches may lead us to an understanding of universal physical features of quantum gravity.

</details>


### [79] [Near-horizon modifications in finite $N$ holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21537)
*Rishkrith Bairy,Mrityunjay Nath,Debajyoti Sarkar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates non-perturbative violations of bulk micro-causality in AdS/CFT at finite N, using late-time boundary cutoffs and HKLL smearing; it confirms consistent non-locality estimates in near-horizon modified AdS₂ and BTZ black hole geometries, where a throat parameter controls the scale of non-locality; in 3D, spectral form factors of probe dynamics—averaged over the throat parameter—exhibit dip-ramp-plateau behavior, mirroring results from stretched-horizon or brick-wall models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how finite-N effects in AdS/CFT induce non-perturbative breakdowns of bulk locality and micro-causality, beyond standard large-N semiclassical expectations.

Method: Explicit bulk reconstruction via HKLL smearing with late boundary time cutoff; analysis of near-horizon modified AdS₂ and BTZ black hole backgrounds; spectral form factor computation for probe fields averaged over the throat parameter.

Result: Consistent non-locality scales emerge across different black hole mimicker geometries, controlled by the throat parameter; in 3D, the averaged spectral form factor displays dip-ramp-plateau structure—signature of chaotic spectral statistics—matching findings in stretched-horizon/brick-wall models.

Conclusion: Finite-N AdS/CFT implies intrinsic bulk non-locality tied to near-horizon geometry; the throat parameter acts as an effective UV cutoff governing causality violation scale; spectral universality (dip-ramp-plateau) suggests robust connection between horizon microstructure and quantum chaos in low-dimensional gravity.

Abstract: If one extends the AdS/CFT extrapolate dictionary to large but finite $N$, we are expected to obtain non-perturbative violations of bulk micro-causality. Previously this was achieved by implementing a late boundary time cut-off, while smearing the boundary operator via the HKLL kernel. By performing explicit bulk reconstructions in the backgrounds of near-horizon modified AdS$_2$ and BTZ black holes, we recover the same non-locality estimates as above. For these black hole mimickers, the near-horizon modification is controlled by a throat parameter which sets the scale of this non-locality. In three bulk dimensions, probe dynamics also exhibits a dip-ramp-plateau structure in their spectral form factor when averaged over the throat parameter. Such structure has also been found recently in the background with a stretched horizon or a brick wall.

</details>


### [80] [Asymptotic limits of constrained instantons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21561)
*Benjamin Elder,Kinga Gawrych,Arttu Rajantie*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper revisits false vacuum decay using a toy scalar field model with an unstable quartic potential, employing constrained instantons; it develops three asymptotic regimes—small constrained solutions (perturbative Affleck-type), large constrained solutions (thin-wall adaptation), and large Lagrange multiplier (dimensional analysis + single numerical solution)—and clarifies validity conditions and previously omitted terms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically revisit false vacuum decay via constrained instantons in a simple scalar field model, address gaps in prior perturbative treatments (e.g., neglected terms in Affleck’s method), and extend the thin-wall approximation by identifying robust asymptotic limits—including one where the thin-wall limit fails but a large-Lagrange-multiplier limit remains viable.

Method: Analytic asymptotic analysis of constrained instanton equations in three distinct parameter regimes: (1) small-field limit (perturbative expansion à la Affleck), (2) large-field/thin-wall limit (Coleman-style adaptation), and (3) large-Lagrange-multiplier limit (scaling via dimensional analysis + exact solution from one numerical integration).

Result: Derivation of corrected perturbative expressions including previously omitted terms; identification of necessary conditions for existence of the thin-wall limit; discovery and full analytic characterization of a new large-multiplier regime yielding scalable, numerically efficient solutions.

Conclusion: Constrained instantons for false vacuum decay admit multiple complementary asymptotic descriptions; the large-Lagrange-multiplier limit provides a robust, broadly applicable alternative when thin-wall assumptions break down, enhancing both theoretical understanding and computational tractability.

Abstract: We revisit the topic of false vacuum decay in field theory. We focus on a toy model of a real massive scalar field with an unstable quartic potential. This model has a false vacuum, and decay out of the false vacuum can be described via the method of constrained instantons, which work by introducing a constraint on the path integral. We identify and develop three different asymptotic limits which enable analytic construction of approximate {constrained} solutions. The first, in which the constrained solution is small compared to the inverse mass of the scalar field, is an application of the perturbative methods of Affleck, although we re-derive the main results and identify several terms which were previously neglected. Second, for very large constrained solutions we adapt the thin-wall approximation of Coleman. However, we find that the large instanton limit does not always exist. In this case we identify another useful limit, in which the Lagrange multiplier used to implement the constraint is large. In this limit, the solution's scaling with the parameters may be found via dimensional analysis and an exact solution is obtained with a single numerical computation.

</details>


### [81] [Quantum Gravity Cutoff from Axions: A Type IIB Landscape Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21650)
*Matthew Reece,Tom Rudelius,Christopher Tudball*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper quantitatively verifies a quantum gravity cutoff bound Λ_QG ≲ 2π√S f for extra-dimensional axions in Type IIB Calabi-Yau compactifications, confirming its validity across Kähler moduli space—including boundary regions where standard axion string scaling breaks down—thereby reinforcing its status as a robust feature of quantum gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the universality of the quantum gravity cutoff bound Λ_QG ≲ 2π√S f for extra-dimensional axions, motivated by naturalness and unitarity arguments, particularly in well-controlled string theory settings.
