Method: Analytical derivation and numerical evaluation of the bound in Type IIB Calabi-Yau compactifications, incorporating both C₂ and C₄ axions and probing boundary regions of the Kähler moduli space where co-scaling of axion strings fails.

Result: The bound Λ_QG ≲ 2π√S f holds quantitatively even near singular boundaries of the Kähler moduli space, including regimes where conventional axion string scaling assumptions break down.

Conclusion: The bound is robust and not an artifact of specific approximations; it persists in realistic weakly-coupled string constructions, supporting its interpretation as a fundamental constraint from quantum gravity.

Abstract: Extra-dimensional axions have coupling strength related to fundamental, ultraviolet physics. It has been proposed that the properties of such axions imply a bound on the quantum gravity cutoff: $Λ_\mathrm{QG} \lesssim 2π\sqrt{S} f$, where $f$ is the axion decay constant and $S$ is the instanton action. In the context of weakly-coupled string theory, we identify $Λ_\mathrm{QG}$ with the string scale $M_s$. In this paper, we carry out a quantitative study of this bound on the string scale in the context of Calabi-Yau compactifications of Type IIB string theory, considering both $C_2$ and $C_4$ axions. We show, both analytically and numerically, that the bound holds even near boundaries of the Kähler moduli space, including those where the co-scaling relationship for axion strings fails. This evidence bolsters previous arguments, based on naturalness and on unitarity, that the bound is a general feature of extra-dimensional axions in quantum gravity.

</details>


### [82] [On the Universality of Probe Complexity in $\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21662)
*Eric L. Graef,Jeff Murugan,Horatiu Nastase,Hendrik J. R. Van Zyl*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies Krylov complexity of single-trace operators dual to open strings on giant gravitons in planar N=4 SYM, showing bounded Lanczos coefficients in protected/few-body sectors—hence insufficient for testing gravitational universality—and proposes a finite-density scaling regime as a precise boundary-theoretic test of universality based on coarse thermodynamic data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether Krylov complexity growth in planar N=4 SYM can serve as a boundary probe of gravitational universality conjectures, particularly whether operator complexity growth is universal and governed only by coarse thermodynamic data rather than microscopic details.

Method: Analyzes Krylov complexity using orthogonal polynomial theory linked to the spectral measure of the Hamiltonian; computes Lanczos coefficients {a_n, b_n} in protected and few-body sectors (fixed magnon number M, large open-string length L); introduces a finite-density scaling limit where magnon density ρ = M/L is held fixed to access thermodynamic behavior.

Result: In the few-body limit (M fixed, L→∞), Lanczos coefficients saturate: a_n = 2Mg, b_n → Mg — indicating integrable, band-limited Krylov dynamics incompatible with chaotic/gravitational universality; thus, such sectors cannot test gravity-side universality. A finite-density scaling regime is formulated to enable a meaningful universality test.

Conclusion: Protected and few-body sectors are inadequate for probing gravitational universality via Krylov complexity; a finite-density thermodynamic limit is required, and universality should manifest as Krylov growth depending only on coarse data (ρ, ε), offering a rigorous CFT framework to test holographic complexity conjectures.

Abstract: We investigate Krylov complexity for single-trace operators dual to open strings attached to giant gravitons in planar $\mathcal{N}=4$ super Yang-Mills theory. We show that in protected and few-body sectors, Krylov dynamics is governed by orthogonal polynomial theory associated to the seed spectral measure, leading to bounded Lanczos coefficients determined solely by spectral support. In particular, for fixed magnon number $M$ and open-string length $L\rightarrow\infty$, we derive $a_n=2Mg$ and $b_n\rightarrow Mg$, demonstrating integrable, band-limited dynamics. This establishes that such sectors are insufficient to test recently proposed gravity-side universality of operator complexity growth. We therefore formulate a finite-density program in which magnons scale with system size, and propose a concrete universality test: whether the leading Krylov growth depends only on coarse thermodynamic data $(ρ,\varepsilon)$ and not on microscopic probe structure. This provides a precise boundary-field-theory framework for testing gravitational universality conjectures.

</details>


### [83] [Holonomies and Boundary Symmetries in the Discrete Warped Chern-Simons Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22028)
*H. T. Özer,Aytül Filiz*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a discrete warped Chern-Simons framework for 3D warped gravity using boundary holonomies and monodromy sectors, deriving entropy purely from discrete SL(2,R) × U(1) monodromy data and recovering continuum warped black hole thermodynamics and WCFT structure in the large-lattice limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formulate a discrete, gauge-invariant description of warped 3D gravity that bypasses reliance on smooth geometries and instead builds thermodynamics directly from boundary holonomy data—motivated by holography with warped CFTs and the need for non-perturbative, background-independent formulations.

Method: Constructs a discrete boundary theory based on ordered products of link holonomies over noncontractible cycles, classifies physical sectors via conjugacy classes (hyperbolic/elliptic/parabolic) of SL(2,R) monodromy augmented by U(1) holonomy; derives entropy from monodromy invariants; takes large-lattice limit to recover continuum Wilson loop conditions.

Result: A discrete entropy formula fully determined by boundary monodromy data; exact reproduction of warped black hole entropy and WCFT Cardy-like structure in continuum limit; explicit correspondence between discrete monodromies and continuous Wilson loops; identification of holonomy sectors as fundamental state labels.

Conclusion: Warped gravitational thermodynamics admits a fundamentally holonomy-based, discrete formulation where boundary monodromy sectors—not smooth geometry—organize physical states; this provides a background-independent foundation compatible with warped AdS₃/CFT₂ duality.

Abstract: We investigate a discrete warped Chern-Simons description of three-dimensional warped gravity based on boundary holonomies and monodromy sectors. Starting from the lower-spin SL(2, R) + U(1) gauge structure associated with warped AdS(3) holography and warped conformal field theories (WCFTs), we construct a discrete boundary framework in which ordered products of link holonomies replace continuous gauge connections along noncontractible cycles. In this setting, boundary monodromies become the primary gauge-invariant observables characterizing the physical sectors of the theory. We show that the hyperbolic, eliliptic, and parabolic sectors naturally arise from the conjugacy classes of the discrete SL(2, R) monodromy, while the additional U(1) holonomy supplies the warped contribution to the boundary charges. Using these monodromy invariants, we derive a discrete entropy relation entirely from boundary holonomy data without relying on a smooth geometric thermal background. The resulting entropy reproduces the characteristic warped black-hole and WCFT structure in the continuum limit. We further demonstrate that the continuum warped holonomy conditions are recovered from the large-lattice limit of the ordered boundary products, establishing a direct correspondence between discrete monodromies and continuous Wilson loops. Our analysis suggests that warped gravitational thermodynamics may be understood from a fundamentally holonomy-based perspective in which boundary monodromy sectors provide an alternative organizational description of the physical states within the discrete warped framework.
  Keywords: warped Chern-Simons theory, boundary monodromies, holonomy sectors, Wilson loops, warped thermodynamics.

</details>


### [84] [Hamiltonian formulation of Carrollian Maxwell theory in Deformed Light-cone Kaluza-Klein-like Null reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22050)
*Limin Zeng*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs magnetic and electric Carrollian Maxwell theories via Kaluza-Klein-like null reduction of a complex Maxwell field in a Bargmann-deformed light-cone background, preserving U(1) Gauss constraint and gauge invariance; it reveals new scalar-field couplings and demonstrates the method’s broader applicability to gauge theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct consistent, gauge-invariant Carrollian Maxwell theories—particularly uncovering previously overlooked scalar-field sectors—by leveraging a geometrically motivated, symmetry-preserving null reduction scheme.

Method: Kaluza-Klein-like null reduction of a complex Maxwell field on a Bargmann-deformed light-cone background; careful scaling analysis to isolate magnetic/electric Carrollian limits; Hamiltonian formulation ensuring first-class U(1) Gauss constraint preservation.

Result: Derivation of both standard magnetic and electric Carrollian Maxwell theories; discovery of novel scalar-field degrees of freedom appearing either coupled or decoupled—unattainable via prior reduction methods; explicit demonstration that gauge invariance and constraint structure survive the Carrollian limit.

Conclusion: The deformed light-cone null reduction method is a robust, symmetry-respecting framework for constructing Carrollian gauge theories; it uncovers richer structure—including scalar sectors—and extends the scope of Carrollian effective field theory beyond existing approaches.

Abstract: We construct magnetic and electric Carrollian Maxwell theories by performing Kaluza-Klein-like null reduction of a complex Maxwell field in a Bargmann deformed light-cone background with manifest gauge symmetry. The procedure preserves a first-class U(1) Gauss constraint throughout the Carrollian limit. Gauge invariance is therefore maintained in our Hamiltonian formulation. By choosing different scalings, we obtain standard magnetic Carrollian theory and electric Carrollian theory. However, a scalar field could appear in the Carrollian theory in a coupled or decoupled way, which has not been found by previous methods. This result fully reveals the diversity of Carrollian theories accessible through the deformed light-cone Kaluza-Klein-like null reduction method. Furthermore, our work provides an explicit example of the correct application of this approach, thereby broadening the scope of its applicability to gauge theories.

</details>


### [85] [Quiver Approach to Symmetry Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30354)
*Vivek Chakrabhavi,Mirjam Cvetič,Jonathan J. Heckman,Shani Meynet*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces an algebraic method using brane probe path algebras to extract global symmetry anomalies of 5D SCFTs from M-theory on Calabi-Yau cones, complementing and simplifying geometric approaches involving intersection numbers or η-invariants.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a computationally tractable and general algebraic framework for determining global symmetry anomalies in 5D SCFTs arising from M-theory on Calabi-Yau threefold cones—especially where traditional geometric methods (e.g., triple intersections or η-invariants) are unknown or combinatorially prohibitive.

Method: Extracts anomaly data from the path algebra of D-branes probing the Calabi-Yau cone X, leveraging representation theory and quiver structure associated with the singularity; applies to toric threefolds including C³/Γ orbifolds and non-orbifold Sasaki–Einstein cones.

Result: Demonstrates equivalence (in principle) and practical advantage of the path-algebraic approach over geometric anomaly computations; successfully computes anomalies for multiple toric examples where geometric methods are cumbersome or unavailable.

Conclusion: The path algebra of brane probes encodes global symmetry anomalies of 5D SCFTs engineered from M-theory on Calabi-Yau cones, offering a robust, algebraic alternative to geometric techniques—particularly valuable for non-orbifold and highly singular backgrounds.

Abstract: Global symmetry anomalies of a quantum field theory (QFT) can be packaged as specific couplings of a higher-dimensional symmetry theory (SymTh). In this work we show that for 5D superconformal field theories (SCFTs) engineered from M-theory backgrounds $X$ a Calabi-Yau cone, this data can be extracted from the path algebra of branes probing $X$. This provides a complementary algebraic approach compared with more geometric computations based on the explicit calculation of triple intersection numbers in a resolved geometry and / or $η$-invariants extracted from the boundary geometry $\partial X$. Our method applies in situations where the counterpart geometric computation is either unknown or combinatorially unwieldy. We illustrate with several toric threefold examples, including orbifolds $\mathbb{C}^{3} / Γ$ and more general non-orbifold Calabi-Yau cones of Sasaki-Einstein five-manifolds.

</details>


### [86] [Thermodynamic and Topological Phase Transitions of AdS Black Holes with Nonminimal $F^{αβ}F^{γλ}R_{αγ}R_{βλ}$ Coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22204)
*Mehdi Sadeghi,Faramarz Rahmani*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies how a non-minimal coupling term deforms the topology and phase structure of a 4D asymptotically AdS black hole, shifting it from W=+1 to W=0 topological class while preserving van der Waals-type phase transitions within a Hawking–Page framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how higher-curvature electromagnetic couplings induce topological and thermodynamic phase transitions in black holes beyond standard Einstein–Maxwell–AdS models.

Method: Perturbative analysis to first order in coupling ε for the non-minimal term F^{αβ}F^{γλ}R_{αγ}R_{βλ}; derivation of thermodynamic quantities; verification of first law and Smarr relation; topological classification via W-invariant.

Result: The black hole transitions from topological class W^{1+} (Reissner–Nordström–AdS limit) to W^{0−} under nonzero ε; van der Waals-type first-order phase transition persists in an intermediate regime embedded in the global Hawking–Page transition; ε acts as a topological deformation parameter.

Conclusion: Non-minimal electromagnetic–curvature coupling induces a topological phase transition while sustaining conventional thermodynamic criticality, establishing a hybrid black hole system where topology and thermodynamics are co-modulated by a single coupling parameter.

Abstract: The conventional and topological phase transitions of a four-dimensional asymptotically AdS black hole with a non-minimal coupling term $F^{αβ}F^{γλ}R_{αγ}R_{βλ}$ are investigated. Using a perturbative approach to first order in the coupling $ε$, the thermodynamic quantities are derived and the first law and Smarr relation are verified. Intriguingly, while the $ε= 0$ limit yields the standard Reissner--Nordström--AdS black hole belonging to the $W^{1+}$ topological class ($W = +1$), switching on the non-minimal coupling fundamentally transforms the topology to the $W^{0-}$ class ($W = 0$). This transition occurs while the van der Waals-type first-order phase transition survives in the intermediate region, embedded within the overall Hawking--Page pattern, rendering the system a hybrid black hole thermodynamic system. The coupling $ε$ thus acts as a topological deformation parameter that alters the universal classification of the system, despite the perturbative nature of the solution.

</details>


### [87] [Hilbert Functions and Line Bundle Cohomology on CICY Threefolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22275)
*Juntao Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explains the piecewise polynomial structure of line bundle cohomology dimensions on CICY threefolds using Hilbert functions of Koszul spectral sequence maps, provides analytic proofs or finite-box certifications for chamber formulae, discovers new wall structures, and constructs cohomology formula libraries for two previously unanalyzed CICYs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To theoretically explain the empirically observed chamber-wise polynomial behavior of line bundle cohomology dimensions on complete intersection Calabi–Yau threefolds—crucial for string phenomenology—and to move beyond data-driven extrapolation toward rigorous, computable foundations.

Method: Leverages the Koszul spectral sequence and Bott–Borel–Weil theory to identify non-vanishing ambient cohomology groups; analyzes rank defects of Koszul differentials via Hilbert functions of associated kernel/cokernel modules; combines analytic arguments with finite-box certification theorems to validate infinite families of line bundles.

Result: Analytically recovers nearly all known piecewise cohomology formulae; proves finite-box certificates for remaining cases; identifies refined wall structures beyond empirical chamber decompositions; constructs explicit cohomology formula libraries for two new CICY threefolds.

Conclusion: Hilbert-function methods provide a rigorous, systematic, and computationally tractable framework for line bundle cohomology on CICYs—enabling faster, certified inputs for string model-building scans.

Abstract: Line bundle cohomology on complete intersection Calabi--Yau threefolds is an important input in string phenomenology. Previous work, based on direct extrapolation and on machine-learning analyses of finite data sets, has shown that these cohomology dimensions often admit chamber-wise polynomial descriptions on the Picard lattice. In this paper we explain this structure through the Hilbert functions associated with the non-trivial maps in the Koszul spectral sequence. Once Bott--Borel--Weil theory determines the non-vanishing ambient cohomology groups, the rank defects of the relevant Koszul maps are governed by Hilbert functions of cokernel or kernel modules. This turns many empirical chamber formulae into explicit rank-defect statements, with proofs that are either analytic or certified on finite boxes. Using this approach, we recover analytically almost all of the piecewise formulae appearing in the existing literature. For the remaining cases, we prove finite-box certificate theorems for infinite families of line bundles in the specified regions. We also identify new wall structures on certain CICYs which refine the chamber decompositions suggested by finite-range data. Finally, we use the same framework to construct line bundle cohomology formula libraries for two CICY threefolds which had not previously been analysed in this way. These results suggest that Hilbert-function methods can provide promising faster inputs for future string model-building scans based on line bundle constructions.

</details>


### [88] [Eight loop form factors, amplitudes and patterns in planar $\mathcal{N}=4$ super-Yang-Mills theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22380)
*Lance J. Dixon,Zhenjie Li*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the three-point form factor for the operator tr φ³ in planar N=4 SYM to 8 loops, leveraging shared polylogarithmic structure with the known tr φ² form factor and identifying all-order patterns in symbol coefficients for its leading discontinuity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend understanding of integrability and analytic structure in planar N=4 SYM by computing a new nontrivial observable—the three-point form factor of the unprotected operator tr φ³—and uncovering universal symbol-level patterns tied to its leading discontinuity.

Method: Symbol calculus and bootstrap methods within the space of generalized polylogarithms constrained by physical principles (e.g., Steinmann relations, cluster adjacency), guided by antipodal duality and comparison with the tr φ² form factor; analysis of iterated discontinuities and coefficient sequences in the symbol.

Result: The tr φ³ three-point form factor is determined through 8 loops; its symbol lies in the same restricted polylogarithmic function space as the tr φ² form factor; all-order patterns are identified for coefficient sequences in the symbol of its leading discontinuity.

Conclusion: The tr φ³ form factor exhibits deep structural parallels with tr φ², reinforcing the universality of the underlying mathematical framework; the observed all-order symbol patterns suggest hidden algebraic or combinatorial structures governing discontinuities in planar N=4 SYM amplitudes and form factors.

Abstract: The simplest nontrivial amplitude in planar $\mathcal{N}=4$ super-Yang-Mills theory is six-gluon scattering in the maximally-helicity-violating configuration. It has been computed to 8 loops with the help of antipodal duality, which relates it to the three-point form factor of a protected operator, the chiral stress tensor super-multiplet, represented also as ${\rm tr} φ^2$. In this talk, we describe the computation to 8 loops of another three-point form factor, for the operator ${\rm tr}φ^3$. This form factor lives in the same restricted space of polylogarithms as the ${\rm tr}φ^2$ form factor. We also report on all-order patterns for sequences of coefficients in the symbols of these polylogarithmic results, for the leading discontinuity of the ${\rm tr}φ^3$ form factor.

</details>


### [89] [Holographic s+p superconductors with nonlinear electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22459)
*Ru-Qing Chen,Hui Zeng,Zhang-Yu Nie*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies a holographic s+p superconductor model with nonlinear electrodynamics in the probe limit, finding that increasing the nonlinearity parameter b suppresses both s- and p-wave condensates (s-wave more strongly), shrinks the pure s-wave phase region, lowers the charge-ratio threshold for s+p coexistence, and induces spontaneous horizon-excluded charge accumulation—requiring a revised definition of superconducting charge density and altering optical conductivity (e.g., introducing a persistent imaginary-part minimum confusing gap extraction).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how nonlinear electrodynamics affects phase structure, condensate competition, and electromagnetic response in holographic s+p superconductors—particularly addressing anomalies in charge density definition and optical conductivity arising from intrinsic field self-interaction.

Method: Numerical solution of equations of motion in the probe limit for a holographic black hole background coupled to scalar (s-wave) and vector (p-wave) fields and nonlinear electrodynamics; construction of condensate and grand potential curves, b–T phase diagrams, and optical conductivity spectra; redefinition of superconducting charge density via background subtraction.

Result: 1) Increasing b suppresses both s- and p-wave condensates, with stronger suppression on s-wave; 2) Pure s-wave phase region shrinks in b–T diagram; 3) Lower q_p/q_s ratio required for s+p coexistence as b increases; 4) Spontaneous charge accumulation outside horizon occurs even in normal phase due to nonlinear EM self-interaction, invalidating standard charge density formula; 5) Revised (background-subtracted) charge density is proposed; 6) Imaginary part of optical conductivity develops a finite-frequency minimum in normal phase for b > 0, persisting near critical temperature in superconducting phase—obscuring gap frequency identification.

Conclusion: Nonlinear electrodynamics fundamentally modifies the thermodynamic and electromagnetic properties of holographic s+p superconductors: it alters phase competition, necessitates a refined definition of superconducting charge density, and introduces spectral features in optical conductivity that complicate conventional gap analysis—highlighting the critical role of bulk EM self-interaction in holographic superconductivity.

Abstract: We investigate a holographic s+p superconductor model coupled to nonlinear electrodynamics in the probe limit. The equations of motion are solved numerically, and the condensates as well as the grand potential curves for phase transitions at different values of the nonlinear parameter $b$ are illustrated. It is found that as $b$ increases, both the pure s-wave and p-wave condensates are suppressed. From the $b-T$ phase diagram, we observe that the region of the pure s-wave phase gradually shrinks with increasing $b$, which is attributed to the stronger suppression on the s-wave condensate compared to the one on the p-wave. Moreover, a smaller charge ratio $q_p/q_s$ is needed for the s+p coexistent phase to appear as $b$ grows. A particularly interesting feature is that, due to the nonlinear self-interaction of the electromagnetic field, charge accumulates spontaneously outside the event horizon from the bulk perspective even in the absence of scalar and vector condensates, thereby invalidating the conventional formula for the superconducting charge density. We improve the definition of the superconducting charge density as the background-subtracted value of the accumulated charge outside the horizon with respect to that in the normal phase. Furthermore, the optical conductivity in the normal phase is also modified by this accumulated charge for finite $b$, and its imaginary part develops a minimum at a finite frequency. This minimum persists in the superconducting phase near the critical point, confusing the extraction of the gap frequency.

</details>


### [90] [Nonlinear Geometrizability of State-Dependent Proto-Area in Approximate Holographic Codes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22598)
*Ruiliang Li*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes exact finite-resolution criteria and necessary/sufficient conditions for when state-dependent proto-area data—derived from approximate quantum error correction in AdS/CFT—can be realized as a local bulk metric, particularly on time-reflection-symmetric asymptotically AdS₃ slices; it introduces geometric obstructions (e.g., gauge-invariant quadratic obstruction via a metric-forced Jacobi equation), provides primal/dual certificates for polyhedral reconstruction, identifies the geometric tangent space as the range of the geodesic X-ray transform, and demonstrates controlled nongeometric behavior in Hamiltonian-skewed codes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether state-dependent proto-area data arising from approximate recovery in holographic quantum error-correcting codes genuinely correspond to a consistent local bulk geometry—or instead encode intrinsic nongeometric features—especially in light of tensions between bulk locality and boundary entanglement structure.

Method: Analyzes recovery maps calibrated on the code channel and held fixed across logical-state families; employs finite-network polyhedral geometry and continuum differential-geometric tools (e.g., geodesic X-ray transform, Jacobi equations); derives exact two-jet compatibility criteria; constructs primal/dual certificates and explicit nongeometry witnesses; applies split-regularity analysis and gauge-invariant Hessian computation.

Result: Derives necessary and sufficient conditions for proto-area two-jets to originate from metric two-jets on asymptotically AdS₃ slices; identifies the geometric tangent space as the image of the rank-two geodesic X-ray transform; obtains a gauge-invariant quadratic obstruction via the normal Hessian of renormalized boundary length; proves stability of reconstruction and provides explicit witnesses of nongeometry; shows Hamiltonian-skewed codes realize tunable nongeometric responses up to quadratic order.

Conclusion: Proto-area data need not reflect a single local bulk metric; geometric consistency is governed by precise jet-level criteria and obstructions, with nongeometry being both detectable and quantifiable—establishing a rigorous framework for diagnosing emergent geometry in holographic codes.

Abstract: State-dependent proto-area data produced by approximate recovery need not be compatible with a single local bulk metric. Using recovery maps calibrated on the code channel and held fixed along a logical-state family, we derive exact finite-resolution criteria and, near the hyperbolic disk, necessary and sufficient conditions for a regular proto-area two-jet to arise from a metric two-jet on a time-reflection-symmetric asymptotically AdS$_3$ slice. Finite networks give a polyhedral realization problem with primal and dual certificates, stable reconstruction, and explicit witnesses of nongeometry. In the continuum, the geometric tangent space is the range of the rank-two geodesic X-ray transform. A metric-forced Jacobi equation determines the normal Hessian of the renormalized boundary-length image and yields a gauge-invariant quadratic obstruction. Under a split-regularity hypothesis, nearby geometric data form a local graph; the two-jet criterion itself is unconditional for regular data. Hamiltonian-skewed codes realize both first-order nongeometry and a response whose first obstruction appears only at quadratic order. The compatible metric perturbation is reconstructed modulo boundary-fixing diffeomorphisms.

</details>


### [91] [The conformal null string in $d+2$ and $d$ dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22498)
*Ulf Lindström*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper confirms that the tensionless string with gauged scale symmetry is a Dirac reduction of the conformal string to Minkowski space, explicitly demonstrating how the higher-dimensional constraint algebra (Virasoro ⋉ su(1,1) Kac–Moody) reduces to the Carrollian–Weyl symmetry in d dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To corroborate the claim that the recently studied tensionless string with gauged scale symmetry arises as a dimensional reduction of the conformal string, specifically via Dirac’s embedding formalism in d+2-dimensional conformal space.

Method: Dirac reduction procedure applied to the conformal string in d+2-dimensional ambient (Dirac) space; analysis of constraint algebras under slicing to Minkowski spacetime; explicit mapping of the Virasoro ⋉ su(1,1) Kac–Moody algebra to the Carrollian–Weyl algebra in d dimensions.

Result: The tensionless string model with gauged scale symmetry is shown to emerge as a consistent Dirac reduction; the full constraint algebra of the conformal string reduces precisely to the Carrollian–Weyl symmetry algebra in d dimensions.

Conclusion: The tensionless string with gauged scale symmetry is not an independent model but a geometrically realized reduction of the conformal string, strengthening its conceptual foundation and clarifying its symmetry origin in conformal geometry.

Abstract: In \cite{Lindstrom:2026quz} it is pointed out how the tensionless string with a gauged scale symmetry discussed in the recent articles, \cite{Sheikh-Jabbari:2026cnj,Sheikh-Jabbari:2026vqh,Sheikh-Jabbari:2026tpf}, is a reduction of the conformal string \cite{Gustafsson:1994kr} to Minkowski space. Here we corroborate this by choices of slices in Dirac $d+2$ dimensional conformal space. We perform a Dirac reduction of the model and its algebra of constraints and see how they map to the constraints in $d$ dimensions, including how the semidirect product of the Virasoro algebra with a su(1,1) Kac-Moody algebra becomes the Corrollian-Weyl symmetry in $d$ dimensions.

</details>


### [92] [Exact solution of the Seven-Vertex Model on a dynamical lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22651)
*Mateus da Silva Junca,Ivan Kostov,Andre Alves Lima*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper fully solves the gravitational seven-vertex model—a one-parameter deformation of the six-vertex model on dynamical lattices—by mapping it to a loop gas on dynamical triangulations with curvature-dependent loop weights; it reformulates the model as a large-N matrix model (7vMM), solves it exactly using Jacobi theta functions, derives its non-algebraic parametric spectral curve, and determines the phase diagram in the cosmological constant–temperature plane, including critical phases and the scaling limit near the tricritical point.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To solve the gravitational seven-vertex model exactly and characterize its phase structure, extending prior partial results and clarifying how curvature-dependent loop weights—unlike topological O(n) weights—affect critical behavior on dynamical lattices.

Method: Reformulation of the model as a large-N matrix model (7vMM); exact solution via Jacobi theta functions; derivation of the parametric (non-algebraic) spectral curve; analysis of the scaling limit near the tricritical point; phase diagram construction in the cosmological constant–temperature parameter space.

Result: Complete exact solution expressed in Jacobi theta functions; explicit parametric form of the non-algebraic spectral curve of 7vMM; full phase diagram identifying massive, dilute, and dense critical phases; identification of critical boundaries and recovery of the previously known scaling solution as an asymptotic near the tricritical point.

Conclusion: The gravitational seven-vertex model is exactly solvable beyond the scaling limit, exhibiting rich critical behavior governed by curvature-coupled loop weights rather than topological ones; its exact solution reveals a non-algebraic spectral geometry and confirms universality-class distinctions from the gravitational O(n) model.

Abstract: We give the complete solution of the one-parameter deformation of the six-vertex model on dynamical lattice introduced in [1] and dubbed gravitational seven-vertex model. The statistical model in question is mapped to a gas of self- and mutually avoiding loops on dynamical triangulations, with a temperature coupling controlling the volume not occupied by loops. The phase diagram is characterised by massive, dilute and dense critical phases, similarly to the gravitational O(n) loop model. There is however an important difference -- in our model the weights of the loops are not topological but depend on the form of the loop and on the curvature defects of the lattice via lattice spin connection. The seven-vertex model on dynamical lattice is nevertheless exactly solvable after being reformulated as a large-N matrix model, which we will refer to as 7vMM, and the solution in the scaling limit was found in [1]. Here we derive the full solution in terms of Jacobi theta functions and present the (non-algebraic) spectral curve of 7vMM in a parametric form. We obtain the phase diagram in the space of the two coupling constants -- the cosmological constant and the temperature -- and identify the critical phases along the boundary of the physical domain. We derive the scaling solution of [1] as the asymptotic of the full solution in the vicinity of the tricritical point separating the phases of dense and massive loops.

</details>


### [93] [Unified theory of oscillons and modes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22680)
*F. Blaschke,T. Romanczukiewicz,K. Slawinska,A. Wereszczynski*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper introduces 'wobblerons'—nonlinear oscillon-kink bound states—and interprets oscillons as localized discrete resonant (non-normalizable) modes, either threshold or antibound, enabled by nonlinearity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified conceptual framework for oscillons as localized non-normalizable modes and extend this to kink-based excitations.

Method: Theoretical analysis using nonlinear field theory, identifying oscillons with threshold and antibound modes, and constructing oscillon-kink bound states ('wobblerons').

Result: Discovery of wobblerons—oscillon-kink bound states—and demonstration that oscillons arise from either threshold or antibound (positive-energy, non-normalizable) modes due to nonlinearity.

Conclusion: Oscillons are not isolated phenomena but manifestations of discrete non-normalizable modes in nonlinear field theories; their generalization to kinks yields wobblerons, enriching the taxonomy of nonlinear localized excitations.

Abstract: We show that an oscillon can be understood as a localized discrete resonant (non-normalizable) mode. Specifically, oscillon in the vacuum arises from the threshold mode, which because of nonlinearity gets localized. Following this idea, we find {\it wobblerons} - nonlinear excitations of kinks, that is, oscillons-kink bound state. Now, the oscillon can also originate in an antibound mode, i.e., a discrete, positive energy but non-normalizable mode.

</details>


### [94] [UV-finite Effective Field Theory from Quantized Irreversible Null-geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22788)
*Si-xue Qin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a UV-finite 4D effective field theory by embedding dynamics in a double-exponential capacity measure from quantized irreversible null-geometry, yielding a universal proper-time cutoff that eliminates UV divergences; overlapping sub-divergences are reinterpreted as distributional artifacts corrected via BPHZ as an algebraic mapping; QED and QCD yield finite bare parameters while preserving key symmetries (Ward-Takahashi, Slavnov-Taylor); the framework generates closed non-perturbative Schwinger-Dyson equations for chiral symmetry breaking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve persistent ultraviolet (UV) divergences and overlapping sub-divergences in standard perturbative quantum field theory by constructing a fundamentally finite effective field theory in four dimensions without dimensional regularization or ad hoc cutoffs.

Method: Embedding the dynamical operator in a double-exponential capacity measure derived from quantized irreversible null-geometry; analytic expansion of the exact operator resolvent to derive a universal macroscopic proper-time bound $T \ge \tau_0$; topological contraction of UV phase space; reinterpretation of overlapping subdivergences as distributional pseudo-singularities arising from analytic oversmoothing; systematic correction using BPHZ strictly as an algebraic mapping operator (not subtraction); derivation of closed non-perturbative Schwinger-Dyson equations.

Result: A UV-finite 4D EFT with analytically defined absolute UV boundary; finite bare electron mass ($m_0 \approx 0.427$ MeV) and renormalization constants in QED preserving $Z_1 = Z_2$; finite bare strong coupling ($\alpha_{s,0} \approx 0.0191$) in QCD preserving Slavnov-Taylor identities; anomaly-free, consistent foundation for non-perturbative chiral symmetry breaking and mass generation.

Conclusion: Ultraviolet finiteness can be achieved natively in four-dimensional spacetime through geometrically constrained operator embedding and proper-time–based topological regularization; overlapping divergences are not physical but arise from continuum approximations violating discrete lattice bounds; symmetry-preserving finiteness is attainable without breaking gauge or chiral structure, enabling rigorous non-perturbative analysis.

Abstract: Standard perturbative quantum field theory is persistently challenged by ultraviolet (UV) divergences and overlapping sub-divergences. In this work, we construct an absolutely UV-finite effective field theory (EFT) natively in four-dimensional spacetime. We embed the complete dynamical operator within a double-exponential capacity measure derived from quantized irreversible null-geometry. By analytically expanding the exact operator resolvent, we demonstrate that a universal macroscopic proper-time bound ($T \ge τ_0$) natively emerges, structurally collapsing the high-order UV phase space via topological contraction. This mathematically defines the absolute UV boundary of the EFT. Consequently, overlapping subgraph divergences are rigorously identified as distributional pseudo-singularities caused by the continuous approximation analytically oversmoothing the discrete lattice bounds. These artifacts are systematically corrected by employing the Bogoliubov-Parasiuk-Hepp-Zimmermann (BPHZ) protocol strictly as an algebraic mapping operator. Evaluating Quantum Electrodynamics in 4D, we obtain a finite electron bare mass ($m_0 \approx 0.427$ MeV) and derive finite renormalization constants, analytically preserving the exact Ward-Takahashi identity ($Z_1=Z_2$). In Quantum Chromodynamics, the exact preservation of Slavnov-Taylor identities yields a calculable finite bare strong coupling ($α_{s,0} \approx 0.0191$). Generating analytically closed non-perturbative Schwinger-Dyson Equations, this framework provides a consistent, anomaly-free mathematical foundation for finite effective field theories to address dynamic chiral symmetry breaking and mass generation.

</details>


### [95] [On the temperature dependence of quasinormal modes in SYK and holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22679)
*Matthew Dodelson,Om Gupta,Márk Mezei,Diandian Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the quasinormal mode (QNM) analysis of the SYK model from infinite to finite temperature, revealing a continuous transition to the JT gravity regime; it shows monotonic relaxation-rate increase with temperature only in the strong-coupling (gravitational) limit, and yields new insights into operator growth.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quasinormal modes of the SYK model evolve with temperature and bridge the infinite-temperature 'Christmas tree' spectrum to the low-temperature JT gravity dual, while probing universality of relaxation dynamics across holographic and quantum many-body systems.

Method: Analytic and numerical computation of quasinormal modes (Ruelle–Pollicott resonances) of the finite-temperature SYK model; comparison with AdS black hole QNMs, dynamical phase transitions, and large-p SYK chain; analysis of operator growth via spectral properties of the Liouvillian.

Result: Finite-temperature SYK QNMs smoothly interpolate between infinite-temperature Christmas tree and low-temperature JT gravity spectra; relaxation rate increases monotonically with temperature only in the strong-coupling (gravitational) regime; new bounds and scaling behaviors for operator growth are derived.

Conclusion: The QNM structure encodes the strength of coupling and effective gravitational description: monotonic thermal dependence of relaxation is a hallmark of semiclassical gravity, absent in weakly coupled or non-holographic systems; SYK serves as a controllable testbed for universal features of quantum chaos and holography.

Abstract: It was recently found that the quasinormal modes (or Ruelle--Pollicott resonances) of the SYK model at infinite temperature form a Christmas tree shape, reminiscent of AdS black holes. We generalise this computation to finite temperature, allowing us to continuously connect the infinite temperature results to the low temperature regime dual to JT gravity. We contrast the movement of the quasinormal modes with a few examples: various AdS black holes, dynamical phase transitions, and the large $p$ SYK chain. We find that the relaxation rate increases monotonically with temperature only at strong coupling, corresponding to the gravitational regime. Byproducts of our investigations are new results on operator growth that may be of independent interest.

</details>


### [96] [A Linearized Obstruction to the Supersymmetric Extension of Conformal Boundary Conditions in Euclidean Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22810)
*Xingyang Yu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper shows that Witten's conformal boundary condition for Euclidean gravity cannot be extended to preserve half of the supersymmetry in linearized minimal supergravity, due to a pointwise, highest-derivative obstruction in the gravitino boundary conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether Witten's conformal boundary condition—which fixes boundary conformal class and mean curvature—can be supersymmetrically extended in supergravity, preserving half the supersymmetry.

Method: Linearized analysis of minimal supergravity; examination of possible gravitino boundary conditions (local, pseudodifferential, APS-type, chiral/Robin) compatible with BRST symmetry and ellipticity; investigation of chiral supersymmetry closure under fixed bosonic boundary data.

Result: No half-supersymmetric extension exists in the linearized regime: any attempt to impose a supersymmetry-preserving gravitino boundary condition forces Dirichlet control on the trace-free extrinsic curvature—the very quantity left free (Neumann) in Witten’s prescription—creating a pointwise, highest-derivative obstruction invariant under gauge transformations.

Conclusion: The obstruction is intrinsic to the linearized, highest-derivative structure and cannot be removed by standard elliptic or BRST-compatible boundary conditions; nonlinear supercovariant boundary terms coupling trace-free extrinsic curvature to gravitino bilinears may circumvent it.

Abstract: Witten's conformal boundary condition \cite{Witten:2018lgb} provides an elliptic boundary-value problem for the finite-boundary perturbative Euclidean gravitational path integral: one fixes the boundary conformal class and the mean curvature, while the trace-free extrinsic curvature is left free as the conjugate response. We show that this perturbative construction admits no half-supersymmetric extension in linearized minimal supergravity. For fixed conformal bosonic data, no half-dimensional gravitino boundary condition (local or pseudodifferential, APS-type included, with any compatible ghost condition at highest-derivative order) closes the full preserved chiral supersymmetry. Supersymmetry first selects the natural local chiral gravitino datum. Acting back on this datum then produces the trace-free extrinsic curvature, precisely the response that the conformal prescription leaves unfixed. The obstruction is therefore not the failure of a particular elliptic ansatz: even the chiral/Robin completion that is LS-elliptic and BRST-compatible at highest-derivative order would impose Dirichlet control on a Neumann response. The obstruction is pointwise in tangential momentum and survives compensating gauge transformations. It is a linearized, highest-derivative obstruction, not a global or nonlinear no-go; nonlinear supercovariant boundary terms may evade it by tying the trace-free extrinsic curvature to gravitino bilinears.

</details>


### [97] [On background fields and a cutoff in sigma models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23088)
*N. V. Kharuk*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper compares two chiral field decomposition methods in a 2D nonlinear sigma model with Heisenberg group symmetry, identifies the background-field-compatible variant, performs one-loop renormalization, computes two-loop power-law singularities, examines extension to classical action, and analyzes cutoff consistency with functional relations in the background field method.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine which chiral field decomposition variant is compatible with background field method (BFM) for generating functional construction and to ensure consistent renormalization and regularization within BFM.

Method: Analytical field-theoretic computation: comparison of two chiral decompositions; background field method implementation; dimensional regularization or similar for one-loop renormalization; perturbative expansion for two-loop singularity analysis; functional identity verification under regulator cutoff.

Result: Only one decomposition preserves BFM consistency; explicit one-loop counterterms derived; power-law divergences identified at two loops; extended classical action formulated; cutoff shown compatible with Slavnov–Taylor or Ward-type identities in BFM.

Conclusion: The background field method imposes stringent constraints on field decomposition; consistency requires adherence to specific geometric/field-structure conditions; proper decomposition enables robust renormalization and preserves essential symmetries under regularization.

Abstract: In this paper, using the example of a two-dimensional nonlinear sigma model with the Heisenberg group, we compare two variants of chiral field decomposition into a background part and a fluctuation. It is shown that only one of these methods is consistent with the construction of the generating functional by introducing a background field. Furthermore, we perform a one-loop renormalization of the quantum action, calculate power-law singularities in the two-loop approximation, and consider transition to an extended classical action. Finally, we study the consistency of the cutoff with special functional relations within the framework of the background field method.

</details>


### [98] [Extended Thermodynamics and Renyi Entropy Beyond Fixed Central Charge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22849)
*Chatchai Promsiri,Phuwadon Chunaksorn,Ratchaphat Nakarachinda,Ekapong Hirunsirisawat*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a novel central-charge Rényi entropy for thermal CFTs on a hyperbolic cylinder in a grand canonical ensemble with fixed charge, volume, and central charge potential; it satisfies all Rényi inequalities, captures entanglement across fluctuating holographic CFTs, and identifies a critical Rényi index n_* separating two statistical regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the physical interpretation of central charge variations in conformal thermodynamics and extend Rényi entropy to account for fluctuations in the central charge as a thermodynamic degree of freedom.

Method: Constructs a central-charge Rényi entropy using the Casini-Huerta-Myers (CHM) map applied to thermal CFTs on a hyperbolic cylinder within a (\tilde{Q}, \mathcal{V}, \mu_C) grand canonical ensemble; analyzes near-extremal configurations and derives residual entropy and characteristic scales via conformal thermodynamics and the CHM correspondence.

Result: The proposed entropy satisfies all four fundamental Rényi entropy inequalities; residual entropy originates from the central charge sector (not thermal excitations); a mass gap defines a characteristic temperature \tilde{T}_*, mapped via CHM to a critical Rényi index n_*, which demarcates a dominant-theory regime (n > n_*) and a multi-theory regime (n < n_*).

Conclusion: Central charge can be consistently treated as a fluctuating thermodynamic variable; the central-charge Rényi entropy provides a physically grounded, holographically motivated generalization of Rényi entropy that reveals qualitatively distinct statistical behaviors governed by n_*.

Abstract: An outstanding problem in the framework of conformal thermodynamics concerns the interpretation of variations in the central charge $C$. In this paper, we construct a novel central-charge Rényi entropy via the Casini-Huerta-Myers (CHM) map by considering thermal CFTs on a hyperbolic cylinder within a fixed charge, field theory volume and central charge potential $(\tilde{Q},\mathcal{V},μ_C)$ grand canonical ensemble. We demonstrate that the resulting entropy satisfies all four fundamental Rényi entropy inequalities throughout the admissible range of $μ_C$, establishing its consistency as a genuine Rényi measure. Physically, this novel measure extends conventional Rényi entropy by capturing the degree of entanglement across a statistical ensemble of holographic CFTs with fluctuating degrees of freedom. Furthermore, our conformal thermodynamic analysis of near-extremal configurations reveals that residual entropy arises from the central charge sector rather than thermal excitations. The mass gap that separates the extremal state and the first thermal excitation introduces a characteristic temperature scale $\tilde{T}_*$, which translates via the CHM map into a distinguished characteristic Rényi index $n_*$. Crucially, we propose that $n_*$ separates the theory space into two qualitatively distinct statistical regimes: a dominant-theory regime ($n > n_*$) governed by the most probable CFT realizations, and a multi-theory regime ($n < n_*$) where a broader spectrum of fluctuating theories and higher-energy modular excitations becomes increasingly relevant.

</details>


### [99] [Bounds on nonlinear electrodynamics via resummed relative entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23323)
*Pietro Conzinu,Daiki Ueda*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper uses relative entropy in electromagnetic backgrounds to systematically probe nonlinear electrodynamic effective field theories (EFTs), deriving sign constraints on EFT coefficients from its non-negativity, distinguishing perturbative truncation artifacts from genuine physical instabilities (e.g., Schwinger effect), and establishing unified consistency/stability diagnostics across fermionic QED, scalar QED, and Dirac-Born-Infeld theory via perturbative, resummed (Borel–Laplace), and non-perturbative (Schwinger proper-time) methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a systematic, model-independent diagnostic for both perturbative consistency and non-perturbative stability in nonlinear electromagnetic EFTs, going beyond leading-order positivity bounds by leveraging the information-theoretic structure of relative entropy.

Method: Computes relative entropy between vacuum states in background electromagnetic fields within nonlinear EFTs; employs perturbative expansions, Borel–Laplace resummation, and Schwinger proper-time method; analyzes sign constraints and violations of non-negativity across coupling regimes and UV completions (including factorial/power-law coefficient growth).

Result: Non-negativity of perturbative relative entropy yields generalized positivity bounds on finite EFT truncations; violations in perturbation theory signal EFT breakdown, while violations in resummed/non-perturbative settings diagnose physical instabilities (e.g., Schwinger pair production); resummed relative entropy imposes universal constraints on coefficient growth in UV completions.

Conclusion: Relative entropy serves as a unified, rigorous diagnostic for both perturbative consistency (via sign constraints) and non-perturbative stability (via instability detection) in nonlinear electrodynamic EFTs, bridging formal EFT principles with physical phenomena like vacuum decay.

Abstract: We investigate nonlinear electrodynamic effective field theories (EFTs) through the relative entropy evaluated in suitable background electromagnetic fields. In this setup, the relative entropy encodes information about the infinite tower of higher-dimensional operators and provides a systematic probe of nonlinear EFT effects. We study these features in fermionic QED, scalar QED, and Dirac-Born-Infeld theory using perturbative analyses, resummation techniques such as Borel--Laplace resummation, and non-perturbative approaches including the Schwinger proper-time method. In the weak-coupling regime, we show that the non-negativity of the perturbative relative entropy imposes sign constraints on finite truncations of higher-dimensional operators, generalizing familiar positivity bounds on leading EFT coefficients. We further show that violations of non-negativity in the strong-coupling regime admit qualitatively different interpretations depending on the framework: perturbatively analyzed violations diagnose the breakdown of the truncated EFT expansion, whereas violations in resummed or genuinely non-perturbative relative entropy signal physical instabilities of the system, such as the Schwinger effect. Extending the analysis to broader classes of UV completions, including theories with factorial or power-law growth of EFT coefficients, we derive general constraints on nonlinear electrodynamic EFT effects from the non-negativity of the resummed relative entropy. Our results suggest that relative entropy provides a unified diagnostic of perturbative consistency and non-perturbative stability in nonlinear EFTs.

</details>


### [100] [NLO Angular Impulse and Leading Singularities to all orders in spin for Kerr Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23450)
*Lucas Haiashi,Gabriel Menezes*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the next-to-leading order (NLO) spin-dependent angular impulse in Kerr black hole scattering using the KMOC formalism and on-shell amplitudes, yielding all-order-in-spin compact integrand-level expressions, validating them via consistency checks (covariant spin condition, spin magnitude preservation, agreement with radial-action results), and extracting a spin-resummed non-relativistic gravitational potential from triangle leading singularities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance amplitude-based methods for classical gravitational dynamics by computing spin-dependent observables—specifically the NLO angular impulse (spin kick)—in binary Kerr black hole scattering, where spin effects are crucial beyond test-mass approximations.

Method: Uses the Kosower–Maybee–O'Connell (KMOC) formalism grounded in on-shell scattering amplitudes and leading singularities; derives spin-dependent angular impulse integrands valid to all orders in spin; performs consistency checks including covariant spin supplementary condition and comparison with radial-action formalism; extracts effective potential from triangle leading singularities.

Result: Compact, all-order-in-spin expressions for the NLO angular impulse; verification that conservative dynamics preserves spin magnitude and covariant spin condition through 2PM order; agreement of quadratic-in-spin result with radial-action calculations; derivation of a spin-resummed non-relativistic gravitational potential reproducing known spin-orbit interaction at linear order.

Conclusion: Amplitude-based approaches—particularly KMOC—are highly effective for computing classical spin-dependent observables in general relativity; this work strengthens their credibility and establishes a robust pathway toward higher-order and more complex spin-tailored gravitational wave modeling.

Abstract: We compute the next-to-leading order (NLO) angular impulse (spin kick) in the scattering of Kerr black holes using the Kosower--Maybee--O'Connell (KMOC) formalism. Our approach is based on on-shell scattering amplitudes and leading singularities, allowing for a direct extraction of classical observables from quantum amplitudes. We derive compact expressions for the spin-dependent angular impulse valid to all orders in the spin variables at the integrand level, and show that these results reduce to known expressions in the appropriate limits. We perform detailed consistency checks: the conservative result preserves both the covariant spin supplementary condition and the spin magnitude through 2PM order, and the quadratic-in-spin conservative result agrees with existing radial-action results after translating between the direct KMOC spin kick and the radial-action observable. In addition, we extract the corresponding non-relativistic gravitational potential from the triangle leading singularities, obtaining a representation that resums spin effects and reproduces the known spin-orbit interaction at linear order. Our results provide further evidence for the efficiency of amplitude-based methods in classical gravitational dynamics, and highlight the KMOC formalism as a powerful framework for computing spin-dependent observables in binary black hole scattering.

</details>


### [101] [Geometric and Statistical Thermo Field Dynamics in de Sitter Spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23484)
*D. S. Cabral,L. A. S. Evangelista,J. C. R. de Souza,A. F. Santos*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates quantum field dynamics for a massive scalar field non-minimally coupled to gravity in de Sitter spacetime, revealing observer-dependent vacuum/thermal interpretations (Bunch–Davies vs. Gibbons–Hawking), and constructs a thermal quantum field theory via Thermo Field Dynamics that unifies geometric (horizon-induced) and intrinsic thermal effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile observer-dependent thermal perceptions of the Bunch–Davies vacuum in de Sitter space—comoving observers see vacuum, static observers see thermal bath—and to develop a unified thermal formalism incorporating both horizon geometry and intrinsic finite-temperature effects.

Method: Applies Thermo Field Dynamics (TFD) with geometric doubling (from cosmological horizon) and statistical doubling (from intrinsic thermal bath); analyzes Bogoliubov transformations, particle number densities, and temporal evolution of Bogoliubov angle in both comoving and static frames.

Result: Demonstrates that TFD doubling reflects physical causal structure + finite-temperature physics; comoving frame yields conserved radiation-like particle density (matching CMB thermodynamics), while static frame exhibits Parker particle creation driven by finite-temperature effects; massive/non-minimally coupled fields introduce a characteristic thermal scale and initial-condition dependence.

Conclusion: Provides a unified framework for quantum fields in de Sitter spacetime that consistently incorporates both apparent horizon-induced (geometric) and intrinsic (statistical) thermal effects, grounded in observer-dependent vacuum structure and causal geometry.

Abstract: The dynamics of a massive scalar field non-minimally coupled to gravity in an expanding de Sitter universe are investigated. It is shown that a comoving observer identifies the Bunch--Davies state as the vacuum, whereas a static observer perceives the same state as a thermal bath at the Gibbons--Hawking temperature. Motivated by this observer dependence, a thermal formulation based on Thermo Field Dynamics is developed by combining the geometric doubling associated with the cosmological horizon with the statistical doubling induced by a intrinsec thermal bath. The resulting construction reveals that the doubling procedure is not merely a mathematical artifact, but rather a manifestation of the global causal structure of spacetime together with finite-temperature effects. The temporal evolution of the Bogoliubov angle is analyzed and the corresponding particle number densities are evaluated in both comoving and static frames. In the radiation limit, the comoving number density remains conserved, providing a thermodynamic evolution consistent with that of the Cosmic Microwave Background, whereas in the static frame finite-temperature effects sti\-mulate Parker particle creation. For massive and non-minimally coupled fields, the interplay between geometric and statistical temperatures gives rise to a characteristic thermal scale and a nontrivial dependence on the initial conditions. These results provide a unified framework for describing quantum fields in de Sitter spacetime in the presence of both apparent horizon-induced and intrinsec thermal effects.

</details>


### [102] [Microscopic entropy of de Sitter spacetime and entropic solution to the old cosmological constant problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23522)
*Mariano Cadoni,Lorenzo Herres,Lorenzo Orlando,Mirko Pitzalis*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes that the dimensionless coupling α in conformal gravity—interpreted as the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy of de Sitter spacetime—encodes horizon degrees of freedom; its monotonic IR-increasing renormalization group flow yields a cosmological constant matching observation, offering an entropic resolution to the old cosmological constant problem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the old cosmological constant problem by interpreting the small observed value of the cosmological constant as an entropic consequence of the large number of horizon degrees of freedom in de Sitter space.

Method: Combines functional renormalization group (FRG) techniques, holographic principles, and emergent gravity frameworks to analyze the scale dependence α(k) of a dimensionless coupling tied to Weyl symmetry breaking and residual scale symmetry in Einstein and conformal gravity.

Result: The RG flow α(k) is shown to be monotonically increasing toward the infrared; imposing this monotonicity yields a cosmological constant consistent with observational bounds—i.e., Λ ~ H₀² Mₚ²—naturally explaining its smallness via the huge Bekenstein-Hawking entropy of the de Sitter horizon.

Conclusion: The small observed cosmological constant is not fine-tuned but emerges entropically from the vast number of microscopic degrees of freedom associated with the de Sitter horizon, providing a robust, symmetry-based explanation within conformal and emergent gravity paradigms.

Abstract: We study the role of Weyl symmetry breaking in conformal gravity and the residual scale symmetry of Einstein gravity. The corresponding action is characterized by a dimensionless coupling $α$, determined by the ratio between the de Sitter and Planck scales. We show that this quantity admits a natural interpretation as the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy of de Sitter spacetime. Combining ideas from the functional renormalization group, holography, and emergent gravity, we propose a microscopic interpretation of $α$ as a measure of the degrees of freedom associated with the de Sitter horizon. In this framework, the renormalization group flow of $α(k)$ encodes the scale dependence of these microscopic degrees of freedom. Requiring this flow to be monotonically increasing toward the infrared leads to a cosmological constant of the same order as the observed one, suggesting an entropic solution to the old cosmological constant problem. This remarkably small value can therefore be understood as a direct consequence of the extraordinarily large number of degrees of freedom in our de Sitter universe.

</details>


### [103] [One-loop contributions in Lorentz-violating scalar QED with nonminimal coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23523)
*F. M. Belchior,J. R. Nascimento,A. Yu. Petrov,P. Porfirio*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes one-loop divergences in a Lorentz-violating (LV) extension of scalar QED with a dimension-five CPT-odd nonminimal coupling, confirming gauge invariance and identifying LV counterterms using dimensional regularization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate perturbative renormalizability and gauge invariance preservation in Lorentz-violating scalar QED with a CPT-odd dimension-five nonminimal coupling to the dual electromagnetic field strength.

Method: Derives vertices from a generalized covariant derivative involving a constant background vector; computes divergent parts of photon vacuum polarization and scalar self-energy at one-loop using dimensional regularization; verifies transversality and cancellation among bubble and seagull diagrams.

Result: The full photon two-point function is transverse; mass-dependent contributions cancel between mixed/nonminimal bubbles and seagull diagrams; resulting LV counterterms include higher-derivative CPT-odd and CPT-even gauge terms and LV kinetic/higher-derivative scalar terms.

Conclusion: Gauge invariance is preserved at one-loop; the nonminimal interaction is consistent within an effective-field-theory framework, and the model generates well-defined Lorentz-violating counterterms.

Abstract: In the context of pertubative aspects of Lorentz-violating theories, we study the divergent one-loop two-point functions of a Lorentz-violating (LV) extension of scalar QED containing a dimension-five CPT-odd nonminimal coupling. The model is defined through a generalized covariant derivative involving a constant background vector and the dual electromagnetic field strength. After expanding the action, we derive the full set of vertices relevant for the photon and scalar self-energies, including the mixed minimal-nonminimal seagull interaction diagram required by the gauge invariance. Using dimensional regularization, we compute the divergent parts of the vacuum polarization and scalar self-energy. The complete photon two-point function is shown to be transverse since the mass-dependent pieces generated by the mixed and purely nonminimal bubble graphs cancel against the corresponding seagull graphs, leaving higher-derivative CPT-odd and CPT-even LV gauge counterterms. The scalar self-energy generates LV kinetic and higher-derivative counterterms. The calculation confirms that gauge invariance is preserved, while the nonminimal interaction should be understood within an effective-field-theory expansion.

</details>


### [104] [Semiclassical decay of de Sitter space into black holes with vortex-deformed horizons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23624)
*Andrés Gomberoff,Carla Henríquez-Baez,Aldo Vera*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies de Sitter space decay into black holes dressed by BPS vortices in a CP^1 field theory, mediated by a vortex-deformed Nariai-type Euclidean instanton with geometry S^2 × Σ; decay rates are quantized by a topological charge, revealing a new topologically structured family of vacuum decay channels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how matter fields—specifically BPS vortices in a CP^1 sigma model—affect the non-perturbative stability and vacuum decay of de Sitter space, going beyond pure gravity instantons.

Method: Constructs a regular Euclidean instanton generalizing the Nariai solution by incorporating CP^1 BPS vortices; analyzes its geometry (S^2 × Σ, where Σ encodes vortex data) and computes decay rates via instanton action, leveraging topological properties of the vortex configuration.

Result: The decay rate is determined by the instanton action, which depends on a discrete topological charge associated with the vortices; the Euclidean geometry remains regular and factorized, confirming viability of the decay channel.

Conclusion: BPS vortices induce a topologically quantized family of de Sitter decay channels, demonstrating that matter-field topology can fundamentally organize gravitational vacuum decay beyond classical geometric considerations.

Abstract: We study the decay of de Sitter space into black holes whose horizons are dressed by BPS vortices of a $\mathrm{CP}^1$ action. The process is mediated by a regular Euclidean instanton obtained as a vortex-deformed generalization of the Nariai instanton. Its Euclidean geometry has the form $S^2\timesΣ$, where $Σ$ is a compact surface whose geometry is shaped by the vortex configuration. The resulting decay rates are controlled by a discrete topological charge, showing that matter vortices open a new topologically organized family of decay channels for de Sitter space.

</details>
