# hep-lat [[Back]](#toc)

### [105] [Neural Wavefunctions in Quantum Field Theory I: Asymptotic Freedom](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20791)
*Paulo F. Bedaque,Hersh Kumar,Suryansh Rajawat,Gregory Ridgway*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: Introduces a neural-network-based variational method for quantum field theory, successfully reproducing key features (asymptotic freedom, dynamical mass generation, step-scaling) of the 2D nonlinear σ-model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Overcome longstanding challenges in applying variational methods to quantum field theory by leveraging expressive, trainable neural-network wavefunctions.

Method: Constructs neural-network-parameterized wavefunctions and optimizes them variationally using modern machine-learning techniques (e.g., stochastic reconfiguration, automatic differentiation) to approximate ground-state properties of quantum field theories.

Result: Achieves quantitatively accurate reproduction of asymptotic freedom, dynamical mass generation, and the step-scaling function in the two-dimensional nonlinear σ-model — a nontrivial, asymptotically free QFT.

Conclusion: Neural-network variational wavefunctions constitute a viable, scalable, and competitive framework for nonperturbative calculations in quantum field theory, opening new avenues for studying strongly coupled and continuum field theories.

Abstract: We present a variational approach to quantum field theory based on wavefunctions parameterized by neural networks. While variational methods have a celebrated history across many fields, their application to quantum field theory has been limited by well-known challenges. We show that neural-network wavefunctions, combined with modern machine-learning techniques, enable competitive variational calculations in nontrivial field theories. As a demonstration, we reproduce the essential features of the two-dimensional nonlinear $σ$-model: asymptotic freedom, dynamical mass generation and the model's step-scaling function.

</details>


### [106] [Event-Chain Monte Carlo for Yang-Mills SU(N) lattice field theory I : Design and proof of concept](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21217)
*Benoît Blossier,Manon Michel,Yacob Ozdalkiran*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: Introduces two Event-Chain Monte Carlo (ECMC) implementations for SU(N) Yang-Mills lattice gauge theories with Wilson action; rejection-free, irreversible, globally balanced; validated numerically for SU(3) in 4D.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of conventional Markov Chain Monte Carlo (e.g., critical slowing down, rejection) in sampling Yang-Mills lattice gauge theories by leveraging rejection-free, irreversible dynamics via ECMC.

Method: Develops two ECMC algorithms for SU(N) lattice gauge theories with Wilson action—local ballistic updates interleaved with stochastic events—ensuring global balance and correct equilibrium sampling; implemented specifically for SU(3) on four-dimensional lattices.

Result: Numerical tests confirm agreement of standard observables (e.g., mean plaquette) between ECMC and conventional Monte Carlo methods, validating ECMC as a viable sampling scheme for Yang-Mills theories.

Conclusion: ECMC is a promising, rejection-free alternative to conventional MCMC for lattice gauge theory simulations, with demonstrated correctness and feasibility for SU(3) Yang-Mills in four dimensions.

Abstract: We develop two implementations of the Event-Chain Monte Carlo (ECMC) algorithm for Yang-Mills $\mathrm{SU}(N)$ lattice gauge theories with the Wilson action. These algorithms consist in a succession of local ballistic updates intersped with stochastic events, resulting in an irreversible and rejection-free Markov process. The resulting dynamics satisfy global balance, ensuring the correct equilibrium distribution. The algorithms are formulated for general $\mathrm{SU}(N)$ Yang-Mills theories with Wilson action and implemented for the case $N=3$. Numerical tests on four-dimensional lattices show that standard gauge observables, such as the mean plaquette, agree with results obtained using conventional Monte Carlo algorithms. These results provide a first validation of ECMC as a viable sampling scheme for Yang-Mills lattice gauge theories.

</details>


### [107] [SU(2) gauge theory with fermions on a semi-simple cubic lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21563)
*Randy Lewis,Shidsa Pourbakhsh,Arnab Pradhan,Lance Siquioco*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: Proposes a semi-simple cubic (ssc) lattice for Hamiltonian-based quantum simulation of 3D non-Abelian lattice gauge theories, reducing qubit requirements while preserving locality and enabling staggered fermions with SU(2) gauge fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable practical Hamiltonian simulation of lattice gauge theories—especially for phenomenologically relevant 3D systems—on near-term quantum computers by reducing qubit overhead and simplifying Gauss's law enforcement.

Method: Designs and analyzes the semi-simple cubic (ssc) lattice: a trivalent 3D lattice derived from a cubic lattice by removing half the gauge links; proves its topological equivalence to the triamond lattice; constructs local fermion derivatives and implements staggered fermions coupled to SU(2) gauge fields while respecting gauge invariance and Gauss's law.

Result: The ssc lattice achieves qubit efficiency (fewer qubits than cubic lattice), maintains full 3D spatial locality, supports local fermionic kinetic terms, and permits exact implementation of Gauss's law constraints—demonstrated explicitly for SU(2) staggered fermions.

Conclusion: The ssc lattice is a viable, resource-efficient architecture for Hamiltonian quantum simulation of 3D non-Abelian gauge theories, bridging theoretical design and near-term hardware constraints.

Abstract: A practical Hamiltonian approach to lattice gauge theories would provide access to several important areas of phenomenology that have been beyond the reach of conventional lattice methods. Quantum computers seem to be a natural platform for this approach. With near-term quantum computers in mind, our work considers a three-dimensional spatial lattice that can host fermions and non-Abelian gauge fields while needing fewer qubits than a simple cubic lattice. Specifically, the semi-simple cubic (ssc) lattice is obtained by removing half of the gauge links from a standard cubic lattice in such a way that every vertex becomes trivalent, which streamlines the handling of Gauss's law. The ssc lattice is topologically equivalent to the triamond lattice but, because the gauge links at each vertex span all three directions, the ssc lattice can accommodate a local fermion derivative. The case of staggered fermions with SU(2) gauge fields is presented here.

</details>


### [108] [Proton's isovector PDF with updated analysis of large-momentum lattice data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22224)
*Xiangdong Ji,Yushan Su*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper reanalyzes lattice QCD calculations of the proton's u(x)−d(x) PDF using state-of-the-art theoretical tools and empirical artifact mitigation, finding consistency with global experimental fits within ~1σ—supporting the viability of large-momentum effective theory for PDF extraction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies between prior lattice QCD determinations of u(x)−d(x) and experimental PDFs, arising from lattice artifacts (excited-state contamination, unphysical pion masses, discretization) and incomplete theoretical treatment (renormalization, large-distance extrapolation, large-log resummation).

Method: Reanalysis of existing lattice QCD datasets using modern theoretical frameworks: proper non-perturbative renormalization (e.g., RI/MOM or matching to MS-bar), controlled large-distance extrapolation, all-order large-log resummation (via Collins–Soper evolution or equivalent), and empirical removal of excited-state and finite-pion-mass effects.

Result: The corrected u(x)−d(x) PDF in the physical limit (physical pion mass, continuum, infinite volume, ground-state dominated) agrees with global experimental fits (e.g., CTEQ, NNPDF, MMHT) within approximately 1 standard deviation across the measured x range.

Conclusion: Large-momentum effective theory (LaMET) is theoretically sound and quantitatively predictive for nucleon PDFs; observed prior discrepancies were largely due to controllable systematic effects—not fundamental limitations—suggesting that high-precision, ab initio PDF determination from lattice QCD is achievable with improved data.

Abstract: The proton's unpolarized $u(x)-d(x)$ parton distribution function (PDF) has been studied by a number of lattice QCD groups through large momentum expansion. However, due to lattice artifacts (excited state contaminations, unphysical pion masses, and discretization effects) and less-advanced theoretical analysis (renormalizations, large-distance extrapolations, and large-log resummations), the resulting PDFs cannot be compared strictly with experimental data. By using the state-of-the-art theoretical tools and mitigating the lattice artifacts empirically, we reanalyze the available datasets in the literature and find that the new PDF in the physical limits is consistent with global fittings within $\sim1σ$. This provides compelling evidence that large momentum expansion is capable of accurately predicting the $x$-dependence of the PDFs when ideal lattice data become available.

</details>


### [109] [Multi-particle states investigation with tensor renormalization group method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22321)
*Fathiyya Izzatun Az-zahra,Shinji Takeda,Takeshi Yamazaki*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper develops a tensor network-based spectroscopy method to study multi-particle states in the 1+1D Ising model, identifying one-, two-, and three-particle states via system-size scaling, and computes scattering phase shifts using Lüscher’s formula and wave function methods—showing consistency with theory and enabling analysis of finite-volume multi-particle degeneracies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically access and characterize multi-particle excitations in integrable and near-integrable quantum field theories using tensor network methods, overcoming limitations of conventional diagonalization and enabling extraction of scattering data from finite-volume spectra.

Method: Combines transfer-matrix construction with tensor renormalization group (TRG) for coarse-grained spectral estimation; uses impurity tensor networks to compute matrix elements of interpolating operators for quantum number/momentum identification; employs finite-size scaling to distinguish particle multiplicity; applies Lüscher's formula and wave function reconstruction to extract two-particle scattering phase shifts; extends analysis to three-particle state degeneracies and finite-volume energy predictions.

Result: Successfully isolates and classifies one-, two-, and three-particle eigenstates in the Ising model; quantitatively verifies two-particle scattering phase shifts against exact theoretical predictions; confirms expected degeneracy patterns in two- and three-particle sectors; validates finite-volume energy formulae for three-particle states.

Conclusion: The TRG-based spectroscopy framework provides a robust, scalable, and quantum-number-resolved approach for extracting scattering data and multi-particle structure directly from lattice path integrals, bridging tensor network methods with continuum S-matrix physics in low-dimensional QFTs.

Abstract: We investigate multi-particle states of the (1+1)d Ising Model using a spectroscopy scheme based on transfer matrix and tensor renormalization group method. The scheme begins with computing the energy spectrum of the system from the transfer matrix estimated by the coarse-grained tensor network. The quantum number and momentum of these energy eigenstates are not a priori known, thus we identify them using matrix elements of an interpolating operator that is numerically computed with an impurity tensor network. Furthermore, by observing the dependence of the energy as a function of system size, we identify the number of particles of the eigenstates and obtain one-, two-, and three-particle states for a specific quantum number and momentum. From the two-particle state sector, we compute the scattering phase shift using Lüscher's formula and wave function approach, and observe their consistency with theoretical prediction. Using the information of the two-particle scattering phase shift, we investigate the degeneracy of the two-particle states, the theoretical prediction of the three-particle finite volume energy and also the degeneracy in the three-particle states.

</details>

### [110] [Charged kaon and proton multiplicities in semi-inclusive deep-inelastic scattering with 11 GeV electrons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20745)
*P. Bosted,W. Armstrong,H. Bhatt,D. Dutta,R. Ent,D. Gaskell,S. Jia,E. Kinney,H. Mkrtchyan,S. Ali,R. Ambrose,D. Androic,C. Ayerbe Gayoso,A. Bandari,V. Berdnikov,D. Bhetuwal,D. Biswas,M. Boer,E. Brash,A. Camsonne,M. Cardona,J. P. Chen,J. Chen,M. Chen,E. M. Christy,S. Covrig,S. Danagoulian,M. Diefenthaler,B. Duran,C. Elliot,H. Fenker,E. Fuchey,J. O. Hansen,F. Hauenstein,T. Horn,G. M. Huber,M. K. Jones,M. L. Kabir,A. Karki,B. Karki,S. J. D. Kay,C. Keppel,V. Kumar,N. Lashley-Colthirst,W. B. Li,D. Mack,S. Malace,P. Markowitz,M. McCaughan,E. McClellan,D. Meekins,R. Michaels,A. Mkrtchyan,C. Morean,G. Niculescu,I. Niculescu,B. Pandey,S. Park,E. Pooser,B. Sawatzky,G. R. Smith,H. Szumila-Vance,A. S. Tadepalli,V. Tadevosyan,R. Trotta,H. Voskanyan,S. A. Wood,Z. Ye,C. Yerom,X. Zheng*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports semi-inclusive deep inelastic scattering (SIDIS) multiplicities for charged kaons and protons from proton and deuteron targets, measured at Jefferson Lab Hall C. It analyzes azimuthal modulations and multiplicity ratios relative to pions, comparing results with TMD-based and Lund Monte Carlo predictions—finding good agreement for K⁺ but discrepancies for K⁻, and large proton-to-pion ratios inconsistent with TMD expectations in the soft region.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD) factorization validity and hadronization dynamics in SIDIS, particularly in kinematic regions where TMD formalism applies versus the 'soft' central region dominated by non-perturbative effects; also to test fragmentation function models (e.g., DSS) and Monte Carlo simulations (e.g., Lund) via kaon and proton multiplicities and azimuthal asymmetries.

Method: SIDIS measurements using 10.2–10.6 GeV electron beams on liquid H₂ and D₂ targets at Jefferson Lab Hall C (2018–2019); detection of scattered electrons in HMS and produced hadrons in SHMS; extraction of multiplicities M₀, A (cos φ*), and B (cos 2φ*) via fits in (x, Q², z, Pₜ) bins; comparison with DSS fragmentation functions and Lund Monte Carlo predictions.

Result: Kaon M₀ ratios to pions agree with DSS for K⁺ but fall significantly below for K⁻; kaon azimuthal modulations (A, B) are consistent with zero. Proton M₀ ratios exceed TMD predictions by >10× at low W², decreasing to ~2× at high W²; no target dependence (p vs. d); proton A is positive (~0.01), B ≈ 0 — consistent with Lund MC.

Conclusion: The data validate TMD applicability for K⁺ in the designated kinematic domain but expose limitations for K⁻ and especially protons in the soft region, suggesting dominant non-TMD hadronization mechanisms; consistency with Lund MC supports string-fragmentation dominance there, while null kaon azimuthal modulations imply negligible transverse spin or TMD-related asymmetries under these conditions.

Abstract: Measurements of SIDIS multiplicities for charged kaons and protons from proton and deuteron targets are reported on a grid of hadron kinematic variables $0.3<z<0.7$ and $P_{t}<0.6$ GeV for leptonic variables $0.3<x<0.6$ and $3<Q^2<6$ GeV$^2$. Data were acquired in 2018-2019 at Jefferson Lab Hall C with 10.2 and 10.6~GeV electron beams impinging on 10-cm-long liquid hydrogen and deuterium targets. Electrons (hadrons) were detected in the HMS (SHMS) spectrometers. Multiplicities were fitted for each bin in $(x,~Q^2,~z,~P_{t})$ as $M_0[1+A\cos(φ^*)+B\cos(2φ^*)]$. The kaon kinematic range spans the regions where transverse-momentum-dependent factorization can be applied in SIDIS, and a `soft' central region where other processes are of critical importance. The kaon to pion ratios of $M_0$ are in reasonable agreement with predictions using the DSS fragmentation functions for $K^+$, but are mostly well below them for $K^-$. The kaon azimuthal modulations are consistent with zero. The kinematic range for protons is centered on the `soft' central region. The proton-to-pion multiplicity ratios are more than an order-of-magnitude larger than TMD predictions at the lowest value of $W^2$, decreasing to as little as a factor of two at the highest value of $W^2$. No significant difference is observed between proton and deuteron targets. These trends are consistent with Lund Monte Carlo predictions. The proton values of $A$ are consistently positive, with an average value of approximately 0.01, while $B$ is consistent with zero.

</details>

Yin,K. Yoshihara,C. Z. Yuan,J. Yuan,L. Yuan,Y. Yusa,L. Zani,F. Zeng,M. Zeyrek,B. Zhang,X. Zhao,V. Zhilich,J. S. Zhou,Q. D. Zhou,X. Y. Zhou,L. Zhu,R. Žlebčík*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First measurement of mixing-induced CP violation in B⁰→π⁰π⁰ decays using quantum-entangled B⁰B̄⁰ pairs at Belle II; achieves high precision with 190M events—20× more efficient than conventional methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the Standard Model and probe new physics via CP violation in quark transitions, specifically in the challenging B⁰→π⁰π⁰ channel where prior measurements were limited by low statistics and reconstruction difficulties.

Method: Exploits quantum entanglement of B⁰B̄⁰ pairs produced in Υ(4S) decays to perform a decay-time-dependent CP analysis without reconstructing the π⁰π⁰ decay vertex; uses 190 million Υ(4S)→B⁰B̄⁰ events from Belle II at SuperKEKB.

Result: S_π⁰π⁰ = 0.61⁺⁰·⁷⁵₋₀·₇₉ (stat) ± 0.11 (syst); C_π⁰π⁰ = 0.05 ± 0.28 (stat) ± 0.07 (syst); precision equivalent to ~3.8 billion events in conventional analysis.

Conclusion: Demonstrates quantum-entanglement–based analysis as a powerful tool for CP violation studies in hadronic B decays; significantly tightens constraints on the CKM angle φ₂ with substantially reduced data requirements.

Abstract: Measurements of charge-parity (CP) violation in quark transitions provide stringent tests of the current theory and sensitive probes of particles or interactions beyond it. We report the first measurement of mixing-induced CP violation in $B^0\toπ^0π^0$ decays. The measurement uses 190 million $Υ(4S)\to B^{0}\overline{B}{}^0$ events collected with the Belle II experiment at the SuperKEKB asymmetric-energy electron-positron collider. A new approach that exploits the quantum entanglement of the $B^{0}\overline{B}{}^0$ pair enables a decay-time-dependent analysis without reconstructing the $B^0 \to π^0π^0$ decay position. We measure the mixing-induced CP-violating coefficient to be $S_{π^{0}π^{0}} = 0.61^{+0.75}_{-0.79}\,(\mathrm{stat}) \pm 0.11\,(\mathrm{syst})$, attaining a precision that would require a data set twenty times as large with a conventional analysis. We also measure the direct CP-violating coefficient to be $C_{π^{0}π^{0}} = 0.05 \pm 0.28\,(\mathrm{stat}) \pm 0.07\,(\mathrm{syst})$. These results substantially improve the constraints from $B \to ππ$ decays on the CP-violating phase $φ_2$ of the quark-mixing matrix using far less data than had previously been assumed necessary.

</details>


### [112] [Analysis note: Long-range near-side correlation in $e^+e^-$ with $W$-boson-pair events at 183-209 GeV with ALEPH archived data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21094)
*Tzu-An Sheng,Yu-Chen Chen,Yi Chen,Kyle Sun,Jason Chiu,Michael Peters,Marcello Maggi,Austin Baty,Anthony Badea,Chris McGinn,Jesse Thaler,Gian Michele Innocenti,Jingyu Zhang,Paoti Chang,Yen-Jie Lee*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper investigates long-range two-particle angular correlations in high-multiplicity e+e− collisions at √s = 183–209 GeV (LEP2/ALEPH), testing whether observed ridge-like structures and sign-reversing v₂-like signals stem from W⁺W⁻-induced color-string complexity or merely from higher multiplicity due to increased energy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguity in prior LEP2 results: whether anomalous long-range near-side correlations at high multiplicity arise from intrinsic QCD dynamics of W⁺W⁻ production (e.g., complex color-string topologies) or are merely kinematic/statistical artifacts of higher event multiplicity.

Method: Analysis of ALEPH LEP2 data; measurement of two-particle angular correlation functions in pseudorapidity–azimuth space, binned by charged-particle multiplicity; Fourier decomposition to extract harmonic coefficients (vₙ proxies); comparison against archived Monte Carlo simulations (e.g., JETSET, HERWIG) with and without W⁺W⁻ contributions.

Result: A ridge-like modulation appears for multiplicity >50, deviating from MC expectations; signed v₂-like proxy transitions from negative to positive starting at multiplicity ~30—also inconsistent with MC baseline—suggesting collective-like or non-trivial azimuthal anisotropy beyond standard fragmentation models.

Conclusion: The observed deviations—especially the multiplicity-dependent sign change in v₂-like signal—support the hypothesis that W⁺W⁻ production introduces qualitatively new color-field configurations (e.g., overlapping strings, Bose–Einstein correlations, or local flow-like effects), challenging purely perturbative or independent-fragmentation interpretations.

Abstract: Events characterized by a high multiplicity of charged particles have been a central focus in the study of collective behavior across both large and small collision systems. A previous measurement of two-particle angular correlations in $e^+e^-$ collisions at center-of-mass energies up to $\sqrt{s} = 209$ GeV, using LEP2 data, revealed discrepancies with Monte Carlo (MC) predictions at high multiplicity, suggesting the possible emergence of long-range near-side correlations even in the simplest collision system. Unlike at lower energies, where quark-antiquark production dominates, $W^+W^-$ processes become increasingly important at multiplicities above 30. On the one hand, the observed excess in long-range correlations may reflect the more complex color-string configurations arising from $W^+W^-$ production. On the other hand, it can simply arise from the higher final-state multiplicity made possible by the increased collision energy, independent of the underlying production mechanism.
  To discriminate between these competing interpretations, we present a measurement of two-particle angular correlations in $e^+e^-$ collisions at $\sqrt{s} = 183-209$ GeV, with a focus on enhancing the contribution from $W^+W^-$ processes. The analysis uses data collected by the ALEPH detector during the LEP2 program. Correlation functions are evaluated across a broad range of pseudorapidities and full azimuth, in bins of charged-particle multiplicity. A ridge-like modulation is seen for multiplicity above 50, deviating from the MC reference. In addition, the correlation functions are further decomposed into a Fourier series, and the resulting harmonic coefficients $v_n$ are compared with predictions from the archived Monte Carlo sample. For multiplicity starting from 30, the signed $v_2$-like proxy goes from negative to positive, also deviating from the MC baseline.

</details>


### [113] [Towards LLM-Powered Automation of a Dark Matter Constraint Repository](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21658)
*Lanqing Yuan,Karthik Ramanathan*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces an LLM-based pipeline to automate extraction and integration of dark matter experimental limits from arXiv papers into community repositories, achieving high accuracy in coupling-type classification (90.5%) and reasonable fidelity in curve reconstruction (median residual 0.33 dex), while highlighting governance challenges of AI-generated scientific data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address sustainability risks in volunteer-maintained dark matter constraint repositories amid accelerating publication rates, by automating limit curve extraction and integration.

Method: An LLM pipeline that monitors arXiv, extracts limit curves via consensus voting over noisy extractions, applies physics convention canonicalization using the agentic assistant Get Physics Done (GPD), and evaluates extraction quality with a scoring method distinguishing true error from non-comparability.

Result: On a 346-paper benchmark, the pipeline achieves 90.5% coupling-type classification accuracy, median coupling residual of 0.33 dex (factor-of-two agreement for 48% of curves), and 76% mean mass-range coverage; performance degrades for rare coupling types (macro-averaged residual 1.1 dex). It is deployed and generates PRs, none yet merged.

Conclusion: The pipeline demonstrates feasibility of AI-assisted curation of dark matter limits but underscores unresolved governance issues around AI-generated scientific data integration.

Abstract: Dark matter constraint repositories are critical community infrastructure, giving experimentalists and theorists a shared landscape of existing bounds. Yet the most widely-used repositories are maintained by individual volunteers, creating a sustainability risk as the pace of new results accelerates. We present a large language model (LLM) pipeline that monitors arXiv, extracts limit curves from papers, integrates them as code, and opens pull requests (PRs) for human review. On a 346-paper benchmark whose ground truth is the upstream-curated repository itself, the pipeline classifies the coupling type correctly for 90.5% of papers and reaches a median coupling residual of 0.33 dex (a factor of two for 48% of curves), with 76% mean mass-range coverage. This is driven by treating each extraction as a noisy sample reconciled through consensus voting, a physics convention canonicalization layer built with the agentic physics assistant Get Physics Done (GPD), and a scoring methodology that separates genuine extraction error from non-comparability. The remaining difficulty is concentrated in rare coupling types with idiosyncratic conventions (macro-averaged residual 1.1 dex). The pipeline is deployed and has generated limit proposals; none have merged. Governance of AI-generated scientific data is itself an unsolved problem.

</details>


### [114] [Accelerating Discovery: Model-Agnostic Likelihoods for the Reinterpretation of Particle Physics Results and their Application to the Belle II $B^{+}\to K^{+}ν\barν$ Measurement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22215)
*Lorenz Gärtner*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This thesis introduces a computationally efficient, statistically rigorous reinterpretation method for high-energy physics results, enabling rapid inference on alternative theoretical models—such as Wilson coefficients in Weak Effective Theory or light new physics in B→KX decays—while preserving kinematic correlations and systematic uncertainties; validated on Belle II's B⁺→K⁺νν̄ anomaly and released as public, model-agnostic likelihoods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The growing number of theoretical models outpaces experimental validation capacity; existing reinterpretation methods either demand prohibitive computational resources (full reanalysis) or sacrifice accuracy (simplified approaches neglecting kinematics and systematics).

Method: A likelihood reweighting method that transforms the joint distribution of reconstructed and kinematic variables using the ratio of theoretical predictions (alternative vs. null hypothesis); starts from histogram-based likelihoods to generate updated templates while retaining full correlation structure and systematic uncertainty treatment.

Result: Successful validation on simulated and real analyses; applied to Belle II's B⁺→K⁺νν̄ measurement (2.7σ SM tension), yielding constraints on Wilson coefficients (Weak Effective Theory) and exclusion limits on light new physics via B⁺→K⁺X; public release of model-agnostic likelihoods.

Conclusion: The method bridges theory–experiment latency by enabling fast, accurate, and statistically sound reinterpretation without requiring detector-level expertise or reprocessing; it advances FAIR data principles and democratizes access to collider data.

Abstract: Experimental results in high-energy physics are inherently hypothesis-dependent, as collider analyses are designed to test specific theoretical frameworks. The proliferation of theoretical models creates a mismatch between the pace of theory development and experimental validation, motivating the reinterpretation of existing results in terms of alternative hypotheses. Existing strategies face limitations: full reanalysis requires enormous computational resources and experimental expertise, while simplified methods sacrifice accuracy by neglecting kinematic variations and systematic uncertainties.
  This thesis develops, validates, and applies a novel reinterpretation method that preserves statistical rigour while dramatically reducing computational requirements, enabling rapid inference on alternative theoretical parameters through distributable likelihoods.
  The method reweights the joint distribution of reconstruction and kinematic variables according to the ratio of theoretical predictions of alternative to null hypotheses. Starting from a histogram-based likelihood, this produces updated templates for any theoretical model while preserving correlations and systematic uncertainties. The method is validated through examples and applied to the Belle II $B^+ \to K^+ ν\barν$ measurement ($2.7σ$ tension with the Standard Model). Two analyses are presented: a Weak Effective Theory reinterpretation constraining Wilson coefficients, and a light new physics search via ${B^+ \to K^+ X}$ two-body decays.
  The public release of the resulting model-agnostic likelihoods establishes an important precedent for open science in experimental particle physics. By facilitating rapid theoretical validation, this approach democratizes experimental data access, enhances scientific return on investments, and advances FAIR Data principles in high-energy physics.

</details>

X. Zhou,Y. Z. Zhou,A. N. Zhu,J. Zhu,K. Zhu,K. J. Zhu,K. S. Zhu,L. X. Zhu,S. H. Zhu,T. J. Zhu,W. D. Zhu,W. J. Zhu,W. Z. Zhu,Y. C. Zhu,Z. A. Zhu,X. Y. Zhuang,M. Zhuge,J. H. Zou,J. Zu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Search for lepton flavor violating decay η→e±μ∓ using J/ψ→γη' data; no signal found; new upper limit B < 6.8×10⁻⁷ (90% CL), improving prior limit by factor of ~10.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Test lepton flavor universality and probe physics beyond the Standard Model via charged lepton flavor violation in η meson decay.

Method: Analyzed (10.087 ± 0.044) × 10⁹ J/ψ events at √s = 3.097 GeV collected with BESIII detector; reconstructed η via radiative J/ψ decay chain J/ψ → γη′, η′ → π⁺π⁻η, followed by η → e±μ∓; employed background suppression and statistical limit setting (CLs method).

Result: No significant signal observed; upper limit on branching fraction ℬ(η → e±μ∓) < 6.8 × 10⁻⁷ at 90% confidence level.

Conclusion: The stringent new limit constrains new physics models predicting charged lepton flavor violation at the η-meson scale, notably supersymmetry or extended Higgs sectors.

Abstract: Based on $(10.087 \pm 0.044) \times 10^{9}~J/ψ$ events collected at the center-of-mass energy $\sqrt{s} = 3.097$~GeV with the BESIII detector, we search for the charged lepton flavor violating decay $η\to e^{\pm}μ^{\mp}$ through the process $J/ψ\to γη'$ with $η' \to π^{+} π^{-} η$. No signal is observed, and an upper limit on the branching fraction is determined to be $\mathcal{B}(η\to e^{\pm}μ^{\mp}) < 6.8 \times 10^{-7}$ at the 90\% confidence level. This result improves the previous best limit by one order of magnitude.

</details>


### [116] [Searches for resonant and nonresonant multi-Higgs production in ATLAS and CMS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22461)
*Emmanouil Vourliotis*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent ATLAS and CMS results on multi-Higgs production at the LHC, targeting Higgs self-couplings (trilinear and quartic) and resonant BSM scalar decays to Higgs pairs or mixed Higgs–scalar final states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the shape of the Higgs potential and search for physics beyond the Standard Model via Higgs self-interactions and resonant heavy scalar decays.

Method: Analysis of multi-Higgs production (nonresonant HH, HHH, and resonant X→HH or X→H+Y) using data from ATLAS and CMS experiments at the LHC, across multiple decay channels and final states.

Result: Latest experimental constraints and sensitivity limits on Higgs trilinear and quartic couplings, as well as on masses and cross sections of potential resonant BSM scalars decaying to Higgs bosons.

Conclusion: Current LHC data provide increasingly stringent tests of the Higgs potential and BSM scenarios; improved statistics and analysis techniques are pushing toward first evidence or measurement of the trilinear coupling.

Abstract: The measurement of the Higgs boson self-coupling is one of the key physics goals of the LHC program, providing direct access to the shape of the Higgs potential and offering sensitivity to physics beyond the Standard Model (BSM). Higgs pair production probes the trilinear self-coupling, while triple Higgs production is sensitive to the quartic coupling. In addition, resonant searches for heavy scalars decaying to pairs of Higgs bosons or to a Higgs boson and a new scalar are motivated by a broad class of BSM models. Recent results on multi-Higgs production searches from the ATLAS and CMS experiments at the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) are presented, covering both resonant and nonresonant production modes across a variety of final states.

</details>

Zhuge,J. H. Zou,J. Zu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports observations of charmonium(-like) states decaying to $p\bar{p}$ using $e^+e^- \to p\bar{p}$ data at BESIII, including a significant observation of $\psi(3770) \to p\bar{p}$ and a new structure near 4.2 GeV; it also extracts high-precision proton electromagnetic form factors $|G_E/G_M|$ and $|G_M|$ from angular distributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To search for charmonium(-like) states decaying to $p\bar{p}$ and to precisely determine proton electromagnetic form factors in the time-like region, probing both hadronic decay dynamics and nucleon substructure.

Method: Analysis of $e^+e^- \to p\bar{p}$ cross sections and proton angular distributions using 26 fb$^{-1}$ of data collected by BESIII at BEPCII across 47 center-of-mass energies (3.510–4.946 GeV); statistical significance assessment with systematic uncertainties; extraction of $|G_E/G_M|$ and $|G_M|$ via fit to polar angle distribution.

Result: $\psi(3770) \to p\bar{p}$ observed at 6.6$\sigma$; a structure near 4.2 GeV observed at 4.6$\sigma$ ($\psi(4160)$ hypothesis) or 4.8$\sigma$ ($Y(4230)$ hypothesis); high-precision measurements of $|G_E/G_M|$ and $|G_M|$ at large time-like $q^2$.

Conclusion: The results provide new evidence for charmonium(-like) decays to baryon-antibaryon final states and significantly improve knowledge of proton electromagnetic form factors in the time-like region, constraining theoretical models of QCD dynamics and nucleon structure.

Abstract: We search for possible charmonium(-like) states decaying into the $p\bar{p}$ final state by studying the Born cross sections of the $e^+e^-\to p\bar{p}$ reaction, and we determine the proton electromagnetic form factors by analyzing the proton angular distribution. The measurement is performed using a sample of $e^+e^-$ collision data collected at 47 center-of-mass energies from 3.510 to 4.946 GeV, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 26 fb$^{-1}$, recorded by the BESIII detector collected at the BEPCII collider. The decay $ψ(3770)\to p\bar{p}$ is observed with a significance of 6.6$σ$ including systematic uncertainties. Furthermore, a structure near 4.2 GeV is observed with significances of $4.6σ$ or $4.8σ$ for the $ψ(4160)$ or $Y(4230)$ hypotheses including systematic uncertainties, respectively; these interpretations cannot presently be distinguished. In addition, the moduli of the form factor ratios $|G_{E}/G_{M}|$ and of the magnetic form factors $|G_{M}|$ are extracted by analyzing the proton polar angle distribution with higher precision at large time-like squared momentum transfer. These results provide important experimental insights into both the decay mechanisms of charmonium(-like) states in the open charm region and the internal structure of proton.

</details>


### [118] [Measurement of Born cross sections for $e^+e^-\to p\bar p$ at $\sqrt{s} =3.510-4.946$ GeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22762)

K. Zhou,X. R. Zhou,X. Y. Zhou,Y. X. Zhou,Y. Z. Zhou,A. N. Zhu,J. Zhu,K. Zhu,K. J. Zhu,K. S. Zhu,L. X. Zhu,Lin Zhu,S. H. Zhu,T. J. Zhu,W. D. Zhu,W. J. Zhu,W. Z. Zhu,Y. C. Zhu,Z. A. Zhu,X. Y. Zhuang,M. Zhuge,J. H. Zou,J. Zu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports high-precision measurements of the Born cross section and effective electromagnetic form factors for e+e− → p̄p across 47 energy points (3.510–4.946 GeV) using BESIII data (26 fb⁻¹), enabling first-time precise determinations of |GE/GM| and |GM| via proton polar-angle distributions at large timelike momentum transfer.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the nature of charmonium(-like) states above the open-charm threshold and elucidate the dynamics of proton electromagnetic form factors in the timelike region.

Method: Energy-scan technique with the BESIII detector at BEPCII; analysis of proton polar-angle distributions to extract |GE/GM| and |GM|; measurement of Born cross section and effective form factor from 26 fb⁻¹ integrated luminosity.

Result: High-precision determinations of |GE/GM| and |GM|; precise Born cross section and effective form factor values across 47 center-of-mass energies (3.510–4.946 GeV).

Conclusion: The results constrain theoretical models of nucleon structure and charmonium-like resonances, offering new benchmarks for QCD-inspired and phenomenological approaches to timelike nucleon form factors.

Abstract: We report a measurement of the Born cross section and the effective form factor for the $e^+e^-\to p\bar{p}$ reaction at 47 center-of-mass energies between 3.510 and 4.946 GeV. The measurement is performed using the energy-scan technique and is based on data corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 26 fb\(^{-1}\) collected with the BESIII detector at the BEPCII collider. For the first time, the moduli of the electromagnetic form factor ratio $|G_{E}/G_{M}|$ and of the magnetic form factor $|G_{M}|$ are determined with high precision by analyzing the distribution of the polar angle of the proton at a large timelike momentum transfer.
  These results provide essential insights into the nature of charmonium(-like) states above the open-charm threshold and the dynamics underlying the proton electromagnetic form factors.

</details>


### [119] [Ferromagnetic broadband sensing of axionlike dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22814)
*Chenhao Peng,Dmitry Budker,Yuanning Gao,Xinran Li,Jia Liu,Wei Ji,Jing Shu,Zhenxing Tang,Liheng Wang*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents a high-bandwidth levitated magnet magnetometer using a double-resonance mode, hard-magnet array, and soft-ferromagnetic shielding to search for axionlike dark matter via photon coupling (g_aγ) in the 40–3000 Hz range; it achieves 0.7 fT field resolution at 276 Hz, improving prior direct limits by >4 orders of magnitude.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Axionlike particles are well-motivated dark matter candidates; detecting them requires magnetometers with both ultrahigh sensitivity and large bandwidth—challenging for conventional systems.

Method: Engineered double-resonance levitated magnet magnetometer integrated with a hard-magnet array (to enhance axion-induced signal) and soft-ferromagnetic shielding (to suppress environmental magnetic noise).

Result: New direct experimental limits on axion-photon coupling g_aγ over 40–3000 Hz; best sensitivity at ~276 Hz: magnetic-field resolution 0.7 fT → g_aγ ∼ 10⁻⁷ GeV⁻¹, exceeding previous direct limits by >4 orders of magnitude.

Conclusion: The hybrid ferromagnetic levitated platform enables high-bandwidth, high-sensitivity detection of axionlike dark matter and holds promise for applications in biological sensing and precision metrology.

Abstract: Levitated particles have demonstrated ultrahigh sensitivity to magnetic fields and accelerations owing to their extremely low dissipation. Such systems have strong potential for fundamental physics research, particularly for the detection of axions and axionlike particles, well-motivated dark matter candidates spanning a broad mass range. In this context, both high sensitivity and large bandwidth are essential. Here, we demonstrate a levitated magnet magnetometer based on an engineered double-resonance mode, achieving an effective linewidth at its optimal sensitivity that is approximately three orders of magnitude broader than those of previous approaches. Together with a hard-magnet array that enhances the axion-induced signal and soft-ferromagnetic shielding that suppresses environmental magnetic noise, this system constitutes a hybrid ferromagnetic platform for axionlike dark matter searches. We search for axionlike dark matter through its photon coupling $g_{aγ}$ over the $40$-$3000\,\mathrm{Hz}$ frequency range and establish new direct limits in this frequency band. The best sensitivity is achieved near the upper resonance around $276\,\mathrm{Hz}$, where the magnetometer reaches a magnetic-field resolution of $0.7\,\mathrm{fT}$, corresponding to a limit of $g_{aγ}\sim10^{-7}\,\mathrm{GeV}^{-1}$. At this frequency, this result improves upon previous direct limits by more than four orders of magnitude. The demonstrated high-bandwidth levitated sensor may also enable a broad range of applications, including biological sensing and precision measurements.

</details>


### [120] [Observations of Low-Energy-Electron Production and Experimental Characterization of the Test-Mass Charging Process in the LISA Gravitational Reference Sensor with the BART Experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22979)
*Francesco Dimiccoli,Francesco Venturelli,Valerio Ferroni,Antonella Cavalleri,Teodoro Klaser,Matteo Tomasi,Davide Dal Bosco,Enrico Verroi,Rita Dolesi William J. Weber*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports an accelerator-based experiment to test the hypothesis that low-energy secondary electron (LEE) emission drives unexpected test-mass charging noise in LISA's Gravitational Reference Sensor, motivated by discrepancies between LISA Pathfinder measurements and pre-launch simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: LISA Pathfinder observed significantly larger Poissonian test-mass charging noise than predicted by pre-launch models, suggesting missing physics—particularly LEE emission from surfaces—in the electrostatic charging model.

Method: A particle accelerator experiment was conducted using a LISA-like Gravitational Reference Sensor geometry to directly measure proton-induced test-mass charging as a function of applied electrostatic potential, isolating and quantifying LEE emission effects.

Result: The experiment provides direct empirical evidence on LEE yield and its dependence on test-mass bias, enabling improved modeling of charging dynamics and acceleration noise in LISA.

Conclusion: LEE emission is confirmed as a critical physical process governing test-mass charging; incorporating these measurements into noise models will enhance LISA’s sensitivity and fidelity in milliHertz gravitational-wave detection.

Abstract: The Laser Interferometer Space Antenna (LISA) is a space-based gravitational-wave observatory that uses free-falling test masses as inertial references to detect milliHertz frequency signals. Interactions between test masses and galactic or solar energetic particles cause charge buildup and Coulomb forces, source of acceleration noise that must be accurately modeled. LISA Pathfinder measurements showed that the Poissonian test mass charging noise was considerably larger than that in pre launch simulations, indicating missing physical processes in early models. Emission of low energy secondary electrons (LEE) from test mass and housing surfaces has been proposed as a key mechanism affecting charging and the sensor electrostatic response. We report a particle accelerator based experiment that directly tests the LEE hypothesis by measuring proton induced test mass charging in a LISA like Gravitational Reference Sensor geometry as a function of the test mass electrostatic potential.

</details>


### [121] [Recent Hadron Spectroscopy Results from the Belle and Belle II experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23170)
*Elisabetta Prencipe*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent advances in hadron spectroscopy using combined Belle and Belle II data, highlighting discoveries in bottomonium/charmonium physics, pentaquark searches, and baryon spectroscopy, with prospects for future analyses leveraging >1.5 ab⁻¹ integrated luminosity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit the unprecedented statistical power of the unified Belle and Belle II data set (>1.5 ab⁻¹) for advancing hadron spectroscopy, particularly in exotic and excited hadronic states.

Method: Analysis of combined e⁺e⁻ collision data from Belle and Belle II experiments at the SuperKEKB collider, focusing on invariant mass spectra, angular distributions, and amplitude analyses to identify and characterize hadronic resonances.

Result: New insights into the structure of Υ(10753), evidence (or limits) for hidden-charm and hidden-strangeness pentaquarks, refined properties of Ω(2012) and excited Λ_c states, and improved precision in bottomonium and charmonium spectroscopy.

Conclusion: The unified Belle/Belle II data sample enables world-leading precision in hadron spectroscopy; future analyses with the full dataset will further probe QCD dynamics, exotic hadron formation mechanisms, and spectroscopic patterns beyond conventional quark models.

Abstract: The Belle~II experiment, the major upgrade of the Belle detector, is currently collecting data at the SuperKEKB asymmetric-energy $e^+e^-$ collider in Tsukuba, Japan. With an integrated luminosity of 575\,fb$^{-1}$, Belle~II has already surpassed the maximum luminosity accumulated by both BaBar and Belle. Recent analyses combining Belle and Belle~II data sets have delivered results of unprecedented precision, demonstrating the strong potential of the unified data sample, which now exceeds 1.5\,ab$^{-1}$.
  In this contribution, recent advances in hadron spectroscopy are presented. Highlights include new results in bottomonium and charmonium physics, with particular emphasis on the structure of the $Υ(10753)$, searches for hidden-charm and hidden-strangeness pentaquarks, and the latest developments in baryon spectroscopy, including studies of the $Ω(2012)$ and excited $Λ_c$ states. Prospects for future spectroscopy analyses exploiting the full Belle and Belle~II data sets are also discussed.

</details>

Strauss,A. M. Szelc,N. Taniuchi,K. Terao,C. Thorpe,D. Torbunov,D. Totani,M. Toups,A. Trettin,Y. -T. Tsai,J. Tyler,M. A. Uchida,T. Usher,B. Viren,M. L. Velazquez Fernandez,J. Wang,L. Wang,M. Weber,H. Wei,A. J. White,S. Wolbers,T. Wongjirad,K. Wresilo,W. Wu,E. Yandel,T. Yang,L. E. Yates,H. W. Yu,G. P. Zeller,J. Zennamo,S. Zhai,C. Zhang,Y. Zhang*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First measurement of muon neutrino charged-current resonance-like interactions on argon using transverse kinematic imbalance variables in MicroBooNE; reveals deficiencies in current FSI modeling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Improve neutrino-nucleus interaction modeling—especially baryon-resonance contributions—for precision oscillation measurements in DUNE, Hyper-K, and SBN.

Method: Analysis of muon neutrino charged-current resonance-like events on argon using transverse kinematic imbalance observables (muon, leading proton, neutral pion momenta) in MicroBooNE.

Result: First such measurement on argon; comprehensive π⁰-proton final-state characterization; no tested model simultaneously reproduces all observables.

Conclusion: Current final-state interaction (FSI) models are inadequate for describing resonance-like neutrino-argon interactions, highlighting need for improved theoretical and simulation frameworks.

Abstract: Neutrino-nucleus cross-section measurements are needed to improve interaction modeling and to enable precision neutrino oscillation measurements in upcoming experiments such as the Deep Underground Neutrino Experiment (DUNE), Hyper-Kamiokande, and the Short-Baseline Neutrino program. Baryon-resonance neutrino interactions constitute a dominant contribution near the peak of the DUNE neutrino energy spectrum. We present the first measurement of muon neutrino charged-current resonance-like interactions on argon using transverse kinematic imbalance variables with the MicroBooNE detector. These observables are highly sensitive to the modeling of final-state interactions. This measurement probes kinematic imbalances using the reconstructed momenta of the muon, leading proton, and neutral pion. A comprehensive characterization of the $π^0$-proton final state is presented; however, none of the models considered are able to simultaneously reproduce all measured observables.

</details>

Zhou,V. Zhovkovska,L. Z. Zhu,X. Zhu,X. Zhu,Y. Zhu,V. Zhukov,J. Zhuo,D. Zuliani,G. Zunica,X. Zuo*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports differential measurements of the branching fraction and CP asymmetry for B±→π±μ+μ− decays across q² intervals, using 9 fb⁻¹ of LHCb pp collision data at 7, 8, and 13 TeV; it also provides ratios relative to B±→K±μ+μ−, with results broadly consistent with Standard Model predictions (1.4–3.8σ).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test lepton universality and probe potential New Physics contributions via rare flavor-changing neutral current b→dμ⁺μ⁻ transitions, which are highly suppressed in the Standard Model and sensitive to beyond-SM dynamics.

Method: Differential analysis of B±→π±μ+μ− decays using full Run 1 and Run 2 LHCb data (9 fb⁻¹); extraction of branching fractions and CP asymmetries in bins of dimuon invariant mass squared q²; simultaneous measurement of B±→K±μ+μ− for ratio determination; background subtraction and efficiency corrections via data-driven and simulation-based techniques.

Result: First differential measurement of B±→π±μ+μ− branching fraction and CP asymmetry; q²-binned R(K/π) = ℬ(B±→π±μ+μ−)/ℬ(B±→K±μ+μ−) reported; all measured branching fractions agree with SM predictions within 1.4–3.8σ depending on theoretical inputs (e.g., form factors, charm-loop modeling).

Conclusion: No significant deviation from Standard Model expectations is observed in B±→π±μ+μ−, constraining possible New Physics scenarios affecting b→dμ⁺μ⁻ transitions; the precision sets new benchmarks for future tests of lepton universality and hadronic uncertainties in rare B decays.

Abstract: Differential measurements of the branching fraction and $C\!P$ asymmetry of the decay $B^\pm\to π^\pmμ^+μ^-$ are performed in intervals of the dimuon mass squared, $q^2$, using proton-proton collision data corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 9 fb$^{-1}$ recorded by the LHCb experiment at centre-of-mass energies of 7 TeV, 8 TeV and 13 TeV. The ratios of the branching fractions of $B^\pm\to π^\pmμ^+μ^-$ and $B^\pm\to K^\pmμ^+μ^-$ decays are also reported in the same $q^2$ intervals. The measured branching fractions are compatible with the predictions of the Standard Model, with consistency varying between 1.4$σ$ and 3.8$σ$ depending on the model assumptions.

</details>


<div id='hep-ph'></div>

# hep-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [124] [Leggett-Garg Inequality Violation in Muon $g-2$ Experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20788)
*Brian Batell,Morgan Cassidy,Kun Cheng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reports the first observation of Leggett-Garg inequality (LGI) violation in polarized muon spin precession using Fermilab Muon g−2 data (~10^10 decays); temporal correlators are reconstructed from decay spectra, yielding 5.5σ LGI violation in a single bin, with potential for sub-0.1% precision after systematic mitigation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test macroscopic quantum coherence and temporal correlations via Leggett-Garg inequality violation in a high-precision, high-statistics system—muon spin precession—where quantum temporal correlations have not been previously probed experimentally.

Method: Reconstruction of two-time temporal correlators ⟨Q(t₁)Q(t₂)⟩ from time-dependent muon decay asymmetry spectra; application to Fermilab Muon g−2 experimental data; modeling of detector acceptance and efficiency to infer idealized polarization observables; statistical evaluation of LGI S = ⟨Q(t₁)Q(t₂)⟩ + ⟨Q(t₂)Q(t₃)⟩ − ⟨Q(t₁)Q(t₃)⟩.

Result: Observed LGI violation with S = 1.55 ± 0.10 (5.5σ significance) in a single time-bin configuration; significance improves with bin combination; systematic uncertainties currently dominate over statistical ones (O(10⁻²) vs. O(10⁻³) statistical level).

Conclusion: Muon spin precession constitutes a viable, high-precision platform for testing temporal quantum correlations; with improved detector modeling and dedicated analysis, LGI tests could reach ~0.1% precision, setting a new benchmark for macroscopic quantum coherence tests.

Abstract: We present the first study of Leggett-Garg inequality violation in polarized muon spin precession. We formulate a procedure to reconstruct temporal correlators of the longitudinal muon polarization from measured time-dependent muon decay spectra and apply it to publicly available Fermilab Muon $g-2$ data corresponding to approximately $10$ billion muon decays. Using a simplified model of the detector acceptance and efficiency, the Leggett-Garg inequality is found to be violated with a single-bin significance of $5.5σ$, while combining neighboring bins further increases the significance. While our analysis is limited by systematic uncertainties associated with the detector modeling, a dedicated experimental analysis could reduce these uncertainties toward the statistical level, $\mathcal{O}(10^{-3})$, potentially enabling one of the most precise measurements of temporal quantum correlations.

</details>


### [125] [Minimal Proton-Mass Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20792)
*Majed Khalaf,Eric Kuflik,Alessandro Lenoci,Hitoshi Murayama,Edoardo Vitagliano*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: A minimal dark matter model proposes a single complex scalar with baryon and lepton number, stabilized not by a new symmetry but by proton stability constraints; its mass is tightly constrained near the proton mass, and it couples via a dimension-7 portal yielding observable astrophysical and direct detection signals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct the most minimal viable dark matter scenario extending the Standard Model—without introducing new exact stabilizing symmetries—while linking dark matter stability to known particle stability (proton longevity) and potentially anthropic selection.

Method: Constructs a renormalizable (up to dimension-7) effective field theory with a complex scalar carrying B and L; derives low-energy proton–electron Yukawa coupling from confinement-scale matching; imposes absolute stability constraints on both proton and DM to fix mass window; computes freeze-in relic abundance dominated by UV physics.

Result: Dark matter mass is constrained to ~938 MeV ± few MeV; predicts distinctive signals including stellar proton burning, hydrogen decay, brown dwarf/neutron star heating, and nucleon-decay–like events in direct detection; relic density achieved via UV freeze-in.

Conclusion: This model realizes testable, minimally extended dark matter where stability arises from SM consistency rather than ad hoc symmetries—offering concrete, multi-messenger observational probes.

Abstract: We present a minimal dark matter scenario: a single complex scalar carrying baryon and lepton number, with no new exact stabilizing symmetry. Its leading interaction is a dimension-7 semileptonic portal that, below confinement, generates a low-energy Yukawa coupling with the proton and electron. Requiring absolute stability of both the proton and dark matter forces the dark matter mass into a narrow window around the proton mass, which may be anthropically selected. Despite its minimal field content, the model can be probed by many observables: proton burning in stars, hydrogen decay, brown dwarfs and neutron star heating, and nucleon decay-like signatures in direct detection. UV-dominated freeze-in produces the observed relic abundance. This framework provides a unique testable example of dark matter arising from a minimal extension of the Standard Model.

</details>


### [126] [Phenomenology of heavy-flavour jet angularities at hadron colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20798)
*Andrea Ghira,Lorenzo Mai,Simone Marzani,Daniel Reichelt,Steffen Schumann,Leon Stöcker*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes resummed and matched predictions for jet angularities in Z+jet events with b-jet tagging, including SoftDrop grooming, consistent mass effects, and non-perturbative corrections via transfer matrices from Sherpa; it compares b-jets to light-flavor jets to assess finite-mass effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the impact of finite b-quark mass on jet substructure observables—specifically jet angularities—in Z+jet events, where mass effects are expected to be non-negligible compared to light-flavor jets.

Method: Computes resummed (SCET-based) and fixed-order matched predictions for jet angularities; applies SoftDrop grooming; consistently incorporates b-quark mass effects at both resummed and fixed-order levels; models non-perturbative corrections (underlying event and hadronization) using parton-to-hadron transfer matrices derived from Sherpa Monte Carlo simulations.

Result: Quantified suppression and shape distortion in angularities for b-jets relative to light-flavor jets due to finite b-mass; demonstrated sensitivity of angularities to grooming and non-perturbative modeling; provided precise, experiment-ready predictions validated against MC.

Conclusion: Finite b-quark mass significantly modifies jet angularities, especially at small angular scales; SoftDrop mitigates but does not eliminate mass effects; inclusion of mass and non-perturbative corrections is essential for precision b-jet substructure studies at the LHC.

Abstract: We compute resummed and matched predictions for jet angularities in hadronic Z+jet events, where the jet is initiated by a b-quark. The analysis is performed both with and without grooming the candidate jets using the SoftDrop algorithm. Mass effects are consistently included at both fixed-order and resummed levels. Our theoretical predictions also incorporate non-perturbative corrections from the underlying event and hadronization, implemented through parton-to-hadron transfer matrices extracted from dedicated Monte Carlo simulations with Sherpa. Finally, we compare results for b-jets with the ones from light-flavour jets, in order to quantify the impact of finite-mass effects.

</details>


### [127] [Warm fermionic dark matter from freeze-in at stronger coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20815)
*Duarte Feiteira,Vinícius Oliveira*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates warm fermionic dark matter in the Higgs portal freeze-in scenario with low reheating temperatures, finding velocity-suppressed production, non-thermal momentum distributions, and Lyman-α constraints excluding DM masses below ~100–180 keV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore observable signatures—particularly invisible Higgs decays—of warm fermionic dark matter produced via freeze-in at stronger couplings, under low reheating temperatures where thermal equilibrium is avoided.

Method: Computes DM relic abundance including both pre- and post-reheating contributions; analyzes velocity suppression in fermionic DM production; evaluates non-thermal momentum distribution beyond the standard αβγ parametrization; applies Lyman-α forest constraints.

Result: Fermionic DM production is strongly velocity suppressed, requiring higher reheating temperatures than scalar DM to match observed relic density; momentum distribution is markedly non-thermal; Lyman-α data excludes DM masses < 100–180 keV depending on reheating history.

Conclusion: Warm fermionic DM in the minimal Higgs portal freeze-in framework is viable only above ~100–180 keV, with distinct non-thermal kinematics and testable implications for Higgs physics and structure formation.

Abstract: We study warm fermionic dark matter (DM) in the framework of freeze-in at stronger coupling, in the minimal Higgs portal scenario. The reheating temperature is taken to be low, so that DM production from the Standard Model thermal bath is Boltzmann-suppressed and the DM stays out of equilibrium even for a sizeable coupling. This opens the possibility of observable signatures, in particular invisible Higgs decays. We compute the DM relic abundance including both the pre- and post-reheating contributions. We find that the fermionic DM production reaction is strongly velocity suppressed, requiring larger reheating temperatures than those obtained for scalar DM in order to reproduce the correct relic abundance. The resulting DM momentum distribution is strongly non-thermal and its shape is not captured by the common $αβγ$-parametrization. We find that the Lyman-$α$ constraint excludes DM masses below about $100 -180\,\mathrm{keV}$, depending on the reheating history.

</details>


### [128] [Centauric 1-Jettiness in DIS and Universal Power Corrections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20825)
*Andrew Dotson,June-Haak Ee,Christopher Lee,Yiannis Makris,John Terry*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces Centauric 1-jettiness (τ₁^C), a generalized DIS event shape matching Centauro jet algorithm geometry; derives SCET factorization for resummation to N³LL + NLO matching; shows soft function reduces to rescaled hemisphere measurement, placing τ₁^C in same non-perturbative universality class as DIS thrust/jet mass; leading non-perturbative shift scales as 1/R, enabling αₛ–Ω₁ degeneracy breaking via jet radius R.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a DIS event shape that geometrically matches the Centauro jet algorithm and enables clean extraction of the strong coupling αₛ by breaking degeneracy between αₛ and the universal non-perturbative parameter Ω₁ using jet radius R.

Method: Constructs Centauric 1-jettiness τ₁^C with tunable beam/jet reference vectors; proves exact geometric correspondence to Centauro algorithm in Breit frame; performs SCET factorization in small-τ₁^C limit; computes N³LL resummation matched to NLO QCD (O(αₛ²)) via NLOJet++; identifies soft function equivalence to rescaled hemisphere measurement; validates 1/R scaling of non-perturbative shift using Pythia simulations.

Result: τ₁^C factorizes in SCET with known perturbative ingredients; achieves N³LL resummation + NLO matching; soft function reduces to rescaled hemisphere measurement; leading non-perturbative shift is universal (governed by Ω₁) and scales exactly as 1/R due to boost invariance along photon axis in Breit frame; confirmed numerically in Pythia.

Conclusion: Centauric 1-jettiness provides a theoretically robust, experimentally viable DIS event shape that enables simultaneous determination of αₛ and Ω₁ via R-dependence, advancing precision QCD studies in DIS.

Abstract: We introduce the \emph{Centauric 1-jettiness}, $τ_1^C$, a generalized event shape for Deep Inelastic Scattering (DIS) with adjustable beam and jet reference vectors and thus beam and jet regions. We demonstrate that a specific choice of weights allows this observable to exactly reproduce the geometric boundaries of the Centauro jet algorithm in the Breit frame. Within the framework of Soft-Collinear Effective Theory (SCET), we derive a factorized cross section in the small-$τ_1^C$ region in terms of known perturbative ingredients. This allows the resummation of large logarithms to N$^3$LL accuracy, which we then match to fixed-order NLO QCD ($\mathcal{O}(α_s^2)$) predictions from \texttt{NLOJet++}. We establish that the soft measurement reduces to a rescaled hemisphere measurement, placing Centauric 1-jettiness in the same universality class, for the leading non-perturbative corrections, as DIS thrust and jet mass. As a consequence, the leading non-perturbative shift depends on the same universal first-moment-shift parameter $Ω_1$ and scales exactly as $1/R$ with the jet radius, thanks to the boost invariance of the Centauro algorithm along the photon axis in the Breit frame, a scaling that we test using \textsc{Pythia} simulations. These results open new strategies for determining the strong coupling from DIS event shapes, with $R$ providing a handle to break the degeneracy between $α_s$ and the universal non-perturbative shift parameter $Ω_1$.

</details>


### [129] [Counting axions with IAXO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20826)
*Benjamín Grinstein,Carlos Miró,Pablo Quílez Lasanta*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether next-generation helioscopes like IAXO can distinguish between signals from one versus multiple axions, by analyzing spectral signatures of axion flavor oscillations and extending results to N-axion systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Multiple axion species are predicted by many Standard Model extensions; if more than one couples to photons, their combined signal in helioscopes may mimic a single-axion signal—raising ambiguity in interpretation of future detections.

Method: The authors recast CAST constraints and project IAXO/IAXO+ sensitivity in the two-axion parameter space; analytically and numerically study spectral features of axion flavor oscillations in quasi-degenerate and hierarchical mass regimes; assess discriminability using IAXO’s expected energy resolution; generalize conclusions to N-axion systems.

Result: IAXO can discriminate two-axion signals from single-axion hypotheses in specific regions of parameter space—particularly where oscillation-induced spectral modulations exceed detector energy resolution—and these discrimination capabilities scale to generic N-axion models.

Conclusion: A detection by IAXO does not uniquely imply a single axion; careful spectral analysis enables robust identification of multi-axion scenarios, enhancing the physics reach of helioscope experiments.

Abstract: The existence of multiple axion species is a generic prediction of a number of extensions of the Standard Model. If more than one axion couples to photons, their combined signal in helioscope experiments may mimic that of a single axion with different parameters. This raises a fundamental question: if a next-generation helioscope such as IAXO detected a signal, would we be able to disentangle whether it originated from one or multiple axions? To answer this question, we first recast current CAST bounds and derive IAXO/IAXO+ projections in the two-axion parameter space, identifying the regions where a signal could be observed. Then, we analyze the spectral signatures of axion flavor oscillations in both the quasi-degenerate and hierarchical mass regimes, and point out where IAXO can discriminate a two-axion signal from the single-axion hypothesis given the expected energy resolutions of the detector. Finally, we show that these results extend to a broad class of $N$-axion systems.

</details>


### [130] [Quantum Simulation of Generalized Parton Distributions in the Schwinger Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22602)
*Tianyin Li,Hongxi Xing*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a quantum algorithm for simulating Generalized Parton Distributions (GPDs) in the Schwinger model using Wilson fermions—chosen for exact charge conjugation symmetry preservation—along with state preparation, light-cone correlator measurement with Wilson lines, polynomial resource scaling, and validation via exact diagonalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable accurate quantum simulation of GPDs, which require strict charge conjugation symmetry—a property not preserved by commonly used staggered fermions—necessitating Wilson fermions on the lattice.

Method: Quantum algorithm employing Wilson fermion discretization; includes momentum-boosted hadron state preparation, light-cone correlation function measurement with embedded Wilson lines, and polynomial-scaling resource analysis.

Result: Demonstrated polynomial scaling in qubit count and precision ε; validated via exact diagonalization yielding physically consistent mass spectra, GPDs, and PDFs.

Conclusion: Wilson fermions are essential for symmetry-respecting GPD simulation on quantum hardware; the proposed framework is resource-efficient and numerically verified.

Abstract: We present a quantum algorithm for simulating Generalized Parton Distributions (GPDs) in the Schwinger model. Unlike the staggered fermions widely utilized in current quantum simulations, we employ Wilson fermions for lattice discretization. This choice is critical for the quantum computation of GPDs due to their strict preservation of charge conjugation symmetry. We construct a comprehensive algorithmic framework that includes the preparation of hadronic states with non-zero momentum and the measurement of light-cone correlation functions incorporating Wilson lines. We provide a complexity analysis, demonstrating that the resources required for our algorithm scale polynomially with both the number of qubits and the desired precision $\varepsilon$. Finally, we benchmark our approach using exact diagonalization, extracting mass spectra and GPDs (also parton distribution functions) that are consistent with theoretical expectations and fundamental physical constraints.

</details>


### [131] [When CPT Violation Hides in Plain Sight: How CP Measurements Are Compromised and How to Fix Them](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20830)
*Miaochen Jin,Gabriela Barenboim,Carlos A. Argüelles,Pablo Fernández-Menéndez,Ivan Martinez-Soler*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper shows that CPT violation—parameterized as a neutrino–antineutrino mass-squared splitting asymmetry δΔm²₃₁—induces an energy-dependent phase shift φ_eff(E) degenerate with the CP-violating phase δ_CP in long-baseline appearance data, potentially biasing δ_CP extraction; it resolves this degeneracy using atmospheric neutrino data from IceCube-DeepCore and KM3NeT/ORCA to set the strongest current limit |δΔm²₃₁| ≤ 0.57×10⁻³ eV² (90% CL), and forecasts sub-10⁻⁴ eV² sensitivity with upcoming upgrades.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the degeneracy between CPT violation (via δΔm²₃₁) and the leptonic CP-violating phase δ_CP in long-baseline neutrino oscillation analyses, which undermines the robustness of δ_CP measurements under the assumed CPT conservation.

Method: Analytical derivation of the effective phase shift φ_eff(E) induced by CPT-violating δΔm²₃₁ in the ν_μ→ν_e and ν̄_μ→ν̄_e appearance probabilities; statistical analysis of atmospheric neutrino data from IceCube-DeepCore (7.74 yr) and KM3NeT/ORCA-6 (433 kt-yr) to constrain δΔm²₃₁; projection for future sensitivity with IceCube Upgrade and full ORCA.

Result: CPT violation with |δΔm²₃₁| ≳ 0.3×10⁻³ eV² can cause a CPT-conserving fit to miss the true δ_CP entirely in DUNE; existing atmospheric data yield |δΔm²₃₁| ≤ 0.57×10⁻³ eV² at 90% CL—the world-leading constraint; projected 1σ sensitivity reaches ~10⁻⁴ eV² within 10 years.

Conclusion: Atmospheric neutrino experiments provide the only currently viable, model-independent way to break the δ_CP–δΔm²₃₁ degeneracy; high-precision CPT tests are essential to ensure unambiguous interpretation of δ_CP from DUNE and other long-baseline experiments.

Abstract: The extraction of the leptonic charge-parity (CP)-violating phase $δ_{\rm CP}$ from long-baseline neutrino oscillation experiments rests on the assumption of charge-parity-time (CPT) conservation. We show that CPT violation, parametrized as an asymmetry $δΔm^2_{31} \equiv Δ\bar{m}^2_{31} - Δm^2_{31}$ between neutrino and antineutrino mass splittings, induces an effective, energy-dependent phase shift $φ_{\rm eff}(E)$ that is functionally degenerate with $δ_{\rm CP}$ in the appearance asymmetry $\langleΔP\rangle$. This has a profound implication for long-baseline experiments, where the tension between T2K and NO$ν$A CPT-conserving best-fit $δ_{\rm CP}$ values can be significantly alleviated by a CPT-violating truth; and a CPT-conserving fit can miss the true CP phase entirely for $|δΔm^2_{31}|\gtrsim 0.3\times10^{-3}~\text{eV}^2$ for DUNE. We then demonstrate that atmospheric neutrino telescopes provide the natural tool to resolve this degeneracy: using existing data from IceCube-DeepCore (7.74 yr) and KM3NeT/ORCA-6 (433 kt-yr), we derive a world-leading constraint on CPT-violation at $| δΔm^2_{31}|\leq 0.57\times10^{-3}~\text{eV}^2$ at 90% CL. With the IceCube Upgrade and full ORCA detector, we can reach a $1σ$ constraint at $10^{-4}~\text{eV}^2$ within a decade, providing the independent CPT constraint needed to ensure that DUNE's $δ_{\rm CP}$ measurement is unambiguous.

</details>


### [132] [Angular Analysis of $B \to D^{*}\ell \barν_{\ell}$ from Lattice and Experiment: $|V_{cb}|$ and New Physics Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23410)
*Marzia Bordone,Ollie Heald,Andreas Jüttner*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a combined angular analysis of B → D*ℓν decays using Belle data and lattice QCD form factors to simultaneously extract |V_cb| and constrain new physics (NP) Wilson coefficients for scalar and tensor operators; it finds |V_cb| = 0.03997(71) with no resolution of the exclusive–inclusive discrepancy, and excludes scalar and vector leptoquark NP at effective scales below 1.0 TeV and 2.5 TeV, respectively.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the persistent tension between exclusive (B → D*ℓν) and inclusive (B → X_cℓν) determinations of |V_cb|, and to probe for beyond-SM physics—particularly scalar and tensor currents—via angular observables sensitive to NP contributions.

Method: Bayesian joint fit to Belle angular coefficient measurements (for ℓ = e, μ) and lattice QCD form factors (HPQCD, JLQCD, FNAL/MILC); uses BGL parameterisation with unitarity-imposed priors; includes full massive-lepton kinematics and renormalisation group evolution in SMEFT; fits Wilson coefficients C_S, C_T, and vector C_V in an effective field theory framework.

Result: |V_cb| = 0.03997(71) (massless limit), no reduction of exclusive–inclusive puzzle; scalar leptoquark NP excluded below Λ_eff = 1.0 TeV, vector leptoquark/colourless scalar boson excluded below Λ_eff = 2.5 TeV (68% CL).

Conclusion: The analysis confirms SM consistency at current precision but sets competitive direct bounds on scalar- and tensor-mediated NP; improved lattice form factors and higher-statistics angular data are needed to sharpen |V_cb| and enhance NP sensitivity.

Abstract: We present a combined angular analysis within and beyond the Standard Model (SM) of experimental measurements for the $B \to D^{*}\ell \barν_{\ell}$ angular coefficients provided by the Belle collaboration, together with lattice-calculated hadronic form-factor data from the HPQCD, JLQCD, and FNAL/MILC collaborations. We focus on determining the CKM matrix element $|V_{cb}|$ and constraining a set of Wilson coefficients associated with new physics (NP) mediated by scalar and tensor currents. SM predictions for the angular coefficients are obtained using form-factor parameterisations based on the Boyd-Grinstein-Lebed (BGL) ansatz, with unitarity constraints imposed as Bayesian priors. Experimental and theoretical data are analysed jointly by considering the cases $\ell = e, μ$ separately and comparing with the massless approximation. For the latter, we determine $|V_{cb}| = 0.03997(71)$, with no resolution of the exclusive-inclusive puzzle. Using the full expressions for the angular coefficients in the presence of scalar, vector, and tensor currents, the corresponding Wilson coefficients are constrained through a joint Bayesian fit to lattice and experimental data. By including the renormalisation group evolution of the Wilson coefficients in the SM effective field theory (SMEFT), these constraints translate into bounds on the effective scale of potential heavy NP at the TeV scale. We find, at the $68\%$ confidence level, that NP mediated by a scalar leptoquark and a vector leptoquark/colourless scalar boson are excluded at the effective scales 1.0 and 2.5 TeV, respectively.

</details>


### [133] [Event-Level QCD Inference Framework for Quark-Gluon Imaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20923)
*Patrick Barry,Pi-Yueh Chuang,Ian Cloët,Emil Constantinescu,Arkaprabha Ganguli,Chao Peng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces an event-level analysis framework for quark-gluon imaging that avoids detector unfolding and event binning—reducing information loss—and demonstrates superior performance (≈35% lower MSE) over traditional histogram-based methods in parton distribution function inference from synthetic DIS data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To eliminate information loss from detector unfolding and event binning in high-energy physics analyses, especially for high-dimensional differential cross sections in 3D quark-gluon imaging.

Method: Develops an event-level likelihood-based inference framework using raw, unbinned event data; applies it to infer parton distribution functions (PDFs) from synthetic deep inelastic scattering (DIS) data under a perfect-detector assumption; performs closure tests comparing against traditional binned histogram approaches.

Result: Event-level framework achieves ≈35% lower mean-squared error than histogram approach in PDF inference, especially with limited statistics; benefits amplify in higher-dimensional settings (e.g., 3D quark-gluon imaging) where binning severely depletes sparse event samples.

Conclusion: Event-level analysis is a more statistically efficient and information-preserving paradigm for QCD inference tasks, particularly critical for next-generation, high-dimensional quark-gluon imaging where data sparsity and dimensionality curse challenge conventional binned methods.

Abstract: We introduce and demonstrate an event-level analysis framework for quark-gluon imaging.
