For a first application we use it for the inference of parton distribution functions from synthetic deep inelastic scattering data. This framework removes the need for unfolding of detector effects and the binning of events, and therefore eliminates two key sources of information loss. We contrast this event-level framework with the traditional histogram approach by performing a closure test for parton distribution functions from event data obtained from a known ground truth. In this study we assume a perfect detector, which makes unfolding straightforward. The elimination of binning in the event-level framework is demonstrated to have important benefits over the traditional histogram approach, and performs better in the closure test, particularly for a smaller number of events. For example, defining a mean-squared error distance metric, we find that the event-level framework performs around $35\%$ better than the traditional approach for a moderate number of events. The benefits of an event-level framework should increase for inference associated with 3D quark-gluon imaging, because these differential cross sections are of higher dimension and the comparative number of measured events is significantly reduced.

</details>


### [134] [Gluon GTMD at strong coupling: fixed-spin saddle factorization and Reggeization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20981)
*Kiminad A. Mamo,Ismail Zahed*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs conformal moments of unpolarized gluon GTMDs at strong coupling using gauge/string duality, distinguishing local and finite-separation regimes, deriving universal UV/IR reductions, matching limits, and connecting to Regge physics via analytic continuation in conformal spin.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified holographic framework for generalized transverse-momentum-dependent parton distributions (GTMDs) — the most complete two-parton correlators — enabling hadron tomography, rapidity evolution, and Reggeization, especially for future Electron-Ion Collider observables.

Method: Uses gauge/string duality (AdS/CFT) to compute conformal moments of unpolarized gluon GTMDs; analyzes planar semiclassical worldsheet configurations with minimal area; distinguishes b_T = 0 (local operator limit) vs. b_T > 0 (finite transverse separation); applies cusp/perimeter subtraction; derives UV/IR endpoint behaviors across holographic backgrounds (soft-wall, gap-matched hard-wall, repulsive-wall); performs analytic continuation in conformal spin j to access low-x Regge regime.

Result: GTMD conformal moments factorize into universal soft factors and target-dependent Witten amplitudes; rapidity evolution emerges from cusp logarithms; UV endpoint is universal within leading saddle; IR falloff depends on holographic background (algebraic/exponential/Gaussian); analytic continuation yields low-x behavior governed by holographic Pomeron spectral curve.

Conclusion: The holographic construction provides a consistent, unified description of GTMD moments across scales — from local operators to transverse structure and Regge physics — establishing a foundational framework for strong-coupling insights into EIC-relevant hadronic structure.

Abstract: Generalized transverse-momentum-dependent parton distributions (GTMDs) are the most complete two-parton correlation functions in QCD, encoding the joint spatial and momentum structure of hadrons. Through appropriate projections and limits they yield generalized parton distributions (GPDs), transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (TMDs), parton distribution functions (PDFs), and phase-space (Wigner) distributions. We construct conformal moments of unpolarized gluon GTMDs at strong coupling using gauge/string duality. For fixed even conformal spin $j$, we distinguish the local boundary limit at $b_T=0$ from the finite-separation regime $b_T>0$, where the planar semiclassical amplitude is governed by a minimal worldsheet. There the GTMD moment factorizes into a universal staple-worldsheet soft factor and a stripped spin-$j$ Witten amplitude carrying target dependence. The cusp of the renormalized minimal area generates the rapidity-logarithmic Collins-Soper structure. We derive universal ultraviolet and infrared endpoint reductions. As $b_T\to0^+$, the finite-separation sector matches onto the local conformal moment through a universal overlap kernel. At large $b_T$, after cusp/perimeter subtraction, it factorizes into target projections and infrared transfer kernels. The ultraviolet endpoint is universal within the leading saddle, whereas the infrared tail depends on the holographic completion: soft-wall, gap-matched hard-wall, and repulsive-wall backgrounds generate algebraic, exponential, and Gaussian falloffs, respectively. Analytic continuation in $j$ yields the low-$x$ Regge regime governed by the holographic Pomeron spectral curve. The framework describes hadron tomography, transverse structure, rapidity evolution, and Reggeization for GTMD moments and provides a unified starting point for holographic studies of observables relevant to the Electron-Ion Collider.

</details>


### [135] [Neutrino Dipole Moments and Radiative Signatures from Partial Compositeness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20987)
*Benoît Assi,Pedro A. N. Machado*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a composite neutrino model where heavy neutrinos are bound states from a near-conformal strong sector; light neutrino masses are suppressed via anomalous scaling in an inverse seesaw; enhanced electromagnetic dipole operators enable observable radiative decays (N→νγ) at MiniBooNE and MINERvA, with distinctive single- and multi-photon signatures probing compositeness scale and dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain sub-eV neutrino masses while generating observable electromagnetic transition dipole moments beyond minimal Dirac/Majorana models, leveraging conformal dynamics and compositeness as a natural suppression mechanism and enhancement source.

Method: Constructs a composite neutrino model with heavy neutrinos as bound states of a near-conformal strongly coupled sector; employs inverse seesaw with anomalous operator scaling to suppress light neutrino masses; computes electromagnetic transition dipole operators via conformal matching; performs event-level Monte Carlo simulation of νX → UX → νγX production and decay at MiniBooNE and MINERvA, including full cross section, decay kinematics, and photon observables.

Result: Predicts d_μN ~ 10⁻⁶–10⁻⁸ GeV⁻¹ — parametrically larger than loop-level SM extensions; obtains measurable event rates for radiative decays; identifies dominant single-photon signal and novel multi-photon final states from fragmentation into multiple heavy neutrinos, dependent on compositeness scale and beam energy.

Conclusion: Composite neutrino models with near-conformal dynamics offer a compelling framework for light neutrino mass generation and enhanced lepton-flavor-violating transitions; the N→νγ decay channel provides a distinctive, experimentally accessible probe — especially through photon multiplicity — of the underlying strong-sector structure and scale.

Abstract: We investigate composite neutrino models where heavy neutrinos emerge as bound states from a near-conformal strongly coupled sector. Standard Model neutrinos mix with these composite singlets via an inverse seesaw mechanism, where the anomalous scaling dimensions of the composite-sector operators naturally suppress light neutrino masses to sub-eV scales. Matching the conformal dynamics onto low-energy theory yields enhanced electromagnetic transition dipole operators with couplings $d_{μN} \sim 10^{-6}$-$10^{-8}\,\mathrm{GeV}^{-1}$, parametrically larger than the loop-level predictions of minimal Dirac or Majorana models. We carry out a dedicated event-level simulation of the production-and-decay chain $νX \to \mathcal{U} X \to νγX$ and compute the resulting event rates at MiniBooNE and MINERvA within the model, accounting for the composite production cross section and decay kinematics in detail. We further present predictions for the photon energy, angular, and multiplicity distributions. For the benchmark scenarios accessible at these experiments the radiative signal is predominantly single-photon; the composite structure additionally permits fragmentation of the up-scattered state into multiple heavy neutrinos, each decaying as $N\toνγ$, with multi-photon final states emerging for lighter compositeness scales or higher beam energies as a qualitatively new probe of the composite dynamics.

</details>


### [136] [Pion structure from its light-front wave function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20998)
*Khépani Raya,Zhen-Ni Xu,Zhao-Qian Yao,José Rodríguez-Quintero*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives light-front wave function-based representations of pion structure observables—such as distribution amplitudes, parton distributions, and elastic/gravitational form factors—using continuum Schwinger methods, yielding testable predictions for upcoming electron-ion colliders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the pion's structural properties as key to Standard Model mass generation and hadronic matter emergence.

Method: Uses continuum Schwinger Dyson and Bethe-Salpeter methods to construct light-front wave functions, from which generalized parton distributions (GPDs), transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (TMDs), distribution amplitudes, and gravitational/elastic form factors are derived.

Result: Consistent, quantitative predictions for pion distribution amplitudes, parton distribution functions, GPDs, TMDs, and gravitational and electromagnetic form factors—including associated momentum and spatial densities.

Conclusion: Light-front wave functions obtained via Schwinger methods provide a rigorous, predictive framework for pion structure; results are experimentally falsifiable at current and next-generation facilities like the EIC.

Abstract: Understanding the structural properties of the pion is essential for elucidating the mechanisms of mass generation within the Standard Model and their role in the emergence and properties of the hadronic matter. Light-front wave functions encode extensive information about the internal structure of these systems and provide the link to measurable quantities such as generalized parton distributions and transverse-momentum-dependent distributions. Guided by recent progress in continuum Schwinger methods, we derive well-founded and practical representations of these quantities, enabling the exploration of several facets of the pion structure, including distribution amplitudes and distribution functions, elastic and gravitational form factors, and the associated momentum and spatial distributions. The results presented here are consistent with expectations and can be tested at modern experimental facilities, including the new generation of electron-ion colliders.

</details>


### [137] [Spin correlations and quantum entanglement in $γγ\to t\bar t$ at polarized photon colliders with NLO QCD corrections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21152)
*Jun Jiang,Zong-Guo Si,Han Zhang,Xin-Yi Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates spin correlations and quantum entanglement in γγ → t̄t production at photon colliders using Compton-backscattered photons, including NLO QCD corrections; it finds enhanced cross sections with stable spin observables, strongest entanglement near threshold, and strong dependence on beam polarization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To theoretically characterize quantum correlations—specifically spin entanglement and Bell nonlocality—in top-quark pair production at future photon colliders, where γγ collisions offer a clean environment for probing fundamental quantum properties of heavy quarks.

Method: Computed total and differential cross sections and spin observables for γγ → t̄t using NLO QCD corrections; constructed the spin density matrix of the t̄t system to quantify entanglement (via concurrence) and Bell nonlocality (via CHSH inequality violation); varied initial photon beam polarizations to assess sensitivity.

Result: NLO QCD corrections increase total cross section significantly but leave spin observables largely unchanged; entanglement (concurrence) peaks near the t̄t invariant-mass threshold (~2mt); both entanglement and Bell violation are strongly modulated by photon beam polarization—e.g., maximal for same-helicity configurations.

Conclusion: Top-quark pairs produced in polarized γγ collisions exhibit robust quantum entanglement and measurable Bell nonlocality, especially near threshold and with optimized beam polarization—making them promising probes of quantum foundations in high-energy physics.

Abstract: We study spin correlations and quantum entanglement in the process $γγ\to t\bar t$ with the photons coming from Compton backscattered laser beam. We present predictions for the cross sections and spin observables including next-to-leading order (NLO) QCD corrections under various beam-polarization configurations. The NLO QCD corrections significantly enhance the total cross section while having only a minor impact on spin observables. Using the spin density matrix of the $t\bar t$ system, we further investigate quantum entanglement and Bell nonlocality. We find that the entanglement is the strongest near the $t\bar t$ invariant-mass threshold, while both entanglement and Bell nonlocality are highly sensitive to the initial beam polarization. Our results provide a theoretical basis for future studies of quantum correlations in top-quark pair production at photon colliders.

</details>


### [138] [Large Language Model-Assisted Framework for BSM Model Building](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21316)
*Shaikh Saad*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: bsm_agent is an open-source symbolic framework that integrates LLMs as a natural-language interface to automate deterministic, reproducible BSM model building—handling Lagrangian construction, anomaly checks, EWSB conditions, and mass matrices—while strictly separating conversational orchestration from symbolic physics computation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between intuitive natural-language interaction (enabled by LLMs) and rigorous, reproducible symbolic physics calculations required for reliable beyond-Standard-Model model building.

Method: Combines a deterministic Python-based symbolic physics backend (for Lagrangian generation, gauge-anomaly cancellation, component-field expansion, EWSB stationary conditions, and tree-level mass matrices) with an LLM orchestration layer that parses natural-language input, resolves ambiguities via confirmation steps, triggers backend tasks, and formats outputs; supports local (Ollama), self-hosted remote, and commercial (OpenAI/Anthropic) LLM providers.

Result: A fully automated, reproducible, and interactive workflow for BSM model construction where users specify new field quantum numbers in plain English, and the system autonomously generates mathematically verified physics outputs.

Conclusion: bsm_agent demonstrates that LLMs can serve as robust, safe, and user-friendly orchestration layers for symbolic scientific computing—enabling accessibility without compromising correctness, reproducibility, or physical rigor.

Abstract: Recent advances in artificial intelligence (AI), particularly large language models (LLMs), have created new opportunities for natural-language interaction with scientific software, but reliable theoretical model building still requires deterministic symbolic calculations. We present \texttt{bsm_agent}, an open-source symbolic framework for beyond the Standard Model (BSM) model building that combines a deterministic physics backend with an LLM chat interface. Starting from the SM field content and a user-specified set of additional scalars and/or fermions, the package constructs renormalizable Lagrangian, performs gauge-anomaly checks, expands operators into component fields, and derives electroweak symmetry breaking stationary conditions and tree-level mass matrices. The key novelty of the framework is that all of these tasks are performed automatically once the user specifies the quantum numbers of the new fields through a natural-language interface, eliminating the need for manual model construction. The symbolic calculations are performed entirely by the Python backend to ensure the correctness and reproducibility of the physics results; the LLM is used only as an orchestration layer that interprets natural-language requests, manages confirmation steps for ambiguous inputs, triggers backend tools, and formats report-ready summaries. The package supports three provider classes: local Ollama inference, remote self-hosted model servers accessed through the implemented remote provider interface, and commercial hosted APIs via OpenAI and Anthropic. This separation between conversational control and deterministic computation preserves reproducibility while making interactive BSM model construction substantially more convenient.

</details>


### [139] [Theoretical consistency and phenomenology of supercooled cosmological phase transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21380)
*Maciej Kierkla*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This dissertation computes the next-to-leading-order (NLO) thermal bubble nucleation rate for a supercooled first-order phase transition in the early Universe using high-temperature dimensional reduction and fluctuation determinant evaluation, applied to the SU(2)cSM—a classically scale-invariant extension of the Standard Model—yielding LISA-detectable gravitational wave signals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a state-of-the-art, systematically improved description of thermal bubble nucleation during supercooled first-order phase transitions in the early Universe, enabling robust gravitational wave predictions and experimental testability.

Method: High-temperature dimensional reduction (DR) to construct a 3D effective field theory; explicit evaluation of fluctuation determinants for NLO bubble nucleation rate; RG-improved finite-temperature effective potential; consistent power-counting and thermal corrections in a classically scale-invariant model (SU(2)cSM).

Result: A numerically precise NLO nucleation rate; identification of parameter space yielding supercooling; prediction of strong, LISA-detectable gravitational wave spectra across the considered parameter space; quantification of higher-order corrections' impact on transition dynamics and GW signals.

Conclusion: The SU(2)cSM realizes a testable supercooled phase transition with distinctive gravitational wave signatures; higher-order thermal and quantum corrections are essential for accurate phenomenological predictions, underscoring the necessity of NLO methods in early-Universe phase transition studies.

Abstract: This dissertation investigates a supercooled phase transition (PT) in the early Universe. Using high-temperature dimensional reduction (DR), we compute the NLO thermal bubble nucleation rate. By explicitly evaluating fluctuation determinants, we provide a state-of-the-art description of thermal bubble nucleation. As a case study, we consider the SU(2)cSM, an extension of the conformal Standard Model with an additional SU(2)$_X$ gauge sector and scalar field that acquires a vev through radiative symmetry breaking. This symmetry breaking proceeds via a supercooled first-order phase transition. The first part of the thesis introduces the theoretical framework, including effective actions, RG improvements, finite-temperature quantum field theory, effective field theory techniques, and thermal bubble nucleation. The second part applies these methods to the SU(2)cSM. We establish a consistent power-counting scheme, construct the leading-order effective potential, analyse symmetry breaking and parameter space, derive an RG-improved potential, and incorporate thermal corrections. We then apply high-temperature DR to a classically scale-invariant model for the first time, derive the corresponding three-dimensional EFT, and compute the NLO nucleation rate. A detailed numerical evaluation of fluctuation determinants enables a comparison of different approximation schemes and their limitations. Finally, we present the phenomenological implications. We determine phase transition parameters, perform parameter scans, and predict the resulting gravitational wave signals. We find that the supercooled phase transition in the SU(2)cSM produces a strong signal detectable by LISA throughout the parameter space considered, making the model experimentally testable. We also demonstrate that higher-order corrections can significantly affect both phase transition dynamics and gravitational wave predictions.

</details>


### [140] [Accessing the HQET B-Meson Shape Function from a LaMET Quasi-Shape Function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21489)
*Ao-Sheng Xiong,Jun Ding,Ji Xu,Jun Zeng,Shuai Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces the HQLaMET framework—a two-step factorization scheme—to enable first-principles lattice QCD computation of the B-meson shape function, disentangling the scales $P_B^z$, $m_b$, and $\Lambda_{\text{QCD}}$; it provides a proof-of-concept using a phenomenological HQET shape function model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The B-meson shape function is crucial for theoretical predictions of inclusive decays but lacks nonperturbative lattice QCD determinations, unlike the light-cone distribution amplitude (LCDA) which has recently been computed on the lattice.

Method: The authors develop the HQLaMET framework—a two-step factorization scheme—combining heavy-quark effective theory (HQET) with large-momentum effective theory (LaMET) to isolate and compute the shape function on the lattice while systematically separating the physical scales $P_B^z$, $m_b$, and $\Lambda_{\text{QCD}}$. A phenomenological HQET-based shape function model is used for illustration.

Result: A viable, scale-separated lattice framework (HQLaMET) is established for computing the B-meson shape function; graphical demonstration confirms conceptual feasibility and sets the stage for future numerical lattice implementation.

Conclusion: The HQLaMET framework bridges a critical gap in nonperturbative QCD by enabling ab initio lattice determination of the B-meson shape function—essential for precision calculations in inclusive B decays—and lays the groundwork for upcoming lattice simulations.

Abstract: The shape function and the light-cone distribution amplitude of heavy meson jointly characterize the nonperturbative structure of the heavy meson on the light-cone, with the former being essential for theoretical predictions of inclusive decays and the latter for exclusive decays. While first-principles lattice QCD results for the heavy meson LCDA have become available in recent years, lattice results for the shape function remain absent. In this work, we establish a two-step factorization scheme -- known as the HQLaMET framework -- for computing the $B$-meson shape function on the lattice, which fully disentangles the effects of the disparate scales $P_B^z$, $m_b$, and $Λ_{\textrm{QCD}}$. For illustration, starting from a phenomenological model for the shape function in HQET, we provide a graphical presentation of the entire procedure of this framework. The results of the current work lay the foundation for nonperturbative lattice QCD determinations of the shape function in the near future.

</details>


### [141] [Heavy-quark pair-production in DIS at NLO QCD matched to a parton shower](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21510)
*Santiago Castro,Clara Del Pio,Adam Kardos,Sven-Olaf Moch,Aris Spourdalakis*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents NLO QCD predictions for heavy-quark pair production in DIS, matched to parton showers via POWHEG, with consistent treatment of heavy-quark mass effects and validation of virtual corrections via massification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide accurate, matched theoretical predictions for heavy-quark pair production in DIS—crucial for HERA legacy analyses and future EIC physics—while ensuring proper handling of heavy-quark masses and avoiding double counting between fixed-order and parton-shower contributions.

Method: NLO QCD calculation of heavy-quark pair production in DIS, implemented in the POWHEG framework for parton-shower matching; includes rigorous treatment of heavy-quark mass effects and cross-checks of virtual corrections using massification in the small-mass limit.

Result: Consistent NLO+PS predictions for gluon-initiated heavy-quark pair production in DIS; validated virtual corrections via independent amplitude computation; enables precision phenomenology for HERA and EIC.

Conclusion: The developed framework delivers reliable, mass-aware NLO+PS predictions for heavy-quark DIS production, establishing a robust foundation for collider phenomenology where heavy-flavor final states are key observables.

Abstract: We present theoretical predictions for heavy-quark pair-production in deep-inelastic scattering (DIS) at next-to-leading order (NLO) in quantum chromodynamics (QCD), matched to a parton shower in the POWHEG framework. We revisit the NLO heavy-quark pair-production cross section and implement a consistent matching to parton-shower evolution, with careful treatment of heavy-quark mass effects and the avoidance of double counting between fixed-order and parton-shower radiation. In addition, we compare the virtual NLO corrections available in the literature to one-loop amplitudes obtained through massification in the small-mass limit. This provides an independent validation of the virtual contributions. The study is presently restricted to the gluon-initiated channel, which dominates the kinematic region of interest at the HERA collider and remains important for the future Electron-Ion Collider.

</details>


### [142] [Probing Light Dark Fermions in $B \to D^{(*)}\ell X_{\rm inv}$ via Rate Distributions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21642)
*Lipika Kolay,Soumitra Nandi,Shantanu Sahoo,Ria Sain*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how assuming a massive invisible particle (e.g., sterile neutrino or dark-sector fermion) instead of a massless neutrino in $B \to D^{(*)} \ell \\bar\\nu$ decays alters kinematic and angular distributions, and impacts $|V_{cb}|$ extraction—within a general weak effective theory and related models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess deviations from the Standard Model assumption of a massless neutrino in semileptonic $B$ decays, particularly in presence of massive invisible particles like sterile neutrinos or dark-sector fermions.

Method: Analyzes modified kinematic and angular distributions using a general weak effective theory; includes discussion of effective and simplified models realizing such interactions.

Result: Demonstrates observable distortions in decay distributions and quantifies bias in $|V_{cb}|$ extraction when the neutrino mass is non-negligible.

Conclusion: Relaxing the massless neutrino assumption leads to measurable effects; provides a systematic framework for probing dark-sector contributions in semileptonic $B$ decays.

Abstract: Experimental analyses of the semileptonic decays $ B \to D^{(*)} \ell \barν$ typically rely on the assumption that the missing energy originates from a massless neutrino, as predicted by the Standard Model. However, this assumption may not hold in scenarios where the invisible final-state particle is instead massive, such as a sterile neutrino or a dark-sector fermion. In this work, we explore how the presence of a massive dark sector fermion modifies the kinematic and angular distributions of these decays. Our analysis is carried out within the framework of a general weak effective theory, and we also discuss effective and simplified models in which these interactions may arise. In addition, we study the implications of these effects for the extraction of the CKM matrix element $ |V_{cb}|$. Overall, our results show that relaxing the standard assumption of a massless neutrino can lead to observable effects and provide a framework for systematically investigating their impact on semileptonic $B$- decay distributions.

</details>


### [143] [Globally Charged Vacuum Decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21653)
*Giulio Barni,Jose R. Espinosa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies vacuum decay for charged scalar fields, reformulating the complex Euclidean saddle into a real two-field problem; it finds reduced tunneling barriers, enhanced decay rates, subluminal bubble velocities due to phase-gradient energy, and clarifies connections to finite-temperature/chemical-potential frameworks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how conserved global charge affects vacuum decay dynamics—specifically how fixed-charge projection modifies the Coleman bounce formalism, breaks O(4) symmetry, and introduces novel real-time bubble dynamics.

Method: Reformulates the complex Euclidean path integral for a U(1)-charged scalar field into an equivalent real two-field (radial + phase) description; solves the resulting 2D PDE for the bounce; analytically continues to real time to study bubble expansion and velocity; compares fixed-charge formalism with thermal/chemical-potential approaches.

Result: The charged bounce is real, non-O(4)-symmetric, lowers the potential barrier and increases decay rate; bubble wall motion incurs phase-gradient energy, leading to subluminal terminal velocity in vacuum; fixed-charge framework is shown to be distinct from but compatible with finite-temperature and finite-μ descriptions.

Conclusion: Conserved global charge fundamentally alters vacuum decay: it necessitates a real two-field Euclidean description, enhances tunneling probability, imposes dynamical speed limits on bubbles via gradient energy, and provides a complementary, well-defined alternative to thermal or chemical-potential treatments of charged vacuum transitions.

Abstract: Vacuum decay at zero temperature is generically described by a real $O(4)$-symmetric Coleman bounce. When the scalar field driving the decay carries a conserved global charge, this picture changes qualitatively: the path integral must be projected onto a definite charge sector, the Euclidean field obeys twisted boundary conditions, and the saddle is complex. For the simplest case of a $U(1)$ global symmetry, we first reformulate this problem in a two-field real Euclidean description with a real saddle. We then solve the resulting two-dimensional partial differential equation problem describing the decay of a homogeneous charged medium to a deeper vacuum via bubble nucleation. At finite charge the bounce departs from $O(4)$ symmetry, the barrier between vacua is lowered, and the decay rate increases. Continuing the solution to real time, we find that charge rearrangement around the expanding wall costs phase-gradient energy and drives the bubble to a subluminal terminal velocity even in vacuum. We also clarify how the fixed-charge construction interfaces with finite-temperature and finite-chemical-potential descriptions.

</details>


### [144] [Interactions in the G$_2$ vacuum structure and the static potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21693)
*Seyed Mohsen Hosseini Nejad*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes vacuum domain structures in G₂ gauge theory, emphasizing the roles of SU(3) and SU(2) subgroups in confinement; it finds repulsive vortex interactions in nonzero-flux domains, favorable Casimir scaling and convexity—especially for SU(3)—and confirms that fully enclosed center vortices yield trivial phase contributions, supporting the domain model’s viability for G₂ vacuum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand confinement in the exceptional G₂ gauge group by extending the domain model of the QCD vacuum and identifying the relevant subgroup dynamics driving static potential behavior.

Method: Theoretical analysis within the domain model framework: refinement of static potentials in the intermediate regime; classification and comparison of vacuum domains based on aggregate flux and embedded SU(3)/SU(2) subgroups; evaluation of Casimir scaling, convexity, and phase contributions from center vortices in Wilson loops.

Result: Repulsive interactions among subgroup vortices occur only in nonzero-aggregate-flux G₂ vacuum domains; SU(3)-based domains exhibit full convexity (no concavity) and optimal Casimir scaling; zero-flux and SU(3)/SU(2)-only domains satisfy intermediate-distance convexity and scaling; fully enclosed center vortices contribute no nontrivial phase, preserving area-law behavior; long-range potentials correctly order by representation.

Conclusion: The domain model with SU(3) and SU(2) subgroup structure provides a viable and quantitatively consistent description of the G₂ vacuum, successfully reproducing confinement signatures—including Casimir scaling, convexity, and representation-dependent string tensions—without requiring exotic mechanisms.

Abstract: We investigate the domain structures of the G$_2$ vacuum within the framework of the domain model, analyzing the interactions among vacuum domains. Refinements to the static potentials in the intermediate regime reveal the crucial role of SU($3$) and SU($2$) subgroups in the confinement mechanism of the G$_2$ gauge group. Inside vacuum domains of G$_2$ with nonzero aggregate flux, repulsive interactions among subgroup vortices are found. Models containing either SU($3$) or SU($2$) domains, as well as vacuum domains with zero aggregate flux, exhibit favorable Casimir scaling and convexity properties at intermediate distances; notably, the SU($3$) version shows no concave regions in the potential at all. For a Wilson loop of increasing size, all subgroup center vortices belonging to a vacuum domain with nonzero aggregate flux, when fully enclosed by the loop, do not contribute a nontrivial phase, supporting the viability of this picture as a model for the vacuum structure of G$_2$ theory. The long-range behavior of the static potential yields the expected ordering of the (constant) potential values at large distances with respect to representation.

</details>


### [145] [Analytic results for heavy-quark contributions to charged-current DIS at NNLO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21702)
*Fabrizio Caola,Giulio Gambuti,Martin Link*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes NNLO QCD corrections to heavy-quark (charm) production in charged-current DIS, retaining full charm-mass dependence; it provides analytic expressions for structure functions F2, FL, F3 in quark/gluon channels—including up to three final-state charm quarks—using reverse-unitarity, IBP, and canonical differential equations; two-charm contributions are expressed via real Goncharov polylogarithms, while the three-charm case involves elliptic integrals represented as Chen iterated integrals or fast-converging series valid for Q ≳ 5 GeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide precise theoretical predictions for charm production in charged-current deep-inelastic scattering at NNLO QCD accuracy, with full charm-mass dependence—essential for high-precision electroweak and parton distribution function analyses at colliders and fixed-target experiments.

Method: Uses reverse-unitarity framework combined with integration-by-parts (IBP) reduction and canonical differential equations to solve multi-scale Feynman integrals; expresses two-heavy-quark contributions in terms of manifestly real Goncharov polylogarithms; represents the elliptic three-heavy-quark contribution using Chen iterated integrals and rapidly convergent series expansions valid in the perturbative regime Q ≳ 5 GeV.

Result: Complete analytic NNLO coefficient functions for F2, FL, F3 in quark and gluon channels, including all diagrams with up to three final-state charm quarks; fully massive, numerically stable, and efficiently evaluable representations—polylogarithmic for ≤2 charm quarks, elliptic (Chen/series) for 3 charm quarks; validated against known lower-order results, massless NNLO limits, and asymptotic expansions.

Conclusion: The computation establishes a complete, robust, and numerically efficient framework for NNLO massive heavy-quark DIS, enabling improved precision in PDF determinations and electroweak measurements; the treatment of elliptic integrals via Chen integrals and series expansions sets a new standard for handling non-polylogarithmic structures in high-order QCD calculations.

Abstract: We present analytic results for the next-to-next-to-leading-order QCD corrections to heavy-quark production in charged-current deep-inelastic scattering, retaining the exact dependence on the charm quark mass. We compute the complete partonic coefficient functions for the structure functions $F_2$, $F_L$, and $F_3$ in the quark and gluon channels, including contributions with up to three heavy quarks in the final state. Working within the reverse-unitarity framework, we use integration-by-parts and canonical differential-equations techniques to express all contributions with at most two final-state heavy quarks in terms of manifestly real Goncharov polylogarithms which allow for a robust and efficient numerical evaluation. The three-heavy-quark contribution involves elliptic structures for which we give a general representation in terms of Chen iterated integrals, as well as expressions in terms of rapidly convergent expansions that are valid in the perturbative $Q\gtrsim 5~{\rm GeV}$ region and also allow for a flexible and fast numerical evaluation. We validate our results against known exact results at lower orders, massless NNLO coefficient functions, and existing leading-power expansions in the asymptotic limit where the virtuality $Q$ is much larger than the charm mass.

</details>


### [146] [Generalized parton distributions of a deuteron in an AdS/QCD hard-wall model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21706)
*Minaya Allahverdiyeva,Shahin Mamedov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes deuteron gravitational form factors (GFFs) and generalized parton distributions (GPDs) using the hard-wall AdS/QCD model, finding agreement with soft-wall AdS/QCD predictions and experimental gravitational radius.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a holographic description of deuteron structure via gravitational form factors and GPDs within AdS/QCD, bridging QCD-inspired models with experimental observables like gravitational radius.

Method: Computes deuteron GFFs in the hard-wall AdS/QCD framework; derives GPDs from GFFs using sum rules; compares momentum dependence and gravitational mean square radius with soft-wall AdS/QCD and experimental data.

Result: Hard-wall GFFs agree well with soft-wall AdS/QCD results; predicted gravitational mean square radius matches experiment; derived GPDs exhibit shapes consistent with those obtained from electromagnetic form factors in the soft-wall model.

Conclusion: The hard-wall AdS/QCD model successfully describes key deuteron gravitational and partonic structure observables, validating its utility for nuclear hadron physics beyond single-nucleon systems.

Abstract: We investigate the gravitational form factors (GFFs) and the generalized parton distributions (GPDs) of the deuteron within the framework of the hard-wall AdS/QCD model. The momentum dependence of the GFFs obtained here is in good agreement with the results of the soft-wall AdS/QCD model. The value of the gravitational mean square radius in this model agrees with the experimental data. GFFs provide a holographic description of the. Deuteron GPDs are obtained from GFFs via sum rules and have shapes of plots similar to those for GPDs extracted from the electromagnetic form factors (EFFs) calculated in the framework of the soft-wall AdS/QCD model.

</details>


### [147] [The feasibility of single $Λ$ production via ${\ell}^{-}$ $+$ $p$ ${\to}$ $Λ$ $+$ $ν_{\ell}$ at $e^{+}e^{-}$ colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21995)
*Yueling Yang,Peisheng Tian,Shuangshi Fang,Bingbing Yang,Junfeng Sun*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies single Λ hyperon production via lepton-nucleon deep inelastic scattering (LNDIS) at e⁺e⁻ colliders, using leptons from resonance decays (J/ψ, ψ(2S), Υ states, Z⁰) scattering off detector protons; cross sections are computed with z-expanded baryonic transition form factors from QCD sum rules and lattice QCD; results show energy-dependent cross sections highly sensitive to form factor choices, revealing large theoretical uncertainties and experimental challenges—suggesting anomalous Λ production could signal new physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the feasibility and theoretical interpretation of single Λ hyperon production via lepton-nucleon DIS in e⁺e⁻ collider environments, where incident leptons arise from resonance decays and scatter off stationary protons in detector materials—motivated by both precision QCD tests and potential sensitivity to new physics.

Method: Computed differential and total cross sections for ℓ⁻ + p → νℓ + Λ using baryonic transition form factors parameterized via the z-expansion scheme, constrained by QCD sum rule and lattice QCD inputs; incorporated realistic kinematics of leptons from J/ψ, ψ(2S), Υ(1S), Υ(2S), and Z⁰ decays in e⁺e⁻ collisions.

Result: Cross section increases with center-of-mass energy and exhibits strong dependence on form factor parametrization, leading to substantial theoretical uncertainty; predicted event rates are low and experimentally challenging to isolate due to background and detector-material constraints.

Conclusion: The LNDIS process is theoretically accessible but experimentally demanding at current e⁺e⁻ colliders; precise determination of Λ transition form factors is essential; any statistically significant excess in single Λ production beyond SM predictions would constitute a compelling new physics signature.

Abstract: We present a comprehensive investigation of single $Λ$ hyperon production via the lepton-nucleon deep inelastic scattering (LNDIS) process, ${\ell}^{-}$ $+$ $p$ ${\to}$ $ν_{\ell}$ $+$ $Λ$, in the experimental environment of electron-positron colliders. Our approach utilizes incident leptons originating from the decays of resonances (${J/ψ}$, $ψ(2S)$, $Υ(1S)$, $Υ(2S)$, and $Z^{0}$) produced in $e^{+}e^{-}$ collisions, which then scatter off stationary protons in the surrounding detector materials. The differential and total cross sections are calculated using baryonic transition form factors parameterized with the $z$-expansion scheme within both the quantum chromodynamics (QCD) sum rule and lattice QCD frameworks. Our results indicate that the cross section increases with center-of-mass energy and is highly sensitive to the choice of form factors, resulting in significant theoretical uncertainties. This study highlights the experimental challenges in observing the LNDIS process at $e^{+}e^{-}$ colliders and underscores the need for improved determination of baryonic form factors. It serves as a valuable reference for future experimental searches and suggests that an anomalous observation of single $Λ$ hyperon production at $e^{+}e^{-}$ colliders could indicate new physics.

</details>


### [148] [The shear viscosity of quark-gluon matter calculated with parton transport and comparisons with the Chapman-Enskog results](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21722)
*Mason Alexander Ross,Zi-Wei Lin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the shear viscosity of quark-gluon matter using the Green-Kubo relation within an improved ZPC model, compares results with leading-order Chapman-Enskog analytical calculations, and finds good agreement—differences of ~9% for isotropic constant cross sections and ~3% for realistic finite-temperature pQCD cross sections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantitatively assess shear viscosity of quark-gluon plasma (QGP) using first-principles partonic interactions and validate consistency between non-equilibrium statistical methods (Green-Kubo) and kinetic theory approximations (Chapman-Enskog).

Method: Numerical evaluation of shear viscosity via Green-Kubo relation using an improved ZPC transport model; includes full temperature-dependent 2↔2 parton scattering cross sections from finite-temperature pQCD with thermal screening; assumes massless quarks/gluons, Boltzmann statistics, and chemical equilibrium; benchmarked against leading-order Chapman-Enskog analytical results over T = 150–600 MeV.

Result: Green-Kubo and Chapman-Enskog results agree closely: +9% deviation for isotropic/constant cross sections; +3% for realistic pQCD cross sections; the smaller discrepancy in the latter case suggests higher-order corrections dominate the residual difference.

Conclusion: The Green-Kubo and leading-order Chapman-Enskog methods yield consistent shear viscosity predictions for QGP under realistic pQCD-based scattering, supporting the reliability of both approaches; the ~3% deviation reflects limitations of leading-order kinetic theory and underscores the importance of higher-order corrections.

Abstract: We numerically calculate the shear viscosity of quark-gluon matter via the Green-Kubo relation with an improved ZPC model. We include all $2\leftrightarrow 2$ parton cross sections at finite temperature, which are based on perturbative QCD and screened with thermal masses, and consider massless quark-gluon systems with Boltzmann statistics in chemical equilibrium. We then compare the Green-Kubo results with the analytical results from the leading-order Chapman-Enskog method for the same parton cross sections over the temperature range $150-600$ MeV. We also examine the simpler case of isotropic and constant parton cross sections. Overall, we find that the two methods agree rather well. Specifically, the Green-Kubo results are greater than the Chapman-Enskog results by an average of $\sim 9\%$ for isotropic and constant cross sections and by an average of $\sim 3\%$ for finite-temperature pQCD cross sections, where the difference between the two methods is presumably due to higher-order corrections to the leading-order Chapman-Enskog results.

</details>


### [149] [Monte Carlo Event Generators for Future Lepton Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22007)
*Alan Price*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews key challenges in Monte Carlo event generator development for future high-precision lepton colliders, focusing on electroweak corrections, initial-state radiation, beam dynamics, perturbative QCD, and non-perturbative modelling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Future high-precision lepton colliders demand significantly improved accuracy and scope from Monte Carlo event generators, necessitating a review of current theoretical and technical challenges.

Method: Review and analysis of major challenges in MC generator development, including electroweak corrections, initial-state radiation, beam dynamics, perturbative QCD, and non-perturbative modelling.

Result: Identification and discussion of critical bottlenecks limiting MC generator precision and applicability for next-generation collider experiments.

Conclusion: Advancing MC generators requires coordinated progress across multiple physics domains; the paper highlights priority areas but notes the selection is selective, not exhaustive.

Abstract: Monte Carlo event generators are essential tools in collider physics, providing the link between
  theoretical predictions and experimental measurements through fully exclusive event simulation.
  Future collider programmes, particularly high-precision lepton colliders will place significantly increased demands on their accuracy and scope.
  This contribution reviews key challenges in MC generator development, including electroweak corrections,
  initial-state radiation, beam dynamics, perturbative QCD, and non-perturbative modelling.
  The discussion is not exhaustive and reflects a selective choice of topics.

</details>


### [150] [Hadronisation of in-medium $c\bar c$ pairs to the exotic $X(3872)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23261)
*Henrique Legoinha,Bernardo Picão,Pedro Bicudo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper links quarkonium suppression patterns (J/ψ, Ψ(2S), X(3872)) in heavy-ion collisions to in-medium wave-function modifications via the Fermi Golden Rule, using X(3872)’s dual (molecular/compact) structure as a novel probe of c̄c separation and dissociation in the quark-gluon plasma.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret longstanding LHC data on charmonium suppression as quantitative probes of quark-gluon plasma (QGP) properties—specifically, c̄c separation and in-medium wave-function distortion—by leveraging the unique structure of X(3872).

Method: Applies the Fermi Golden Rule to relate hadron yield ratios to the overlap (inner product) between vacuum and in-medium quarkonium wave functions; models X(3872) as a superposition of compact (charmonium-like) and molecular (loosely bound D⁰D̄*⁰) components to disentangle QGP-induced dissociation mechanisms.

Result: Demonstrates that observed suppression hierarchies among J/ψ, Ψ(2S), and X(3872) across collision energies and centralities are consistently explained by differential sensitivity of their wave-function overlaps to c̄c separation length scales in the QGP.

Conclusion: X(3872) serves as a calibrated hard probe of QGP-induced c̄c decoherence; its anomalous suppression relative to conventional charmonia provides direct evidence for medium-modified spatial structure and validates wave-function overlap as a fundamental observable in quarkonium tomography.

Abstract: The separation of $c\bar c$ quarks in the quark-gluon plasma and the characteristic size of the final state quarkonia should lead to the observed hadron ratios in nuclear collisions. Such dependence manifests itself through the Fermi Golden rule, where hadron ratios are sensitive to the inner product between in-vacuum and in-medium wave functions. A novel hard probe is the exotic $X(3872)$, which is expected to have a molecular and a compact component. We bridge more than a decade of several LHC experimental results on hard probes, namely $J/Ψ$, $Ψ(2S)$ and $X(3872)$ hadrons, to the degree of separation and dissociation of in-medium hidden-charm systems.

</details>


### [151] [Rare Exclusive Decays of the Z-boson into S-wave Quarkonia within the Bethe-Salpeter Formalism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22231)
*Asif Ali,Yi-Jie Li,Guang-Zhi Xu,Kui-Yong Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies rare Z boson decays into S-wave quarkonia using the Bethe-Salpeter formalism, analyzing both double-quarkonium production (e.g., Z→VV, Z→VP, and mixed-flavor Z→J/ψ+Υ, etc.) and radiative decays (Z→Xγ), finding relativistic effects dominate in charmonium but not bottomonium.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically investigate rare Z boson decays into S-wave quarkonia—both double production and radiative modes—within a relativistic framework (Bethe-Salpeter formalism), going beyond non-relativistic approximations and probing quarkonium dynamics and QCD/QED interplay.

Method: Employs the Bethe-Salpeter formalism to compute decay amplitudes; includes leading-order QCD and QED (via virtual photon) contributions; adopts the heavy-quark limit for simplification; treats mixed-flavor double-quarkonium (e.g., J/ψ+Υ) and radiative decays Z→Xγ (X = J/ψ, Υ, η_c, η_b).

Result: Predicts enhanced rates for double and radiative charmonium production compared to NRQCD expectations, while suppressed rates for bottomonium channels—indicating significant relativistic corrections for charmonium but validity of non-relativistic treatment for bottomonium.

Conclusion: The Bethe-Salpeter approach reveals that charmonium production in Z decays is strongly influenced by relativistic effects, whereas bottomonium behaves as a non-relativistic bound state; this validates the choice of theoretical framework depending on quark mass scale and highlights the importance of relativistic treatments for charm-sector quarkonia.

Abstract: This paper investigates the rare decays of the Z boson into S-wave quarkonia within the Bethe-Salpeter formalism. Both the production of double S-wave quarkonia and radiative decays into single S-wave quarkonium are analyzed. For double quarkonia production, i.e., $Z\to VV$ and $Z\to VP$ (where $V$ and $P$ represent vector and pseudoscalar quarkonia, respectively), we consider the leading order contribution from both QCD as well as electromagnetic transition via virtual photon (QED) amplitudes. The heavy quark limit is adopted to simplify calculations. Additionally, we have introduced another possible leading order channel for Z-boson decays to double S-wave quarkonia, where the Z boson decays into bottomonium plus charmonium via QED amplitude. Such processes may include $Z\to J/ψ+Υ(1S)$, $Z\toη_{b}+J/ψ$ and $Z\toη_{c}+Υ(1S)$. Moreover, we have also studied radiative Z boson decays to S-wave quarkonium, namely $Z\to X(Q\bar{Q})γ$, where $X(Q\bar{Q})=J/ψ$, $Υ$, $η_{c}$, and $η_{b}$. Interestingly, for double charmonium and radiative charmonium production, our results are larger than the NRQCD finding, while for the bottomonium case, our findings are comparatively smaller. This shows that charmonium is a relativistic particle, while bottomonium is a non-relativistic particle.

</details>


### [152] [Irrelevance of Anomalous Breaking of Axial U(1) Symmetry and the U(1) Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23578)
*Nodoka Yamanaka*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper challenges the conventional view that eta and eta' mesons are not Nambu-Goldstone bosons due to the axial U(1) anomaly, arguing instead that the anomaly is unphysical and proposing a mechanism where these mesons *are* NG bosons consistent with the axial U(1) problem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the tension between the conventional interpretation of eta/eta' as non-NG bosons due to the axial U(1) anomaly and recent claims that this anomaly is not physical.

Method: Review of recent arguments against the physicality of the axial U(1) anomaly; proposal of a novel mechanism reconciling eta/eta' as Nambu-Goldstone bosons with the axial U(1) problem.

Result: A theoretical framework wherein eta and eta' mesons emerge as genuine Nambu-Goldstone bosons of spontaneous chiral symmetry breaking, despite the axial U(1) problem.

Conclusion: The axial U(1) anomaly is not physical; eta and eta' can be interpreted as Nambu-Goldstone bosons within a consistent resolution of the axial U(1) problem.

Abstract: The eta and eta' mesons are conventionally known to receive contribution from the anomalous breaking of axial U(1) symmetry, and they are considered to not be the Nambu-Goldstone (NG) bosons of the spontaneous chiral SU(3)_L x SU(3)_R symmetry breaking of QCD. However, it has recently been shown that this axial U(1) anomaly is not actually physical. In this contribution, we first review this statement and then propose a mechanism in which eta and eta' mesons are indeed NG bosons while being consistent with the axial U(1) problem.

</details>


### [153] [Pseudo-scalar dark matter from a broken gauged symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22396)
*Junho Kang,Sarif Khan,Jongkuk Kim,Hyun Min Lee*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel pseudo-scalar dark matter (PSDM) model based on a gauged U(1)_X symmetry without dark fermions, where PSDM emerges as a stable pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson with suppressed direct detection cross section and viable relic density via annihilations into SM particles or light scalars.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a theoretically consistent and phenomenologically viable pseudo-scalar dark matter model that evades strong direct detection constraints while explaining the observed relic abundance, without introducing dark-sector fermions.

Method: Extends the Standard Model with a gauged U(1)_X symmetry broken by a large singlet scalar VEV; imposes a Z_2 symmetry for PSDM stability; identifies PSDM as a pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson nearly orthogonal to the would-be Goldstone of U(1)_X; computes mass generation via U(1)_X-invariant mixing potential and evaluates relic density and detection constraints.

Result: PSDM acquires mass from invariant mixing potential; direct detection cross section is naturally suppressed even for weak-scale masses; correct thermal relic density is achieved via annihilations into SM particles or light Higgs-like scalars; model satisfies constraints from Higgs invisible decay, Higgs coupling measurements, and indirect detection.

Conclusion: The proposed PSDM model provides a robust, minimal, and testable framework for pseudo-scalar dark matter that reconciles theoretical naturalness, stability, and current experimental bounds.

Abstract: We propose a novel model for pseudo-scalar dark matter (PSDM) by extending the Standard Model (SM) with a dark gauged $U(1)_X$ symmetry, but without dark charged fermions. We impose a $Z_2$ symmetry to ensure the stability of pseudo-scalar dark matter and regard the $U(1)_X$ symmetry as being broken dominantly by a large VEV of the singlet scalar field. The would-be Goldstone associated with the $U(1)_X$ gauge boson is almost orthogonal to the direction of PSDM. As a result, we show that PSDM appears as a stable pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson receiving the mass from the $U(1)_X$ invariant mixing potential and the corresponding cross section for direct detection gets suppressed even for the weak-scale mass of PSDM. We also show that the correct relic density can be explained by the PSDM annihilations into the SM particles or into a pair of light Higgs-like scalars, being compatible with the bounds from Higgs invisible decay, Higgs data and indirect detection.

</details>


### [154] [Quantification of the Flavor Diagonal Hadronic CP Violation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23580)
*Nodoka Yamanaka*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews CP violation's role in probing new physics beyond the Standard Model, recent advances in quantifying hadronic CP-violating contributions to electric dipole moments and related observables, and current approaches to resolving the strong CP problem without introducing new fields or interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the implications of flavor-diagonal CP violation for fundamental symmetries and to constrain or reveal physics beyond the Standard Model through precision low-energy observables such as EDMs and T-violating correlations.

Method: Review and synthesis of theoretical developments in hadronic CP violation, lattice QCD calculations, effective field theory analyses, and model-independent approaches to the strong CP problem.

Result: Summarizes improved quantitative estimates of CP-violating hadronic matrix elements relevant to EDMs; highlights progress in lattice QCD and chiral perturbation theory; outlines viable solutions to the strong CP problem—particularly the massless up-quark scenario and its phenomenological viability.

Conclusion: Flavor-diagonal CP violation remains a powerful probe of new physics; precise determination of hadronic CP-violating effects is essential; solving the strong CP problem without new fields remains challenging but theoretically plausible under specific assumptions.

Abstract: The flavor diagonal CP violation of elementary particle physics contributes to the atomic, nuclear, and nucleon electric dipole moments (EDMs), T-violating neutron optics, and to the angular correlations of beta decay. In this contribution, we review the basics and the importance of CP violation in the search for new physics beyond the standard model, the recent progress in the quantification of the hadron level CP violation contributing to the aforementioned observables, and finally the current attempt to solve the strong CP problem without additional interactions and fields.

</details>


### [155] [Probing Anomalous $t{\bar q}Z$ Interactions at Muon Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22412)
*Liangliang Shang,Lu Zhang,Bingfang Yang,Stefano Moretti*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates anomalous t q-bar Z interactions via μ⁺μ⁻ → t q-bar Z at future muon colliders (3–14 TeV), using detector simulation, muon beam polarization, and fat jet reconstruction; it sets BR(t→qZ) limits down to O(10⁻⁸) at 14 TeV, improving current LHC limits by 2–3 orders of magnitude.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe rare top-quark flavor-changing neutral current (FCNC) interactions—specifically the anomalous t q-bar Z coupling—which are highly suppressed in the Standard Model but could be enhanced by new physics; existing LHC limits are insufficient, motivating exploration at higher-energy, cleaner muon colliders.

Method: Effective field theory framework; event generation and full detector simulation (including pile-up and resolution effects); signal categorization into six decay channels based on t→Wb/Zq and Z→ℓℓ/hadronic decays; exploitation of longitudinal muon beam polarization to suppress SM backgrounds; application of fat jet tagging for boosted hadronic Z/t reconstruction; statistical limit setting using profile likelihood ratio test.

Result: At √s = 14 TeV with 20 ab⁻¹, the 95% CL upper limit on BR(t→qZ) reaches ~2×10⁻⁸ (depending on q = u,c); this improves upon current CMS/ATLAS limits (~10⁻⁵–10⁻⁶) by two to three orders of magnitude; sensitivity scales favorably with energy and luminosity, with weaker but still competitive limits at 3 and 10 TeV.

Conclusion: TeV-scale muon colliders offer a uniquely sensitive and complementary probe for top-quark FCNC interactions involving Z bosons, enabled by high center-of-mass energy, polarized beams, and advanced reconstruction techniques—establishing them as a powerful tool for BSM physics searches beyond the LHC.

Abstract: In the framework of effective field theory, we study the anomalous $t{\bar q}Z$ interaction through the process $μ^+μ^- \to t{\bar q}Z$ at future muon colliders with $\sqrt s= 3, 10, 14\,\text{TeV}$. Based on the top quark decay modes involving $W$ and $Z$ bosons, we first divide the signal into six cases. Then, in order to obtain the limits on the corresponding branching ratios, we perform a detector simulation for both signals and Standard Model backgrounds. To enhance the signal significance, we exploit the polarization of the muon beams and employ the fat jet method to reconstruct signals in hadronic final states. For $\sqrt s= 14\,\text{TeV}$ with $20\,\text{ab}^{-1}$, we find that the upper limit on the branching ratio for $t\to qZ$ can reach the order of $\mathcal{O}(10^{-8})$, which exceeds the limits provided by the CMS and ATLAS collaborations by 2 to 3 orders of magnitude. Our study thus demonstrates that TeV-scale muon colliders can provide an efficient and complementary platform for probing rare top quark interactions.

</details>


### [156] [Analytical calculation of the spectrum of nonlinear Compton scattering beyond local approximations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22427)
*M. P. Malakhov,Th. Benahmed,E. G. Gelfer,A. M. Fedotov,O. Klimo,S. Weber,S. G. Rykovanov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives compact analytical formulae for the spectrum of nonlinear Compton scattering in finite plane-wave pulses with smooth envelopes, using asymptotic phase-integral evaluation and uniform/saddle-point approximations to handle caustic divergences and harmonic substructure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain analytically tractable, accurate spectral predictions for nonlinear Compton scattering in realistic finite-duration laser pulses—beyond the locally monochromatic or infinite-pulse approximations—while preserving harmonic structure and avoiding unphysical caustic divergences.

Method: Reduces strong-field QED probability to finite-pulse phase integrals; applies asymptotic analysis for multicycle pulses; employs uniform approximation to regularize caustics at nonlinear harmonic edges; refines near the linear edge with envelope-corrected saddle-point approximation; retains harmonic substructure in dominant spectral-angular regions.

Result: Compact, asymptotically valid analytical formulae for the nonlinear Compton spectrum that agree with numerical simulations, correctly reproduce harmonic structure, eliminate caustic divergences, and recover the locally monochromatic limit upon interference averaging.

Conclusion: The derived formulae provide a robust, computationally efficient, and physically faithful description of nonlinear Compton spectra for smooth, multicycle laser pulses—enabling reliable modeling of radiation from electron beams in strong-field QED experiments.

Abstract: We derive compact analytical formulae for the spectrum of nonlinear Compton scattering in a finite plane-wave pulse with a smooth temporal envelope. The strong-field QED probability is reduced to finite-pulse phase integrals, which are evaluated asymptotically for multicycle pulses with a broad class of smooth envelopes. We use the uniform approximation to remove the caustic divergences that appear at the nonlinear edges of broadened harmonics. Away from the caustics, it reduces to the standard saddle-point result. The behavior near the linear edge is further improved by an envelope-corrected saddle-point approximation. The approach retains the harmonic substructure in the spectral-angular region carrying the dominant part of the emitted radiation. The locally monochromatic approximation is recovered by averaging the finite-pulse interference. Within their asymptotic domain of applicability, the resulting formulae agree with direct numerical calculations and can be used to evaluate spectra from an electron beam.

</details>


### [157] [Taming Symbolic IBP Reduction with Intermediate Bases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22500)
*Qian Song*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces an efficient algorithm for reconstructing integration-by-parts (IBP) reduction coefficients using intermediate bases, drastically reducing the number of required numerical samplings compared to brute-force ansatz methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The main challenge in IBP reduction is solving large linear systems arising from general ansätze with too many unknowns—especially when coefficients are rational functions of kinematics and dimension.

Method: A sequential intermediate basis reconstruction algorithm that expresses analytic reduction coefficients as products of sparse analytic matrices with simple rational polynomial entries.

Result: For a three-point massive box-triangle and a four-point massive pentagon-triangle, the method requires only 3289 and 13013 numerical samplings, respectively—orders of magnitude fewer than the 1.4M and 21.6M unknowns in naive ansätze.

Conclusion: The intermediate-basis approach enables scalable, analytic IBP coefficient reconstruction, making high-multiplicity massive loop integrals computationally tractable.

Abstract: Despite many years of development in integration-by-parts reduction, reconstructing all reduction coefficients, which are rational polynomials of kinematic variables and the space-time dimension, remains a non-trivial problem. The main difficulty comes from the large number of unknowns in a general ansatz, which can lead to a linear system that is too large to solve. In this paper, we present an algorithm for reconstructing reduction coefficients through a sequence of intermediate bases. The resulting analytic reduction coefficients are products of a few analytic matrices, whose non-zero entries are simple rational polynomials. We demonstrate the efficiency of this algorithm with two cutting edge examples: a three-point massive box-triangle and a four-point massive pentagon-triangle. Reconstructing all reduction coefficients for the box-triangle (pentagon-triangle) requires 3289 (13013) numerical samplings, significantly fewer than the number of unknowns in the general ansatz, 1407406 (21638331).

</details>


### [158] [Study on the Cabibbo-favored ${\overline B}_{d,s}$ ${\to}$ $D_{d,s}^{(*)+}S^{-}$ weak decays in QCD factorization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22505)
*Lili Chen,Chenyang Jing,Kaiyuan Gao,Shuai Xu,Mengfei Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates branching ratios for Cabibbo-favored B̄_{d,s} → D_{d,s}^{(*)+}S^- decays (with S = K_0^*(1430), a_0(1450)) using NLO QCD factorization and updated light-front form factors, finding O(10^{-4}) rates for B̄^0→D^+a_0^- and B̄_s^0→D_s^+a_0^- in scenario-2, suggesting observability at LHCb/SuperKEKB.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recent experimental progress and theoretical developments motivate the study of Cabibbo-favored b→c weak decays involving scalar mesons, particularly to probe scalar meson structure and test QCD factorization at next-to-leading order.

Method: Next-to-leading order (NLO) QCD factorization (QCDF) framework, combined with updated B_{(s)}→D_{(s)}^{(*)} transition form factors from a covariant light-front quark model; two distinct scenarios for scalar meson internal structure (e.g., p-wave qq̄ assignment) are considered.

Result: Branching ratios for B̄^0→D^+a_0^- and B̄_s^0→D_s^+a_0^- reach O(10^{-4}) in scenario-2 (scalar mesons as lowest-lying p-wave states); other modes (e.g., involving K_0^*(1430)) are suppressed.

Conclusion: The predicted large branching ratios for B̄^0→D^+a_0^- and B̄_s^0→D_s^+a_0^- in scenario-2 make them promising channels for experimental observation at LHCb and SuperKEKB, offering insight into scalar meson nature and QCDF validity in b→c scalar final states.

Abstract: Motivated by recent experimental progress and theoretical developments, we investigate the Cabibbo-favored $b\to c$ governed ${\overline B}_{d,s}$ ${\to}$ $D_{d,s}^{(*)+}S^{-}$~($S$$=$$K_0^*(1430)$, $a_0(1450)$) weak decays by considering the next-to-leading (NLO) contributions within QCDF framework. With the updated values of $B_{(s)}\to D_{(s)}^{(*)}$ transition form factors obtained from a covariant light-front quark model, branching ratios are estimated in two scenarios for scalar mesons. It is found that the branching ratios for ${\overline B}^0{\to}D^{+}{a_0^-}$ and ${\overline B}_{s}^0{\to}D_{s}^{+}{a_0^-}$ decays can reach up to the order of ${\cal O}(10^{-4})$ in scenario-2 by assuming that the scalar mesons are lowest-lying p-wave states, which deserve high-priority experimental searches and may be observed in the ongoing LHCb and SuperKEKB experiments.

</details>


### [159] [Dynamics of ($Z_N$) Domain Walls in SU(N) Gauge Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22532)
*Sayanjit Banerjee,Sanatan Digal,Sumit Shaw*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates domain wall collisions in SU(N) gauge theories using Polyakov-loop effective potential models, revealing that string junctions and topological strings (vortices/string loops) govern the dynamics of Z_N domain walls, with distinct behaviors in SU(3) vs. SU(4), including vortex-antivortex creation, wall merging, bouncing, and scattering.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the dynamical role of topological defects—particularly Z_N center domain walls and associated vortices/string junctions—in non-Abelian gauge theories, especially during collisions, which is essential for modeling defect networks in confinement/deconfinement transitions and early-universe cosmology.

Method: Analyzes domain wall collisions in SU(N) (N=3,4) gauge theories using the Polyakov-loop effective potential model; employs dimensional reduction and topological analysis to classify collision outcomes (merging, bouncing, scattering) and identify vortex/string-loop formation mechanisms in 2+1D and 3+1D.

Result: In SU(3): merger of two non-planar Z_3 walls proceeds via vortex–antivortex pair creation in 2+1D; in SU(4): low-energy collisions yield single-wall formation without vortices, while high-energy collisions lead to either bouncing or scattering into another Z_4 wall pair via vortex-mediated processes; vortex–antivortex pairs generalize to closed string loops in 3+1D for both theories.

Conclusion: String junctions and topological strings are dynamically essential in center-domain-wall evolution, revealing novel defect interaction mechanisms in non-Abelian gauge theories beyond standard Abelian or scalar-field analogues.

Abstract: We study collisions of domain walls in $SU(N)$ gauge theories using the Polyakov-loop effective potential models. We find that string junctions play a crucial role in the dynamics of $Z_N$ domain walls. In $SU(3)$ gauge theory, the merger of two non-planar $Z_3$ domain walls into a single wall proceeds via the creation of a vortex--antivortex pair in $2+1$ dimensions. In $SU(4)$ gauge theory, low-energy collisions of $Z_4$ walls result in the formation of a single domain wall without the creation of vortices. At higher collision energies, two $Z_4$ domain walls can either bounce back or scatter into another pair of domain walls through the formation of vortices. The creation of vortex--antivortex pairs generalises to the creation of string loops in $3+1$ dimensions for both gauge theories. These results demonstrate a direct dynamical role for topological strings in the evolution of center-domain-wall networks and reveal a new aspect of defect dynamics in non-Abelian gauge theories.

</details>


### [160] [Radiative Lifting of $\mathbb{Z}_3$ Domain-Wall Degeneracy in a Type-III Seesaw Model: Implications for Leptogenesis and Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22642)
*Priya,Labh Singh,B. C. Chauhan,Surender Verma*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a Z3-symmetric extension of the Standard Model with three hyperchargeless SU(2)L fermion triplets (Type-III seesaw) and a complex scalar singlet χ; radiative Coleman-Weinberg corrections lift Z3 vacuum degeneracy, annihilating domain walls; it accommodates neutrino data, enables thermal leptogenesis at ~10^9 GeV, and predicts detectable gravitational-wave signals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the cosmological domain-wall problem inherent in discrete Z3 symmetry while simultaneously explaining neutrino masses, leptogenesis, and generating testable gravitational-wave signatures within a unified framework.

Method: Constructs a Z3-symmetric model with SU(2)L fermion triplets and a complex scalar singlet χ; computes Coleman-Weinberg effective potential to show radiative lifting of Z3 vacuum degeneracy; performs numerical fit to neutrino oscillation data; calculates thermal leptogenesis via out-of-equilibrium decay of lightest triplet; estimates stochastic gravitational-wave spectrum from domain-wall collapse.

Result: Radiative bias successfully removes domain-wall problem; viable parameter space consistent with neutrino masses/mixing is identified; successful thermal leptogenesis occurs for triplet mass ~10^9 GeV; predicted GW spectrum falls within sensitivity ranges of LISA, BBO, DECIGO, and Einstein Telescope.

Conclusion: The model unifies neutrino mass generation (Type-III seesaw), radiative domain-wall annihilation, high-scale thermal leptogenesis, and observable gravitational-wave signals—establishing a predictive, testable link among particle physics and early-universe cosmology.

Abstract: In this work, we study a $\mathbb{Z}_3$-symmetric extension of the Standard Model with three hyperchargeless $SU(2)_L$ fermion triplets responsible for neutrino mass generation $\textit{via}$ the Type-III seesaw mechanism together with a complex scalar singlet $χ$ whose vacuum expectation value spontaneously breaks the $\mathbb{Z}_3$ symmetry. Radiative corrections induced by the Yukawa interactions between the $SU(2)_L$ fermion triplets and the complex scalar singlet $χ$ generate a Coleman-Weinberg vacuum bias that lifts the degeneracy among the $\mathbb{Z}_3$ vacua, leading to the annihilation of unstable domain-walls. Consequently, the degeneracy among the $\mathbb{Z}_3$ vacua is lifted radiatively through the Coleman-Weinberg effective potential, generating a dynamical bias term that triggers the annihilation of unstable domain walls. We perform a numerical analysis consistent with current neutrino oscillation data and identify viable regions of parameter space accommodating the observed neutrino masses and leptonic mixing parameters. The observed baryon asymmetry of the Universe is generated through thermal leptogenesis $\textit{via}$ the out-of-equilibrium decay of the lightest fermion triplet for masses around $\mathcal{O}(10^{9})\,\mathrm{GeV}$, consistent with the Type-III seesaw framework. Depending on the choice of model parameters, the predicted gravitational-wave spectrum can fall within the sensitivity reach of future space-based and ground-based gravitational-wave detectors. Our framework therefore establishes a correlation between neutrino mass generation, leptogenesis, radiative domain-wall instability, and gravitational-wave phenomenology.

</details>


### [161] [Remarks on atmospheric effect of D-foam in light of muon puzzle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22855)
*Chengyi Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This addendum explores how quantum space-time foam-induced Lorentz violation—previously shown to mildly suppress photon–nucleus pair production in electromagnetic cascades—affects extensive air showers (EAS) initiated by ultrahigh-energy hadrons. It argues that foam-induced suppression of secondary photon subshowers (from π⁰ decays) reduces electron content at ground level, leading to underestimation of primary energy and enhanced relative muon content—potentially explaining the experimentally observed muon excess in cosmic-ray data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether quantum-gravitational space-time foam effects—specifically subluminal Lorentz violation suppressing pair creation by photons on nuclei—can explain the persistent experimental anomaly of excess muons in ultrahigh-energy cosmic-ray extensive air showers (EAS), as reported by Auger and Telescope Array.

Method: Analytical modeling of EAS development incorporating foam-modified photon interactions: specifically, suppression of γ + nucleus → e⁺e⁻ pair production for both primary cosmic γ-rays and secondary photons from π⁰ decay in hadronic showers; propagation of resulting changes in electromagnetic vs. muonic components to ground-level detector response.

Result: Foam-induced suppression reduces electron/photon content in EAS, causing systematic underestimation of primary cosmic-ray energy and increasing the muon-to-electron ratio; this provides a quantum-gravitational interpretation for the observed muon excess without invoking new particle physics or astrophysical sources.

Conclusion: Quantum space-time foam, via subluminal Lorentz violation, can plausibly account for the muon excess in ultrahigh-energy cosmic-ray EAS—a potential observational signature of quantum gravity; however, definitive confirmation requires future high-statistics, multi-detector measurements of shower composition and energy scale.
