Abstract: In our recent paper arXiv:2509.00552, we used a stringy model for quantum space-time foam to suggest that the so-induced subluminal Lorentz violation~(LV) for photons would not lead to experimentally unacceptable changes in the developments of particle showers initiated by cosmic $γ$-rays in the Earth's atmosphere, in contrast to other approaches to LV. The result indicated, nonetheless, at the same time that the foam can mildly modify the electromagnetic cascades under certain conditions, by suppressing pair creation on nuclei by primary photons. In this addendum, we consider how this modification affects the detection of extensive air shower~(EAS) initiated by an ultrahigh-energy cosmic-ray particle~(viz., a primary hadron), like proton with $E \sim 10^{19}~\textrm{eV}$, given that secondary photon subshowers following $π^{0}$ decays could be similarly influenced. We argue that fewer electrons would reach the detector and hence the energy of the primary particle may be underestimated due to foam effects, enhancing in such a way the muon content in EASs. This opens up the possibility of interpreting the alleged ``excess'' of muons, as reported by Auger and Telescope Array collaborations recently and many other experiments on high-energy cosmic rays, with a quantum-gravitational effect. Future observations are anticipated to confirm whether this anomaly really exists.

</details>


### [162] [A novel approach for studying two-particle momentum correlation function in relativistic nuclear collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22865)
*Zhi-Lei She,Wen-Chao Zhang,An-Ke Lei,Dai-Mei Zhou,Hua Zheng,Li-Lin Zhu,Qiang Wang,Yu-Liang Yan,Zhong-Qi Wang,Ben-Hao Sa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a modified mixed-event technique within the PACIAE transport model to compute two-particle momentum correlation functions, incorporating a correction factor ξ(k*) for final-state interactions and quantum statistics; results agree well with ALICE data for various baryon–baryon and meson–baryon pairs in pp collisions at 7 TeV, and enable self-consistent extraction of emission source radii without assuming inter-particle interaction models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome challenges in calculating two-particle momentum correlations within transport models—particularly accounting for final-state interactions and quantum statistics—while enabling model-independent source characterization in relativistic nuclear collisions.

Method: Employs the mixed-event technique on PACIAE-simulated final hadronic states (FHS), introducing a momentum-dependent modification factor ξ(k*) to correct for inadequacies in modeling final-state interactions and quantum statistics; uses hadron-hadron cross sections from the additive quark model, avoiding ad hoc assumptions about correlated-pair interactions.

Result: Excellent agreement with ALICE measurements of Kp, pp, pΛ, and ΛΛ correlation functions in pp collisions at √s = 7 TeV; self-consistent extraction of particle emission source radii from simulated FHS; validation of the PACIAE+ξ(k*) framework for diverse hadronic pairs.

Conclusion: The modified PACIAE approach provides a robust, first-principles-compatible method for computing two-particle correlations and extracting source information, paving the way for studies of exotic multi-hadron systems such as dimesons, dibaryons, and diexotic hadrons.

Abstract: Two particle momentum correlation functions provide a nontrivial tool for probing the strong interaction and/or extracting particle emission source information in relativistic nuclear collisions. Although transport models can describe the microscopic phase-space evolution of the collision system, calculating correlation functions within the framework of transport models remains challenging. In this paper, we employ the mixed-event technique to calculate two particle momentum correlation function as $C(k^*)= \mathcal{N} ξ(k^*)\frac{N_{\mathrm{same}}(k^*)}{N_{\mathrm{mixed}}(k^*)}$ based on the parton and hadron cascade model PACIAE simulated final hadronic state (FHS) with introducing a modification factor $ξ(k^*)$ to improve the treatment of final-state interactions and quantum statistics effects in the PACIAE model. The simulated results show good agreement with the ALICE data for $Kp$, $pp$, $pΛ$, and $ΛΛ$ momentum correlation functions in $pp$ collisions at $\sqrt{s}=7$ TeV. On the other hand, the particle emission source radius of the correlated pairs are also evaluated based on the simulated FHS self-consistently. Since the PACIAE model employs hadron-hadron cross sections derived from the additive quark model, the calculation of two-particle momentum correlation functions does not require prior assumptions about the interaction between the two correlated particles. This successful ``PACIAE + modification factor" approach may shed light on the future study of momentum correlation functions for dimesons, dibaryons, and even diexotic hadrons.

</details>


### [163] [Twisting Small-$x$ Gluon Tomography with Orbital Angular Momentum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22871)
*Wei Kou,Xurong Chen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper extends small-x gluon tomography in diffractive dijet DIS by introducing orbital angular momentum (OAM)-resolved measurements using twisted lepton wave packets, enabling tunable Bessel-mode projections of the gluon Wigner distribution's elliptic component; it identifies a novel 'finite-rate null'—a normalized projection zero in the elliptic response—that is inaccessible with plane-wave probes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To access finer transverse spatial and OAM structure of small-x gluons in hadrons beyond standard plane-wave tomography, particularly the elliptic component of the gluon Wigner distribution, which is constrained by rotational symmetry and difficult to isolate with fixed transverse readouts.

Method: Replaces the standard plane-wave lepton current with a twisted (vortex) wave-packet current in hard diffractive dijet deep inelastic scattering; derives the resulting OAM-resolved elliptic correlation observable A₂⁽ᴹ⁾(q_T, R_γ), where q_T is the transverse Bessel scale—not photon k_T—and computes the normalized linear response including subtraction from total diffractive rate deformation.

Result: A family of OAM-resolved elliptic correlations A₂⁽ᴹ⁾(q_T, R_γ) emerges, enabling tunable Bessel-mode projection of the target’s small-x elliptic gluon structure; the normalized elliptic response exhibits a 'finite-rate null'—a configuration where A₂⁽ᴹ⁾ = 0 while the diffractive rate remains nonzero—acting as a projection zero of the response to the elliptic gluon component.

Conclusion: Twisted lepton currents provide a new, externally controllable projection basis for small-x gluon tomography, revealing geometric features (e.g., sign and zeros of elliptic response) inaccessible to plane-wave probes; the finite-rate null serves as a direct experimental signature of the target’s intrinsic gluonic deformation and validates the OAM-resolved Wigner tomography framework.

Abstract: We propose an orbital-angular-momentum-resolved extension of small-$x$ gluon tomography in hard diffractive dijet deep inelastic scattering. In the standard plane-wave setup, the elliptic correlation between the dijet relative momentum and the target recoil probes the elliptic component of the small-$x$ gluon Wigner distribution through a fixed transverse readout. We show that replacing the plane-wave lepton current by a twisted wave-packet current promotes this readout into a tunable OAM--Bessel projection kernel. The exchanged virtual photon is not treated as an asymptotic vortex particle; the OAM dependence enters through the transverse structure of the lepton electromagnetic current. The resulting observable is a family of $M$-resolved elliptic correlations $A_2^{(M)}(q_T,R_γ)$, where $q_T$ denotes the transverse Bessel scale of the projection kernel, not the transverse momentum of the exchanged photon. We derive the normalized linear response and show that it contains a subtraction from the deformation of the total diffractive rate. Consequently, the elliptic response can vanish while the diffractive rate remains finite. This finite-rate null is a normalized projection zero of the response to the target elliptic gluon component, not a disappearance of diffraction. It is not available as a tunable mode zero in the standard single plane-wave readout, and provides an external projection basis in which the response to the same small-$x$ elliptic geometry can be probed with either sign or tuned to zero.

</details>


### [164] [Diffuse Supernova Neutrinos with Secret Neutrino Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22898)
*Praveen Bharadwaj,Utpal Chattopadhyay,Dilip Kumar Ghosh,Arnab Sarker*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how scalar-mediated non-standard neutrino self-interactions (νSI) distort the Diffuse Supernova Neutrino Background (DSNB) spectrum via resonant scattering on the cosmic neutrino background (CνB), and assesses detection prospects and flavor-discriminating sensitivity at JUNO, Hyper-Kamiokande (with Gd), and DUNE.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe sub-GeV-scale neutrino self-interactions mediated by a light scalar φ using spectral distortions in the DSNB—complementing cosmological and supernova bounds with flavor-resolved, terrestrial experimental sensitivity.

Method: Full three-flavor quantum kinetic calculation incorporating PMNS mixing and four flavor-diagonal coupling scenarios (universal, e-, μ-, τ-specific); computes νiνk → φ → νjνl resonant scattering effects on DSNB spectra; projects event rates and 3σ exclusion limits in (mφ, g) parameter space for JUNO, Hyper-K (Gd), and DUNE.

Result: DSNB exhibits broad, flavor-dependent spectral depletions sensitive to coupling structure and neutrino mass ordering; projected sensitivities reach g ∼ 10⁻⁸ for mφ ∼ 100–300 eV—improving upon existing bounds by several orders of magnitude below 100 eV; DSNB provides unique flavor-discriminating power absent in cosmological or SN constraints.

Conclusion: DSNB is a powerful, flavor-sensitive probe of light scalar-mediated νSI; next-generation detectors can decisively test νSI models previously inaccessible to cosmology or single-supernova analyses.

Abstract: The Diffuse Supernova Neutrino Background (DSNB), an isotropic flux arising from the cumulative neutrino emission of all stellar core-collapse events throughout cosmic history, is expected to be detected by next-generation neutrino observatories. As DSNB neutrinos propagate over cosmological distances through the cosmic neutrino background (C$ν$B), they may undergo non-standard neutrino self-interactions ($ν$SI), leaving distinct spectral imprints on the observed flux. In this work, we investigate the impact of scalar ($φ$)-mediated $ν$SI on the DSNB within a full three-flavor framework that retains the complete PMNS structure. We consider four representative flavor-diagonal coupling structures--universal, $e$-, $μ$-, and $τ$-specific. The resonant scattering $ν_iν_k\toφ\toν_jν_l$ off the lightest, relativistic C$ν$B state produces broad spectral depletion whose pattern depends on the coupling structure and the neutrino mass ordering, generating distinctive signatures across the six flavor fluxes. We compute the resulting event spectra at JUNO, Hyper-Kamiokande with gadolinium loading, and DUNE, and derive projected $3σ$ sensitivities in the $(m_φ,~g)$ parameter plane. We find that these experiments can probe couplings as low as $g\sim10^{-8}$ for $m_φ\sim100$--$300$ eV, surpassing existing bounds by up to a few orders of magnitude in the sub-100 eV mass range. Moreover, unlike the flavor-blind cosmological and supernova bounds, the DSNB sensitivity is flavor-discriminating, offering a unique opportunity to identify the underlying flavor structure of $ν$SI in the event of a detection.

</details>


### [165] [Signatures of gravity-mediated dark matter interaction in theories with large extra dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23178)
*A. Clarke,V. V. Flambaum,M. Pospelov,I. B. Samsonov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates gravity-mediated dark matter detection in ADD models with large extra dimensions, deriving direct and indirect detection bounds on the dark matter mass (m_χ) and fundamental Planck scale (M_*), reaching down to M_* ~ few TeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard gravitational coupling makes dark matter undetectable in labs; however, in ADD models with n large extra dimensions, enhanced short-distance gravity via Kaluza-Klein graviton exchange may render such dark matter observable through direct (nucleon scattering) and indirect (resonant annihilation) channels.

Method: Computes effective DM–nucleon interaction strength from cumulative KK graviton exchange in ADD models; derives direct detection limits using Xe-based experiments (e.g., XENON/LUX); computes resonant annihilation cross section χχ → SM via on-shell KK modes and compares with Fermi-LAT gamma-ray constraints.

Result: Derives competitive exclusion bounds in the {m_χ, M_*} parameter space—direct detection constrains M_* up to ~few TeV for m_χ ≳ 10 GeV; indirect detection via γ rays provides complementary, often stronger, limits for resonant masses m_χ ≈ m_KK/2.

Conclusion: Purely gravity-coupled dark matter is not necessarily inaccessible: in large-extra-dimension scenarios, both direct and indirect searches impose stringent, testable constraints on the fundamental scale M_* and DM mass m_χ, challenging minimal ADD realizations.

Abstract: Dark matter particles that couple to the Standard Model only through gravity are usually regarded as inaccessible to laboratory detection. This expectation can change in theories with $n$ extra spatial dimensions, where gravity is enhanced at short distances and the potential scales as $1/r^{1+n}$. We reconsider the gravity-mediated dark matter (DM) interactions in Arkani-Hamed-Dimopoulos-Dvali (ADD) models with $n$ large extra dimensions. The cumulative exchange of the gravitational Kaluza-Klein (KK) modes leads to the effective strength of interactions with the Standard Model nucleons that scales as $m_pm_χM_*^{-4}$, where $ m_χ$ is the mass of DM and $M_*$ is the fundamental $4+n$ dimensional mass scale. We confront this interaction with sensitivity achieved in the large Xe-based underground direct detection experiments and derive bounds on the $\{m_χ,M_*\}$ parameter space that stretches all the way to $M_*\sim$ few TeV. We also address the indirect detection of scalar $χ$ that can resonantly annihilate via the on-shell KK modes into the SM particles $W^\pm,Z,h$. The annihilation cross section for the process scales as $\langleσv\rangle \sim m_χ^nM_*^{-n-2}$, and stringent limits on the same parameter space can be derived from observations of high-energy galactic $γ$ rays.

</details>


### [166] [Pair Production of Singlet Vector-Like B Quarks in Boosted bZ Final States at a Muon Collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23187)
*Eda Alici*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores the discovery potential of singlet vector-like bottom quarks (B) at a 10 TeV muon collider via the boosted Zb final state, achieving 5σ sensitivity up to MB ≈ 4.3 TeV with 10 ab⁻¹ luminosity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the search reach for vector-like B quarks beyond current LHC limits by leveraging the high-energy, low-background environment of a future 10 TeV muon collider.

Method: Simulated signal (μ⁺μ⁻ → BB̄ → (Zb)(Zb̄)) and SM backgrounds (ZZjj, ZZbb̄, tt̄) at leading order using MadGraph5_aMC@NLO; interfaced with Pythia 8 for parton showering/hadronization and Delphes for muon-collider detector simulation; employed a cut-based analysis exploiting boosted kinematics (e.g., high-pT b-jets, small Δφℓℓ, large hadronic activity); statistical significance estimated via Asimov approximation with/without 10% background systematic uncertainty.

Result: For BR(B→bZ)=0.25 and 10 ab⁻¹, 5σ discovery reach is MB ≈ 4.3 TeV (statistical-only) and MB ≈ 4.2 TeV (with 10% systematic uncertainty).

Conclusion: A 10 TeV muon collider offers superior sensitivity to heavy vector-like B quarks compared to the LHC, thanks to its clean environment, high center-of-mass energy, and favorable signal-to-background ratio in the boosted Zb channel.

Abstract: We investigate the pair production of singlet vector-like bottom quarks at a future $10~\mathrm{TeV}$ muon collider in the boosted $Zb$ final-state topology. The signal process is considered as $μ^{+}μ^{-}\to B\bar{B}\to (Zb)(Z\bar{b})$, with one $Z$ boson decaying leptonically and the other hadronically, leading to a final state with an opposite-sign same-flavour dilepton pair, multiple jets, and two $b$-tagged jets. Signal and Standard Model background events are generated at leading order with MadGraph5\_\allowbreak aMC@NLO and subsequently interfaced with \textsc{Pythia}~8 for parton showering and hadronization. Detector effects are simulated with \textsc{Delphes} using a muon-collider detector configuration. A cut-based analysis exploiting boosted kinematics, including hard leading-$b$-jet transverse momentum, small dilepton angular separation, and large hadronic activity, is performed to suppress the dominant $ZZjj$, $ZZb\bar{b}$, and $t\bar{t}$ backgrounds. Statistical sensitivities are evaluated with the Asimov approximation, including both zero and $10\%$ background systematic uncertainty scenarios. For the singlet benchmark $\mathrm{BR}(B\to bZ)=0.25$ and an integrated luminosity of $10~\mathrm{ab}^{-1}$, a $5σ$ discovery reach up to $M_B\simeq 4.3~\mathrm{TeV}$ is obtained, reduced slightly to $M_B\simeq 4.2~\mathrm{TeV}$ when systematic effects are included. These results demonstrate that a $10~\mathrm{TeV}$ muon collider provides a powerful and clean environment for probing vector-like $B$ quarks well beyond present LHC limits.

</details>


### [167] [Charting Dark Matter down to the neutrino floor/fog](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23457)
*Giorgio Arcadi,Abdelhak Djouadi,Stefano Profumo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores whether thermally produced WIMPs in the 2HD+a model can naturally reside in the 'neutrino fog'—the region where neutrino coherent scattering dominates direct detection—while satisfying relic density and indirect detection constraints. It identifies parameter regions with suppressed singlet-doublet mixing (sinθ → 0) where annihilation into ha/Ha sustains correct relic abundance, loop-induced direct detection cross-sections fall below the neutrino floor, and the light pseudoscalar a becomes long-lived (displaced vertices at colliders). In the zero-mixing limit, a may constitute an additional stable DM component. Viable parameter space is mapped against LZ, Fermi-LAT, and projected XLZD/CTA sensitivities; single-component DM remains viable above ~100 GeV, while two-component scenarios are largely excluded unless specific couplings are suppressed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether thermally produced WIMPs can naturally occupy the neutrino fog regime—where coherent neutrino scattering limits sensitivity of next-generation direct detection experiments—while simultaneously satisfying cosmological (relic density) and astrophysical (indirect detection) constraints.

Method: Comprehensive parameter scans over Type-I and Type-II Yukawa configurations in the 2HD+a model; inclusion of strongly suppressed singlet–doublet A–a mixing (sinθ → 0); analysis of annihilation channels (ha, Ha), loop-induced direct detection cross-sections, collider-displaced-vertex signatures, and two-component DM scenarios; comparison with current (LZ, Fermi-LAT) and projected (XLZD, CTA) experimental bounds.

Result: Single-component WIMP DM in this model naturally resides below the neutrino floor for m_DM > 100 GeV; two-component DM (with stable a) is largely excluded by current data unless specific parameter relations suppress haa couplings; displaced-vertex signatures emerge from long-lived a in the sinθ → 0 limit; loop-suppressed direct detection cross-sections align with neutrino fog expectations.

Conclusion: The 2HD+a model provides a theoretically motivated, phenomenologically viable framework for WIMPs in the neutrino fog—especially in the suppressed-mixing limit—supporting continued investment in tonne-scale direct detection experiments; however, multi-component DM scenarios require fine-tuned couplings to evade present constraints.

Abstract: Next-generation direct detection experiments will probe dark matter (DM) scattering cross-sections deep into the neutrino fog, the regime where coherent neutrino scattering becomes an irreducible background. We investigate whether thermally produced weakly interacting massive particles (WIMPs) can naturally populate this regime while satisfying relic density and indirect detection constraints. Adopting as a case study the 2HD+a model, we have performed comprehensive parameter scans over the Type-I and Type-II Yukawa configurations. We have included the limit of strongly suppressed singlet--doublet {\it A-a} mixing sin$θ\! \to \! 0$ and we show that annihilation into {\it ha} and ${\it Ha}$ final states sustains the correct relic density while loop-induced direct detection cross-sections naturally land inside the neutrino fog; in the same limit the light pseudoscalar boson becomes long-lived, featuring displaced-vertex signatures when produced at colliders. Finally, in the case of zero mixing, we have considered a new possibility for DM phenomenology as the $a$ state becomes cosmologically stable and, consequently, an additional DM component. We map all the viable parameter space against current LZ and FERMI-LAT bounds and projected XLZD and CTA sensitivities. We find that the single component setup lies naturally below the neutrino floor for DM masses above 100 GeV while, on the contrary, most of the parameter space of the two component DM scenario is strongly disfavored already considering present limit. The parameter space of both single and two component DM scenario can be nevertheless broadened by considering specific relations among the model parameters to suppress the coupling between the 125 GeV bosons and two $a$ states. Our results represent in any case a motivation to fully exploit future tonne-scale detectors.

</details>


### [168] [Next-to-next-to-leading-order QCD corrections to ${}^3S_1^{(8)}$ gluon fragmentation function for quarkonium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23594)
*Feng Feng,Yu Jia,Wen-Long Sang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: First NNLO QCD calculation of the $^3S_1^{(8)}$ gluon fragmentation function in NRQCD, yielding precise polarized/unpolarized results, resolving endpoint logarithms, and enabling improved phenomenology for quarkonium production at hadron colliders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide high-precision theoretical input for testing the color-octet mechanism in quarkonium production via gluon fragmentation, particularly for large-$p_T$ $J/ψ$, $ψ'$, and $χ_{cJ}$ at hadron colliders.

Method: Computation of NNLO QCD corrections to the ${}^3S_1^{(8)}$ gluon fragmentation function within the NRQCD factorization framework, including full treatment of endpoint logarithms ($z\to 1$) and both polarized and unpolarized cases, with high numerical precision.

Result: NNLO corrections are positive and substantial over most $z$; endpoint logarithmic singularities are fully reconstructed; combined with threshold resummation, yields phenomenologically viable fragmentation function inputs.

Conclusion: This NNLO result constitutes a crucial step toward definitive tests of the color-octet mechanism by enabling more reliable predictions for quarkonium production cross sections and polarization observables at high $p_T$.

Abstract: We present the first computation of the next-to-next-to-leading-order (NNLO) QCD corrections to the ${}^3S_1^{(8)}$ gluon fragmentation function for quarkonium within the nonrelativistic QCD (NRQCD) factorization framework, accurate to the lowest order in the velocity expansion. The calculation is performed with high numerical precision and encompasses both polarized and unpolarized cases. We find that the NNLO corrections are positive and substantial across most of the $z$ region. Furthermore, the logarithmic singularities near the endpoint $z\to 1$ are fully reconstructed, providing essential inputs for future threshold resummation beyond leading-logarithmic accuracy. Combined with threshold-resummed formulas in the large-$z$ region, our results yield phenomenologically viable inputs for the $^3S_1^{(8)}$ gluon fragmentation function. This enables a more reliable description of large-$p_T$ $J/ψ$ ($ψ'$) and $χ_{cJ}$ production and polarization at hadron colliders, representing a crucial step toward a definitive test of the color-octet mechanism.

</details>


### [169] [Observables and conformal properties of dark matter admixed isentropic neutron stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23620)
*Arijit Das,Prashanth Jaikumar,Adarsh Karekkat,Tanumoy Mandal*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs an equation of state for isentropic dark-matter-admixed neutron stars (DMANS) with hot cores and cold crusts, incorporating self-consistent temperature and DM density profiles for GeV-scale fermionic DM. It finds that DM-induced central density enhancement persists from cold NS results, but observable DM effects require high stellar masses. Non-monotonic speed-of-sound behavior emerges at large core DM densities. Crucially, thermal (entropy-driven) and DM-induced softening effects compete, influencing macroscopic properties and conformality indicators—suggesting DM admixture in isentropic stars can mimic quark-matter conformality signatures previously ascribed to cold NS.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how dark matter admixture affects the structure and thermodynamic properties of isentropic (hot-core, cold-crust) neutron stars, particularly whether DM accumulation alters macroscopic observables and mimics signatures traditionally associated with quark matter (e.g., conformality).

Method: Constructed a self-consistent equation of state for isentropic DMANS incorporating realistic temperature and dark-matter density profiles for GeV-scale fermionic dark matter; solved stellar structure equations (Tolman–Oppenheimer–Volkoff) with this EoS; analyzed speed-of-sound profiles, central density enhancement, conformality indicators (e.g., sound speed vs. conformal limit), and competition between thermal pressure and DM-induced softening.

Result: DM accumulation robustly enhances central density, as in cold NS; observable DM effects require high-mass configurations; speed of sound shows non-monotonic behavior at large core DM densities; thermal and DM softening effects oppose each other, shifting the onset of conformality toward the core; DM admixture can reproduce conformality signatures otherwise attributed to quark deconfinement in cold NS.

Conclusion: Dark-matter admixture in isentropic neutron stars significantly impacts their internal structure and thermodynamic signatures—notably, it can mimic conformal behavior linked to quark matter in cold neutron stars, implying that such signatures alone cannot uniquely identify exotic phases without accounting for DM and thermal effects.

Abstract: We construct an equation of state for isentropic dark-matter-admixed neutron stars (DMANS) with a hot core and relatively cold crust incorporating self-consistent temperature and DM density profiles for GeV-scale fermionic DM. We show that the enhancement of central stellar density due to DM accumulation, previously reported for cold neutron stars, remains robust. Substantial observable effects of DM accumulation arise only for sufficiently massive stellar configurations. Similar to earlier studies of cold NS, the speed of sound profile is shown to exhibit non-monotonic behavior for sufficiently large DM density in the core. We identify a competition between thermal effects due to nonzero values of entropy per baryon and softening effects of the dark sector which drive macroscopic properties and conformality indicators in opposite directions. This competition determines the onset of conformality near the stellar core and indicates that conformality signatures attributed to quark-matter in cold NS could be mimicked by DM admixture in isentropic stars.

</details>
